Himeno: "-- or at least there's an urban legend that goes like that!"
Mayu: "Talking about Manten Aquarium again? That's your new thing, isn't it?"
*While stuffing her lunch into her mouth, Tamagawa Himeno spoke, her eyes sparkling. I had no idea which point in the story got her so excited.*
Himeno: "That's right! Isn't this the scariest part? That the girl jumped to her death, smiling, after coming back from Manten Aquarium... It's pretty bad."
Mayu: "Yet you don't seem the least bit scared, Himeno..."
Himeno: "That's not true! I've got chills running down my spine."
Mayu: "Someone who's got chills running down her spine wouldn't be eating a pastry so cheerfully... and hey, that's my meatball!"
*During a break in the conversation, Himeno stole a side dish from my boxed lunch.*
Himeno: "Mmm, this minced meat from growing calves and little piglets... it's the best!"
Mayu: "Why do you have to put it like that? Geez, why'd you have to make me lose my appetite...? I'll give you the rest of it."
Himeno: "All ri~ght! I'll ta~ke it!"
*Himeno takes the rest of the meatballs from my lunch with amazing rhythm.*
*The reason that I -- Nakanobe Mayumi -- lost my appetite wasn't because Himeno's story scared me.*
*Manten Aquarium and I are connected in a way that I'd rather not think about.*
*Of course, Himeno has no idea about that connection. If she knew, she'd never mention Manten Aquarium around me again.*
*At the same time, I've been afraid that this thing might cause Himeno to stop talking to me. Himeno is pretty much my only friend.*
*It isn't that I haven't talked to anyone since entering junior high, just that the only person I can actually call a friend is Himeno.*
Mayu: "Seriously, I think this whole Manten Aquarium rumor obsession is getting to be a bit much."
Himeno: "Maybe. There's so many rumors though -- like that there's a man's ghost in the tanks, that the Deep-sea Fish booth is only open on a full moon, that the water changes into blood, or that you can hear crying from behind the tanks..."
Himeno: "Ah, that earlier one goes with this one. Also..."
Mayu: "No, that's fine, you don't need to say anymore!"
*I say firmly, causing Himeno to make a sour face.*
*Soon after, Himeno changes the subject, first to a TV drama that aired the night before, then to some cute fake nails with cats on them she found in a magazine.*
*She then talks about a classmate who was dating a boy from a nearby private school -- alighting on one subject before quickly changing to the next.*
*Normally, the subject of Manten Aquarium shouldn't have popped up again.*
Himeno: "Anyway, getting back to what we were talking about earlier..."
Mayu: "Huh, what exactly are we getting back to?"
Himeno: "Manten Aquarium! I looked into it... and this Sunday will be a full moon."
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*That was all I could say, the rest of my words unable to come out.*
Himeno: "So we should go and see if the rumors are true or not!"
Mayu: "B-But..."
Himeno: "I know you aren't interested in stuff like urban legends, Mayu! But next week we'll start preparing for the end of the semester and I won't be able to hang out with you... Lately we haven't gone anywhere together, have we?"
*If the full moon was next Sunday, I'm sure Himeno wouldn't have said anything. But I suspect because it happens to be this Sunday and next week happens to be our exams, she's chosen Manten Aquarium.*
*I know that I'm not hiding my confusion or my hesitation very well.*
*Even so, Himeno turns her carefree grin my way.*
Himeno: "Since it's an aquarium, even if the rumors aren't true we can still have fun, right?"
Mayu: "Well... that's true..."
*Usually, anywhere I go with Himeno, I have fun. Whether or not we'd have fun was not what I was worried about.*
Himeno: "By the way, Mayu. In the East Building of Manten Aquarium, they have a Hagen Daats."
Mayu: "As in the eat-in restaurant?"
Himeno: "They had closed up at one point, but it looks like they're reopening for a limited time."
Mayu: "Really?"
Himeno: "Really! They have the cookies and cream flavor you like too, Mayu!"
*Of course I hesitate. But in the end, that settles it.*
Mayu: "Then... we'll go! As long as we definitely get some cookies and cream. Saying you have no money because you did too much shopping is out of the question!"
Himeno: "I know~ After all, I'm something of a glutton myself."
Mayu: "Fine, fine!"
*In the end, we settle on Manten Aquarium and ice cream for Sunday. Since I have already received my reward money for moving on to the next grade, I don't need to worry about my allowance.*
Mayu: "Still... it's been five years..."
Himeno: "Did you say something, Mayu?"
Mayu: "Nope, nothing."
*I bite my tongue, covering the words that had carelessly spilled out.*
Himeno: "I'm looking forward to the aquarium. It's probably been about ten years."
Mayu: "I'm looking forward to the cookies and cream. It's probably been about ten years."
Himeno: "That's not true!"
Mayu: "It's the truth! That's the only thing I'll eat there!"
*Himeno goes "You little--" as she playfully stretches my cheeks. The end of lunch chime sounds, and Himeno goes back to her own seat.*
*If it's with Himeno, I'll be fine. That's what I told myself at the time.*
*Sunday is three days after that. The day I promised to go with Himeno to Manten Aquarium has arrived. We are to meet at noon at the Manten-cho Station. As is customary for me, I arrive thirty minutes early.*
*Leaning against a pillar, I do nothing but space out.*
*Tons of people pass before me. Manten is really crowded on days off. Even so, there aren't really that many families or groups of students, everyone around me appearing to be adults. Because of that, I quickly begin to feel uneasy.*
*Within this crowd, I am alone.*
*Inexplicably, my head begins to hurt.*
Mayu: "I wonder if Himeno will be here soon..."
*I mutter to myself, then look at my shoes.*
*Deep blue sandal boots that were this year's newest product. When Mom and I had stopped off at a department store on Saturday, she bought them for me after some coaxing on my part.*
*They were far from what you'd call high quality, but they were still a bit pricey on a junior high schooler's allowance.*
*I'm not usually the type to beg, but I couldn't take my eyes off of these boots. My mom bought them with almost no resistance, saying, "You're going out of town, so you need to look your best."*
*I liked both their form and their color, and found them a better fit for the summer season than anything else.*
*I take my cell phone out and check the time. There are still fifteen minutes to go until we're to meet.*
*Since Himeno is always three minutes later than the time we establish, I've got about another twenty minutes. I finished reading my paperback on the train, so I have nothing to kill time with.*
*There is nothing I can do about it, so I decide to keep myself from boredom by watching the people passing by. If I don't keep myself distracted, horrible thoughts will revive in my mind.*
Mayu: "I'm always worrying about what will happen if I'm late though."
*It had actually been about a month and a half since Himeno and I had gone out. We always talked at school, but rarely spent time together afterwards.*
*Anyone who saw us probably thought we were opposites.*
*Outings like this aside, I often spend my days off at home.*
*When I was in elementary school, our family would go out often, but lately that's been limited to dining out on Saturdays.*
*So when I'm asked what I do when I stay at home, I usually just say I do nothing. Doing homework and watching TV is stuff that seems pretty common to me.*
*On the other end of the spectrum is Himeno, who goes out regularly. I often hear stories of when she went to karaoke on weeknights or about how she traveled out of town on Sundays.*
*She's a member of an active sports club and often wants to hang out with them afterwards.*
*Going out of town on the already limited days we have off tends to make me think, "Even though it's a day to rest, I don't feel rested at all."*
*In other words, something like this is a difference between Himeno and I.*
*Without much common ground between us, why we became friends is--*
Mayu: "--!"
*Suddenly, the light fades from my vision.*
*It wasn't just that it had become dark, but that I had the sensation of being unable to breathe.*
*But rather than sweating it, I get a little mad.*
Mayu: "Ng--!"
Himeno: "Ahaha, sorry, sorry!"
*Hearing my wordless protest, Himeno pulled her hands away. I get playing the "Who am I?" game while covering someone's eyes, but covering up someone's mouth too was just like Himeno.*
*When I puff out my cheeks and object, Himeno just smiles at me.*
Himeno: "It's because you were staring into space, Mayu!"
Mayu: "... Auu."
*Instead of apologizing, Himeno blew on the nape of my neck. Without thinking, I made a weird sound. Embarrassed, I looked around two or three times, but the pedestrians walking by seemed unperturbed and didn't even look our way.*
*The culprit who had surprised me moves in front of me before I realize it.*
Mayu: "You're mean, Himeno!"
Himeno: "Ahaha-- but I got to hear something good."
Mayu: "No, you didn't. Morever, you're..."
*As I say that, I check the time on my smartphone. It's three minutes to noon, our agreed upon meeting time.*
Mayu: "It's not... time yet. How rare."
Himeno: "That's rude, geez. I'll make you make a weird noise again."
*Saying that, Himeno opens and closes her fingers in small, swift grasping motions. At times like this, I know I had better change the subject or get messed with by Himeno again.*
Mayu: "Oh, by the way, did you buy a new one?"
Himeno: "You mean my necktie? Yes I did! I thought I should get one to match my shorts. You and I haven't hung out since around midterms, Mayu. If I buy something today, I'll really be out of money."
Mayu: "... What about the aquarium? You're still set on finding out about the urban legends then?"
Himeno: "O~f cou~rse. That's my number one goal. But as usual, I can't count the things I want to do on just one hand."
Mayu: "OK, OK, I get it. As long as I eat cookies and cream. That is absolute."
Himeno: "I haven't forgotten~ Anyway, Mayu, aren't your sandals new?"
*Himeno says as she looked at my feet, talking about three times as fast as she does in class.*
Mayu: "Yeah, nice, aren't they?"
Himeno: "They're super cute! How cool, I want a pair too~"
Mayu: "Nope, then we'll be too similar. And these are the only pair of sandals I have for the summer."
*Talking like this, it put me at ease.*
*Unlike myself, who is childish, Himeno knows a lot about fashion and trends. Even here in Manten, it seems like she blends in. Most importantly, by being with Himeno and constantly chatting, I no longer feel lonely.*
Mayu: "Oh yeah, Himeno, why did you bring such a huge bag?"
Himeno: "I'm glad you asked."
*With a "Heehee," Himeno opens the Boston bag she had let sink down from her shoulder. A variety of things come out, such as a flashlight, insect repellent, a simple blanket, a first aid kit, matches, and snacks like chocolate and cookies.*
Mayu: "What is all this? Himeno, where do you plan on going?"
*Ignoring the twitch developing in my face, Himeno thumps her chest.*
Himeno: "Geez, to the aquarium! But since we're investigating urban legends, we should at least have some supplies."
*Rather than an investigation, the equipment she has reminds me more of going camping in the mountains. The snacks and blanket aside, where in a city this size would we need insect repellent or a flashlight?*
*I keep those feelings to myself and just smiled. I let out a breath that is half exasperated, half amused. I don't get sick of being with Himeno.*
*Whether Himeno is aware of my exasperated gaze or not, she starts to take something out, then quickly shoves it back in. She gives me a smile implying some secret.*
Himeno: "... Ah, no wait! It's too soon for you to see that~"
Mayu: "Ah! What were you about to pull out?"
Himeno: "I'm not tell~ing."
*Upon saying that, Himeno quickly closes the bag. The way I see it, it is a wonder she fit all of the supplies she had out just a moment ago. I wonder where it's all being stored.*
Mayu: "By the way, no club today?"
*I ask with the underlying question of whether or not today is a day off for the club. At my question, Himeno averts her eyes.*
Himeno: "No~pe, probably... no club today."
Mayu: "Really?"
*Giving a brief answer, I start walking.*
*Upon hearing her reply, I immediately understand it as, "Ah, she skipped it again," but not wanting to ruin the merry mood, I merely quicken my pace. I'm not in any clubs, but even I know it's not good to skip.*
*Speaking honestly, I want her to show up for her club, as she seems to be respected even by students from other schools. Coming here together like this makes me equally guilty though.*
*Once I get thinking, I don't usually stop, so the conversation shifts to a ramen shop that catches my eye.*
Himeno: "O~h, Mayu, do you wanna get lunch?"
Mayu: "Huh, you caught that?"
*Feeling as if she has seen right through me, we head into a nearby coffee shop.*
*It is, apparently, famous following its coverage in a magazine for a sandwich that was overflowing with vegetables. I say apparently because on excitedly finding the restaurant, Himeno shared this.*
*Compared to a hamburger shop, this place is a bit intimidating and trendy to me, but since I'm with Himeno it doesn't bother me.*
*Shoving the roast chicken sandwich with extra olives that I ordered into my mouth, the unease I have been feeling disappears.*
*In contrast to my fervor, Himeno eats her avocado-and-shrimp sandwich in small bites.*
Himeno: "Well then, about our plans for today~"
*Himeno starts in as soon as we have finished eating.*
Himeno: "We'll start with the aquarium! No matter what, that's the main thing! So we'll look into the 'old man's ghost' and the 'closed off Deep-sea Fish booth' rumors. After that, we'll go to the shops and finally have some ice cream!"
Mayu: "I think it's better to get the cookies and cream first."
Himeno: "If you eat two times you'll mess up your stomach. And get fat. You won't be able to wear a bathing suit."
Mayu: "Aw... Himeno, it's no fair for you to put it that way."
*It isn't fair. Himeno is in the Exercise Club so she will be fine, but for someone like me who doesn't exercise enough, it makes me stop and think. Having said I wanted to go to the pool, it is all the more important.*
Himeno: "OK, then we'll stick with my plan. That was a delicious meal~"
*Urged on by Himeno, I decide to leave the ice cream shop for another time.*
*Talking about silly things together, we leave the station behind. As opposed to the station's excellent cooling system, the city streets have a peculiar humidity and heat to them that envelopes our bodies.*
*The beginning of July -- summer has only just begun and I can already tell it is going to be a tough one.*
*We weave our way through the crowds until we are ten minutes away from the station, finally ending up in front of a skyscraper.*
*The building is a symbol for the people of Manten, a combined facility for commerce -- what you might call a fashion building in reference to the businesses it contains.*
*Manten is a town known as a place people go for amusement. If you really want to have a good time, they have a movie theater, arcades, and several shops, though you need to have money.*
*Because of that, young men and women are more numerous than boys and girls.*
*In this town of people older than us, Himeno scans the panels for each floor without fear, saying "Hmm, the aquarium is..." People are concentrated around the panels.*
*Of little help to Himeno and trying to take up as little space as possible, I leave the search to her and gaze up at the building. I look up and up, but the top of the building is so far I think my neck will snap.*
Mayu: "That's magnificent..."
*This building was built not long after I was born. It had been finished before I could walk, but the first and only time I came here was in elementary school.*
*The instant I think back on my first visit, my head begins to hurt.*
Mayu: "No... I have to try not to think about it."
*Those are things from long ago, things from the past, things that are done. I can do nothing but deal with the pain that feels as if something is pressing against the inside of my skull.*
*I finally hear Himeno calling me in a loud voice, "The elevator's this way! Come on!" I push away my headache and step through the automatic door.*
*The information at the entrance and the placement of the crane game are exactly the same as that day, giving me a slightly bad feeling.*
*From the elevator landing, we need to head for the top floor of the separate Manten East Building.*
*The elevator is the type that has a glass window so you can see out. That is the only thing that distracting me from my gloom.*
*A crowd of people get on with us, but almost all of them are gone by the time we reach the floor that contains the aquarium. They probably stopped off at the apparel or sundries shops.*
*It isn't that we aren't interested, but Himeno recommends, "It's crowded now, so let's come back when it's less busy," and that keeps us back, deciding to stick with the plans we made back at the coffee shop.*
*Stepping onto our floor, the atmosphere immediately switches to a lively one. The sounds of chatter are drowned out by the roar of a large fan.*
*The lighting is dim and tinted slightly blue. The windows are also covered, giving the feeling that it has suddenly become night.*
*As this is an aquarium, it makes sense that the concept is based around the ocean. Yes, the pungent smell of salt is a major change.*
Mayu: "Amazing... it's like we've dived into the deep sea."
Himeno: "That's what I thought too. Instead of being targeted at families, lately it seems more like a date spot for adults -- or at least that's what the magazines said, but it's true."
*Even though this is my second time coming here, it feels like the first time.*
*Next to the entrance is the souvenir shop, though it lacks the usual boisterous voices. It appears to be a place that can be reached both from the inside and outside, but it doesn't seem to get many customers.*
*Just as Himeno had said, the aquarium seems to target adults. In an atmosphere like that, even Himeno looks nervous.*
Himeno: "Hey, Mayu..."
Mayu: "What's up?"
Himeno: "I forgot to tell you one important thing."
*Himeno makes a mysterious face and brings her mouth up to my ear.*
Mayu: "What? Did you drop your wallet or something?"
Himeno: "No, that's not it... This is something that I heard that's a bit of an urban legend, but they say the amount of people going missing is on the rise. There's a rumor that this month alone, the number missing is well into the double digits."
Mayu: "... Huh?"
*We have this conversation in front of the metal rack that holds the map of the aquarium. Aside from the map, there is a guide to Manten East Building and information about affiliated hotels.*
*During that time, we hear the sound of the elevator opening and closing, but do not see the people going into the aquarium.*
Himeno: "Manten used to be a shopping district, right? That seems to be why, even if there were a lot of runaways, the media wouldn't pay much attention--"
Mayu: "But...?"
Himeno: "Supposedly many of the missing persons had stopped off at Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "That rumor, who did you hear it from...?"
Himeno: "Who indeed? No one in particular. You know how rumors spread..."
*At Himeno's words, I feel a cold shiver along my spine.*
*Regardless of how credible the rumor is, a sense of dread shoots through me.*
*Considering my past, it's natural that I would feel connected to missing persons in my age range, especially if they are runaways. If that is the case it would send chills along the back of my neck.*
*Why on Earth--*
Mayu: "You could have told me before this..."
Himeno: "Weelll, I thought if you heard about it, Mayu, you might not have wanted to come."
Mayu: "Oh, so you forgetting to tell me was a lie then, you little--"
*I had intended to protest firmly, but it still ended rather anticlimactically.*
*Ghosts may exist, and they still may be unable to come back, but that doesn't mean I'm interested in visiting places where they are.*
*This is proof that Himeno has her own strange interests, yet it still makes me feel even more worried.*
Himeno: "This rumor has a continuation... specifically 'people who saw the Deep-sea Fish booth' never came home!"
*Himeno relays those two pieces of information that frighten me with a smile.*
*The rumor itself aside, she wanted to pass over the question of why people were able to see the "closed off Deep-sea Fish booth" in the first place, not wanting to discuss it at all.*
Mayu: "Wait a minute, Himeno--"
*Something else is tied in beneath that fear. Something about the aquarium... and the missing persons who didn't come home.*
*That was, essentially--*
Himeno: "A-Are you ok, Mayu? Sorry, I guess I scared you a bit too much. I think this story's pretty exaggerated, so don't worry about it! I heard it's a fun aquarium. I think we should take those stories with a grain of salt... right?"
*Though her kindness is misdirected, it's kindness just the same. Having involuntarily sat down, I take the hand Himeno extends to me. As I slowly rise to my feet, I respond, "... Yeah, you're right."*
*To think that I would hear of a connection to my precious memory in a place like this.*
*It's strange, after five years the memories should have faded. I tell myself this, then let out a small sigh.*
*Himeno is actually showing concern, so I sit down on a bench in the rest area next to the souvenir shop. Himeno even brings me a can of cocoa. As I'm drinking it, she buys our tickets.*
*With her doing all of this, I have to go with her.*
Hiyoshi: "Are you okay, Miss?"
*The fact that I'm thinking thoughts like "This canned cocoa is a little weak," is proof that I'm cooling down. As I'm starting to feel better, a male voice calls out to me.*
*While holding my half-finished cocoa, I turn in the direction the voice came from. Rather than the boy's voice I expect, it belongs to an adult, so I respond timidly.*
Mayu: "Um... I'm OK, so..."
Hiyoshi: "Oof, no need to be so vigilant."
*He's wearing a gray knit cap and a brown running shirt. He's tall, even for a guy, so I have to look up at him. His build is almost all muscle, and my shoulders shudder a bit in surprise.*
*He appears well over the age of twenty, so I can't think of a reason he would want to talk to us.*
*Whether he realizes I'm looking up at him or not, he plunks down right next to me. Even after he sits down, we aren't sitting at the same height. Pretending to fix my posture, I move away from him a little.*
Mayu: "... I'm not."
*I say, as if he had flushed it out of me. Actually, it was less my physical condition and more what was on my mind, but I was hesitant to explain that to a stranger from the get go.*
Hiyoshi: "Guess not, your face looks really upset. You all right? Can you get back home? Need me to take you to the station?"
Mayu: "U-Um..."
*The smile he gives me baffles me. The reason was because that smile seemed unnatural. I definitely had no recollection of him, and I've never had this much contact with a man who was not a relative.*
Hiyoshi: "Ah, no need to worry. I work part-time in this building, so I'm familiar with Manten."
Mayu: "I'm fine, really."
*Thanks to my nerves and my wariness, I end up speaking unnaturally fast. Moreover, if he really is a kind person, I might end up abusing his good intentions.*
Hiyoshi: "... How cold."
Mayu: "Ah, it figures."
*I say, again without thinking. Without my knowing what he's so happy about, the man's mouth loosens into a grin.*
Hiyoshi: "Hmm, you're pretty clever... I give up. But you know, I seriously am worried about you? Your face is pale and all."
Mayu: "That's..."
*His kind words taking me by surprise, I inadvertantly avert my eyes. For some reason, I become strangely embarrassed, and begin tapping my index finger on my can of cocoa.*
Hiyoshi: "Well, that aside, since you came to have fun let me show you around."
Mayu: "We are really just fine."
Hiyoshi: "... Geh."
*The words come out so naturally they surprise even me. I have now become a complete idiot, and my eyes meet his.*
Hiyoshi: "I work part-time in this building, so I'm familiar with it. This area, that is."
Mayu: "........."
*Looking at it positively, it seems I have been posed a question, but looking at it negatively, it seems I've had a question thrust on me, so I remain silent.*
Hiyoshi: "He~y... are you listening?"
*I'm looking down and take out my smartphone.*
*Surely looking unwell in a public place is likely to garner you sympathy. But having people I don't know worry about me is unpleasant, so I straighten my spine as much as I can.*
Hiyoshi: "Is that... your attempt to show me you're fine?"
*In reality, I am causing him trouble.*
Hiyoshi: "Well, no need to worry. I work part-time in this building, so I'm familiar with Manten."
Himeno: "Hey, what are you doing?"
Mayu: "Himeno!"
*I gasp and look up. Himeno is there carrying two tickets and some pamphlets.*
*With her hands on her hips and her cheeks puffed out, Himeno glares at the man with narrowed eyes.*
Himeno: "I think the only ones allowed to hit on junior high schoolers would be high schoolers."
Hiyoshi: "N-No I'm not trying to hit on anyone... This girl just looked unwell is all."
Mayu: "I think it was... what you'd call hitting on someone."
Hiyoshi: "Geh."
Himeno: "You're the worst!"
*Himeno pulls my arm with a jerk, pulling me backward. Then she inserts herself between the man and I.*
Himeno: "Hey... Mister..."
*Himeno mutters, facing the man's chest. On hearing her say "Mister..." and giving me a puzzled look, the man shows us the plastic nametag hanging from his neck.*
Mayu: "Was it... hitting on someone?"
*I turn to the man and tilt my head. Since Himeno has come, I've relaxed a bit and can look him in the eyes.*
Hiyoshi: "You're... asking me that?"
Himeno: "So that WAS what you were doing!"
*Himeno says that in an exasperated voice in my place. She says, "You're the worst," and grabs my arm, pulling me to her side. The man rests his elbows on his knees and shrugs. He punctuates the shrug with a sigh.*
Himeno: "Mayu, let's go... and hey, Mister...?"
*Himeno mutters, facing the man's chest. On hearing her say "Mister..." and giving me a puzzled look, the man shows us the plastic nametag hanging from his neck.*
Mayu: "........."
*Not knowing how to respond, I stay silent. On seeing that I'm troubled, he turns to Himeno who has entered his field of vision. Upon doing so, Himeno interjects with a "Hey!"*
Himeno: "See, you can't trust someone who just says kind things."
Mayu: "Are you... speaking from personal experience?"
Himeno: "No... and what are you coming at me like that for, Mayu? Hey Mister, please don't make such a weird face! And, Mister...?"
*Himeno mutters, facing the man's chest. On hearing her say "Mister..." and giving me a puzzled look, the man shows us the plastic nametag hanging from his neck.*
Hiyoshi: "Ah, I must have forgotten to take this off."
Mayu: "Himeno, speaking of which, he did say he works part-time in this building."
Himeno: "Ah, then is that... his entry card or something?"
*He slaps his knees and rises to his feet. He probably played some kind of sport or something, because the movement as he stands looks so effortless.*
Hiyoshi: "I'm Hiyoshi Kenji. I work part-time at the shop downstairs. With this entry card, I can get into Manten Aquarium at a discount."
Mayu: "Is it... a company employee discount?"
Hiyoshi: "Something like that. I'm not exactly a company employee, but I get in at half-off anyway. So... I come here often."
Himeno: "Really~ That's quite unexpected for you, Mister."
Hiyoshi: "When you become older, you'll crave these soothing experiences as well... and I'm not old enough to be a 'Mister!'"
Mayu: "Now he says it..."
Himeno: "Compared to us, you're definitely old enough to be a 'Mister.'"
*Hiyoshi-san grimaces angrily, then sniffs in a self-deprecating manner.*
*He appears to be a little older than a college student so calling him old enough to be a "Mister" is probably rude, but Himeno only said the impression he gave was pretty close.*
*With the aquarium as a shared point of interest, Himeno's attitude towards Hiyoshi-san softens. As proof, Himeno talks with Hiyoshi-san about the shops in this building.*
*I don't really know a lot about brands, so I sip my remaining cocoa and listen from the outside. My iced cocoa had become lukewarm cocoa.*
Himeno: "Well, Mister, are you also well-versed in 'urban legends'?"
*On hearing Himeno's question, my head pops up in surprise. The corners of Hiyoshi-san's mouth lift into a grin.*
Hiyoshi: "Hmm, that's rather unexpected for you as well. So you're... interested in such things?"
Himeno: "Of course I am. You're only young once, so you have to satisfy your curiosity. We're looking into the closed down Deep-sea Fish booth. There's a rumor that it's open on the day of the full moon... Do you know anything about it?"
*Hiyoshi-san places his hand on his chin and looks down, grunting loudly. He glances at me, looks over at the entrance, then finally turns to Himeno and opens his mouth.*
Hiyoshi: "I think you need to give up on that one thing. Naturally, since I'm a regular here, I often hear rumors like 'there are spirits floating in the tanks' or 'every time a person dies, the jellyfish tank increases by one' and so on."
Hiyoshi: "Himeno-chan, the story you mentioned shouldn't be anything more than one of those rumors, but..."
Himeno: "Stop dancing around it. Just tell me!"
Hiyoshi: "... Previously, I knew someone who went looking into this just as you girls are doing now, Himeno-chan. He went missing soon after."
Mayu: "Miss... ing?"
*Without thinking about it, I join their conversation. If someone has gone missing here at Manten Aquarium, it holds significance for me as well.*
Himeno: "Is that... for real?"
*I glance back in surprise, Himeno is still looking at him with doubt in her eyes. Hiyoshi-san listens to what Himeno said and with a playful smile, responds, "Ju~st kidding, it's a lie."*
Hiyoshi: "I wonder if it is... a lie."
*Because of the words he muttered, my curiosity has been piqued. I couldn't imagine the tense face he made as he told the story of an acquaintance who went missing being faked.*
Himeno: "That's right~ But if that's the case, then this really might interest you, Mayu."
Mayu: "I've... thought from the beginning that it would be better just to give up."
Himeno: "No way. I already bought the tickets anyway."
*Himeno's face shows that she's enjoying herself to the end, and she takes my arm to gently pull me closer to her. This was the signal that she was ready to go, and in my mind I prayed that nothing would happen.*
Hiyoshi: "Well then, wanna walk around with me?"
Mayu: "No way."
*For some reason, unaware of what I was doing, I flat-out refused. On hearing my insistence, Himeno takes my hand and begins giggling.*
Himeno: "Mayu, that made the old guy feel bad."
Mayu: "Ah... I, uh... I'm so sorry."
*Realizing I had said too much after all, I turn to him and bow.*
*Hiyoshi-san apparently mutters something in a low voice, but because of the distance between us I can't make out what he says.*
Himeno: "Mayu. That should finish it."
*Not sure of what she meant, we headed for the gate to Manten Aquarium.*
Mayu: "I think we'll pass."
Hiyoshi: "It's fine, don't worry about it. I have the day off anyway. Besides I think you'd be better off having someone as knowledgable about this place as I am..."
Himeno: "Mister, that's not a good look. Mayu said she's got no interest in you."
*Himeno's late response seems to pierce right through Hiyoshi-san.*
Mayu: "Himeno, i-it's not that I'm not interested. But it's not that I am, either..."
Himeno: "... Mayu, don't say anymore than you already have."
*A bitter grin spreading across his face, Hiyoshi-san waves to us.*
*Without looking back, we head for the gate to Manten Aquarium.*
Mayu: "... That's fine."
Hiyoshi: "Oh, surprising how quick you got into a good mood."
*Hiyoshi-san snaps his fingers and rushes to our side.*
Himeno: "Out, out~!"
*As she says this, Himeno steps in front of me and makes a big "X" mark with both hands.*
Himeno: "Mayu, don't be tricked by an old guy like him! He uses kind words to get close to you but he's thinking weird things inside his mind."
Hiyoshi: "Himeno-chan, that's not it at all."
Mayu: "What do you mean by weird things, Himeno?"
Himeno: "W-Well..."
*Hiyoshi-san narrows his eyes and grins widely. He glosses over my question and asks, "What, Himeno-chan?" For some reason, Himeno's face goes red, and she grabs me tightly by the wrist.*
Himeno: "Sexual harassment! Definitely sexual harassment, Mister!"
*Without giving Hiyoshi-san a second look, Himeno drags me to the gate.*
*Hiyoshi-san smiles and shrugs his shoulders.*
*The aquarium entrance is so dark that we can't see in front of us until we are inside. I can feel the cold air on my skin. I get a sensation of something heavy, causing me to rub my arm.*
*Naturally there is no mark indicating something has touched me and, called back by Himeno's "Hey, let's go," I pass my ticket through the machine.*
Himeno: "Meh~ It figures that the whole 'if you come on the day of a full moon, all the water will change into blood' thing is a lie. The water's normal."
Mayu: "... Of course it is, geez."
*Right away, Himeno says so regretfully. Even so, she's still unable to hold back her excitement at having come to the aquarium, and her steps are fittingly light.*
*As we come out of the gate there are several small tanks nearby. Manten Aquarium's first and most celebrated exhibit is the one displaying fish native to Tokyo Bay.*
*There are four tanks in this corner, from left to right, each one showing the environments of the river flowing into Tokyo Bay: brackish waters, shoals, and open water come one right after the other.*
*Himeno giggles as rockfish, porgy, and other fish we recognized from the supermarket are also there.*
*This booth also showcases environmental problems in order to prompt consciousness of the issue, causing a tightening in my chest. Water pollution is a serious problem. Tomorrow I think I'll try asking Mom to use a little less detergent.*
Himeno: "Hmm, suddenly it gotten like this--"
*Himeno's reaction is the polar opposite of mine. Even as I'm reading the panels, she's uninterested in the display.*
*Just like that, I read every last panel. Himeno has long since stopped watching me, crossing her arms and puffing out her cheeks.*
Mayu: "Thanks for waiting, Himeno."
Himeno: "Geez, you're too serious, Mayu~ If you act like that the whole time, the day will be over... er, they'll close."
Mayu: "It'll be fine. This area isn't that big... And it's not like I'm going to read everything."
*As soon as she hears what I said, an expression of dissatisfaction appears on Himeno's face.*
Himeno: "Well, let's head to the next area. Next is... the tunnel tank! There might be ghosts. Oh, maybe that'll be with the human-like fish?"
Mayu: "Geez, c'mon already."
*Even saying something like that, upon entering the aquarium I had begun to enjoy myself. Upon cheerfully calling out to Himeno, my previous unease has disappeared, leaving me ready to move forward with a light step.*
Himeno: "Amazing..."
Mayu: "Really amazing, and pretty..."
*With no door to partition this booth and the next, passing beside this tank took us to the next one, where larger breeds of fish swim about.*
*This is no ordinary tank, but rather a giant tube-like tunnel, with the left and right sides connected above our heads in an arc.*
*As we walk through the tunnel, we're able to watch the fish moving about in three dimensions. We bring our faces as close as we can to the glass, giving the illusion that we're in the ocean.*
*Just watching the mantas, the rays, the various types of sharks, and the sea turtles can be intoxicating for us humans.*
*When I was a child I thought the ceiling might crack, but now I pass that feeling up, leaving only the throbbing of my heart.*
*The water is a giant bundle of blue, various colors within it shooting every which way.*
*Being able to see how big they are up close, separated by mere centimeters of glass, is so impressive it gives me the greatest realization of our shared existence.*
*Here, Himeno's pace slows and she takes her steps one-by-one.*
Himeno: "Hey, Mayu, look at those huge fangs!"
Mayu: "Himeno, that fish is cute too."
Himeno: "Uwa, the stingray's face is creepy..."
Mayu: "Really~ I think it's cute."
Himeno: "Ah, a manta, it's huge--!"
Mayu: "That little one over there looks yummy."
*Talking as the words come to us, our conversation seems out of sync. Our feelings of being touched, however, are the same, and Himeno's enjoyment is obvious.*
*Being surrounded by the water and the fish as we are, we feel as though we're freed from any sense of time.*
Himeno: "Mayu, doesn't this ray's face look human?"
Mayu: "Maybe so..."
Himeno: "It does! The mystery of the human-faced fish is solved."
Mayu: "N-Naturally... Well, if you're ok with it, then so am I."
*Thus, we learned the true nature of one of the urban legends.*
*I was fine either way, but for Himeno this way is probably best. Though from Himeno's enthusiastic stance, even as she watches the fish she probably realizes just how implausible it all is.*
*Rather than get tired of it, though, I'm having fun.*
*We move leisurely through the tunnel, coming to a stop at the center.*
*Up to this point, only the big things have caught our eyes, but on closer inspection of the tank, there's also sand piled up to our knees, with shrimp, shellfish, small fish, and other such creatures dwelling there.*
*Compared to the manta rays, the small shrimp, which can't even be considered child-sized, seem so cute and innocent as they swim about, breaking through the sand.*
Mayu: "Hey, look Himeno."
Himeno: "Ahh~ It's ti~ny."
*As I expected, Himeno has followed the human-faced ray, and on seeing my finger tip, speaks in a playful tone. Upon sensing the tremors from Himeno poking the glass, the ray kicks at the sand and flees deeper into the tank.*
Mayu: "Man, it's gone now."
*As I say this to Himeno, I smile at a small shrimp. Himeno does the same, smiling widely.*
*Before long the two of us are looking up at the ceiling, toying with the question of just how high it is. Even looking up, we can only see the fish swimming, never the ceiling itself.*
*Looking up from the bottom of the sea, one would normally see the rays of the sun piercing the water, but looking up here was the opposite, with the bottom of the sea seeming to continue forever.*
*It could have been the reflection of the light, or the ceiling really might have been too high. As I realize I can't be sure, my eyes settle on a small but characteristically long-nosed shark swimming past.*
Mayu: "Sharks..."
Himeno: "Sharks...? That's a ray, isn't it? You just saw it from behind earlier."
*As soon as I mutter that, Himeno responds. It's true that although I'm interested in this type, Himeno has fixated on the human-faced ray.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
Himeno: "What's up?"
*Himeno asks, but I falter. This conversation gives me a sense of what you might call déjà vu. How strange, I felt as if... I've had this conversation before. At that moment, my vision is dyed ultramarine.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*It was no longer Himeno in front of me, but someone else that had caught my attention.*
Mayu: "This can't... be real...?"
Himeno: "Hey, Mayu?!"
*The ultramarine color unchanged, within it a face like mine passes before me. A face like mine from five years ago.*
*Wearing an ultramarine overcoat that merges with the color of my vision, the girl turns to me and seems to be giving me a gentle smile.*
Himeno: "Mayu! Wait!"
Mayu: "Ma... ri..."
*Without hearing Himeno calling me back, I start running. We haven't finished looking at everything in the tube tunnel tank, but there's something more important right in front of me.*
*Long hair that looks almost transparent. A thick, blue coat. Cute boots with fur on them. There was no way I could forget that outfit.*
*That's because it was the outfit my little sister -- Nakanobe Mari -- wore when she went missing here five years ago.*
*The girl quickens her pace as if playing with me and disappears out the other side of the tunnel. There are hardly any people there, but I lose sight of her between the shadows of the tank and the corner.*
Mayu: "Mari -- how...? Why...?"
Himeno: "Hey Mayu, wait!"
*Breathing heavily, Himeko chases after me. I say "Sorry," and break away from her, searching for the vision of Mari.*
*As I come out of the tunnel I survey the area, spotting a winter skirt nearby. I chase after that swaying form.*
*Behind me I can hear the intensity of Himeno's rushing steps and quicken my pace.*
Mayu: "Kya!"
*Having fallen into a trance, I realize what's going on when I slam into someone and fall on my backside.*
Sayo: "Tch."
*The girl clicks her tongue as she picks up what she had dropped. As I start to say "Excuse m--" to her, she's already moving past me and running off somewhere.*
*Because of that brief incident, I'm unable to find my way out of my confusion.*
Mayu: "Anyway, Mari..."
*I feel bad about the person I'd run into, causing me to feel flustered. In that exchange, I lose sight of both that long hair and that long skirt.*
Mayu: "But you were going this way, right...?!"
*I'm not sure myself where I'm running to, but I think you can say I'm definitely running with all my heart.*
*I pass between more tanks than I can remember, but I don't pay them one bit of attention.*
*I just continue following Mari's back, running. Mari is just as bashful as she was the day she disappeared.*
Himeno: "Mayu, get ahold of yourself!"
*Those few words end my chase. To be more accurate, it was ended by Himeno herself, who had grabbed my wrist.*
Mayu: "Huh......"
Himeno: "You suddenly took off, and you wouldn't turn around no matter how many times I called you. Even when you dropped something you had no idea. What happened all of a sudden?"
Mayu: "Wait Himeno, if I can go after Mari, I'll explain everything, so..."
Himeno: "Hold it!"
*I look back at Himeno upon hearing her extremely hurt voice. I see tears forming in Himeno's eyes, and I turn my whole body to face her.*
*I stand in silence like this for several seconds. If I tell Himeno about Mari, I'm sure she will regret bringing me to Manten Aquarium. When she followed me, she had to have seen Mari.*
*It's natural that she would have some misgivings about me chasing some total stranger, since she doesn't know Mari.*
*Even so, I'm not sure what to say. If I apologize now and play dumb, we can probably go back to playfully searching the aquarium for information.*
*But that is not something I can do. It isn't the similarities that are the problem. Mari is here and just as she had been on that day -- there is no way I can go home without taking her with me.*
Mayu: "I......"
Himeno: "Mayu! Tell me what's going on! Who is Mari? Why do you keep running off without saying anything?"
*Tears are pouring out of Himeno's eyes. I can't understand why she's so sad. But still, my little sister--*
Mayu: "My little sister, she was here."
Himeno: "Little... sister...?"
Mayu: "See, the girl I was chasing, she's my little sister. Mari disappeared five years ago... at this aquarium."
*Looking at Himeno's tear-stained face, I no longer feel like hiding anything. Perhaps in the depths of my consciousness, I'm hoping Himeno will become satisfied and say she wants to look for Mari with me.*
Mayu: "I'll explain in more detail later."
*On top of that when I look back once more, Himeno squeezes my hand and holds me back. This is the gentlest, but the strongest, that she's held my hand.*
*Himeno stares into my eyes, then tilts her head to the side.*
Himeno: "What do you mean... you were chasing someone?"
Mayu: "I told you, the little girl that was in front of me! Wearing the ultramarine coat and the boots, remember? That was Mari, exactly how she looked long ago."
Himeno: "Mayu, snap out of it!"
*Himeno shouts. It isn't simply a loud voice, but one weighed down with great sadness. It definitely gets through to me, but that doesn't mean it will stop me.*
*Because without a doubt, I was right. Mari had been right before my eyes, so I had to chase--*
Himeno: "Let's just say that, even if this Mari girl is here, she would have grown in five years, and her clothes wouldn't be the same. It's summer, so she wouldn't be wearing a coat... Moreover......"
Mayu: "She was here! I saw her!"
Himeno: "No, Mayu, no... Mayu, you weren't... chasing anyone. You just took off running, leaving me behind... ok? Could you just calm down for a bit?"
Himeno: "Our tickets will get us back in, so how about some cocoa in the rest area? I think you're tired, since you were a little shaky earlier."
*Himeno seems pretty frantic, so I know she isn't lying just to persuade me.*
*But there is no way that my seeing Mari there was a lie. That's why I start to speak without really knowing what I want to say.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
Himeno: "Mayu... ok?"
*Wiping away her tears, Himeno smiles at me. Surely she has plenty of strong feelings about this whole thing. I'm sure she has loads to say about me keeping the Mari thing and my reason for resisting visiting Manten Aquarium quiet.*
*Even so, it seems as though Himeno has accepted me. She just smiles and lets it go.*
Mayu: "That's... probably right... Surely if we're by the entrance, we'll know if Mari comes out......"
Himeno: "Mayu... that's good. That's right, that's more like you! Let's go have some cocoa, and you can tell me everything."
*As she smiles, Himeno once again takes my hand into her soft palm.*
Himeno: "Oh, that's right, you dropped this! You gotta be more careful!"
*With the hand that isn't holding my own, she hands me something small.*
*It's a keychain. The blue droplet shape is inlaid with stardust and also holds a starfish charm. It isn't exactly rare, being a giveaway item.*
*But why did Himeno have it? Only questions arise in my mind. Why, when that keychain was--*
*On thinking about it, I instinctively shake off Himeno's hand.*
Himeno: "Hey, what are you doing? After I picked it up for you and all!"
*Himeno's voice is like an arrow, piercing through me. The pain in my chest is nothing more than discomfort.*
*What is Himeno saying? That isn't mine, so I can't possibly have dropped it--*
*And now, I can't look at Himeno's face.*
Mayu: "Liar."
Himeno: "Huh... liar...?"
Mayu: "That's... not mine. It isn't something I dropped. I-It was Mari's. Mari dropped it."
Himeno: "That can't be! I'm not lying! Really... there was no 'Mari-chan' so she couldn't have dropped the keychain!"
*Himeno was becoming gruff as she continued.*
Himeno: "Really... isn't it you who's lying? No one... no one else is here!"
Mayu: "That's enough... Himeno, you dummy."
*Saying only that, I turn my back on Himeno. When I glance back, Himeno's shocked expression is getting further away.*
*She was there, Mari had definitely been there.*
*Because the keychain Himeno had, it belonged to Mari. There was no way I could forget, it was just before Mari went missing--*
Mother: "For the two of you, just this once."
*Upon saying that, Mom handed us each 100 yen coins.*
*That day was a cold winter day. At the Manten Aquarium souvenir area, the smell of construction lingered faintly in the air.*
*There were manta and penguin stuffed animals, stationary with the logo on it, and various souvenirs. At the entrance to that souvenir stand, there were the GachaGacha machines containing that keychain.*
*I don't remember all of the various types, but star sand was packed in the plastic drops, and the charms were all modeled after fish at the aquarium.*
*It was fairly typical for a GachaGacha, but at that time it seemed very enticing to me.*
*It was the same for Mari, and we gazed into the GachaGacha machine. Mari was the one who would usually beg our parents at this point, but neither of us had bought a souvenir and both of us said we wanted the keychains.*
*Since Mom couldn't be completely broken, she emphasized the "one time only" bit.*
Mari: "Ya~ay!"
*Mari held out both hands with glee, while I probably just smiled. I remember that inside, I was just as happy.*
*As to which of us went first, it was of course the more assertive Mari. She was always like this, a cheerful and inquisitive child.*
Mari: "How cu~te. Mr. Star~"
*Mari got a starfish charm keychain. At this point, Mari still didn't know the difference between a starfish and a star.*
*I was next to get one. It was a pink drop, with a jellyfish charm.*
Mayu: "Ah, a jellyfish..."
Mari: "Ah~! That's the one I wanted most~!"
*Mari pointed the index finger of her tiny hand, exclaiming in a loud voice. Mari was just learning how to use first person when she mentioned herself. Instead of a normal "I" or "me", she said it slowly.*
Mari: "Hey~ big sister, trade m--"
Mayu: "No way. I want this one."
Mari: "Uu~ ......"
*Naturally, as I had already fallen in love with that design, I obstinately turned her down.*
*Upon begging my mother for "One more time!", my mother, who had warned us earlier, held her purse strings tight, leaving Mari to sulk.*
Mari: "Fine! Stupid big sister~!"
*Without listening to my mother's admonishments, she ran off.*
*Sulking after being called stupid, I naturally didn't chase her right away. My mother, being optimistic about the aquarium's small size, also waited awhile to go looking for Mari.*
*And now, all I'm left with are my regrets. If only I had gone after her sooner, if only I'd traded her keychains in the first place...*
*Even so the past is unchanging, and I am left with only my regrets to bear. So in case I could ever trade with Mari again, I've stubbornly carried around that keychain, the one with a pink droplet shape and the jellyfish charm, like a treasure.*
*I rummage through my bag, seeking to feel the keychain in my hand.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*Why? At a time like this, I had forgotten to bring it. The keychain wasn't in the bag nor in my pockets.*
Mayu: "Huh..."
*A sigh naturally slipped out of my body.*
*After all, I've lost sight of Mari, and strayed from Himeno. Himeno has the building map, so there's nothing I can do.*
Mayu: "... Huh......?"
*At that moment, a pungent aroma floats by and causes me to speak in surprise. Unable to bear the irritating stench, I pinch my nose.*
*Still, the stench seems to twist in my nose. It's made of the smell of sewage, sulfur, and the nauseating stink of vinegar.*
*Squish.*
*As soon as I try to get away from the smell, something soft creeps along the bottom of my foot. As I hesitantly look down, I realize the true identity of the putrid odor.*
*It's... a corpse. I'm not sure what type, but it's as big as my armspan.*
*The body is slowly decaying and hasn't retained its original form, yet by its bulging, round eyes and slightly extended tail fin, I can just barely tell it was a fish.*
*I couldn't tell right away, but it has a silhouette similar to a pirarucu. I feel like I've seen those eerie glaring eyes, characteristic of a freshwater fish.*
Mayu: "I feel... sick..."
*I move my foot slowly and a small fragment of the corpse sticks to the bottom of my shoe, on the underside of which I notice something wriggling desperately.*
*The slight sound of flapping wings departs from beside my ear, and I understand that the wriggling thing is an insect.*
Mayu: "Ah!"
*Shaking, chills run up my spine as if someone had pressed ice to it. I take a step back and drop my cell phone. The phone reacts with the backlight and LCD screen coming on, illuminating the dead body.*
*The syrupy dead body absorbs the light, and the winged insect that flees to avoid it. There is also a meandering white nematode that seems not to notice.*
*The backlight soon disappears, leaving its stench amidst the darkness. The shadows of those hideous insects are scorched into my retinas, and they sway like the light off a heat haze in the darkness.*
*I immediately shut my mouth, enduring what had come up. The intermingled acidic taste of the vomit that had come up and the scent of stomach acid lingering in my nostrils mixed with saliva and caused my eyes to water in pain.*
*Why was it so painful?*
*I can't figure out the answer to my doubts, not at all.*
*I throw up everything that had welled up inside me. It doesn't all come up at once, taking two, three times of convulsing to get all of the vomit out.*
*It was disgusting. Still even more disgusting, was the fact that the insects had gathered around my vomit as if it were a feast."
*What in the world was happening? Why did I have to relive these memories?*
*I can't figure out the answer to my doubts, not at all.*
*I cover my eyes with both hands. I couldn't have made it if I didn't. *
*But shutting out my vision leaves my sense of smell, causing the smell of rotten water and the stench of the dead body to become more intense, so I uncover my eyes.*
Mayu: "No... what is this...?"
*This is a place for the customers, is it really all right for this to be here? On top of that, I was in the middle of chasing Mari, wasn't I? No, that isn't the problem.*
*It's strange enough that there is a place like this in the middle of an aquarium.*
Mayu: "Anyway, I have to get out of here..."
*The stench is so overpowering, it's as if I can see it.*
*I want to get out of here quickly, satisfying myself with the argument that it's a garbage dump or something, and that if I only get away I'll be fine.*
*At any rate, I can go back the way I came -- no, that's no good. I might run into Himeno. At this moment, I don't want to see her face.*
*My eyes having adjusted a bit to the darkness, I search for a way out.*
*Here the tanks are broken, the little remaining water stinking, and the bodies of living things have tumbled out here and there. I think about how happy I am that I can't see. Even so, to find the exit I'll have to look around.*
*My nose has begun to go numb, and thanks to that I'm somehow able to retain my sanity.*
*Before long I find a dim emergency light behind one of the tanks and head towards it as if it were the last hope I could cling to.*
*I feel squishing several times beneath my feet, but I try to ignore it and move on. As I move towards the emergency light, it seems the smell is becoming faint.*
Mayu: "... Hm?"
*I get used to the scattered shards of glass and the rotten fish corpses just before I reach the door.*
*The tip of my shoe feels as if it has kicked something hard, something different from before. Wondering what I had kicked, I casually look down.*
Mayu: "No... no..."
*I get the sensation that all the blood has stopped flowing through me, and that my heart has stopped completely.*
*The round thing I kicked has patches of hair, two eyes, a nose with a bone to it, and a mouth -- all together it is a human face."
*Speaking accurately, it's a head rather than a face. It's completely lifeless and rolls away with a flopping sound when my foot touches it. Seeing the state the head is in, I begin to feel ill again despite having finally calmed down from before.*
*Of the white cranium, only the forehead area seems to be exposed. It seems this is new and has only just begun to decay.*
*No, for starters.*
*There is no way a human head should be rolling around here. It's my imagination, or it has to be from some model.*
*I haven't yet looked in the direction it was rolling.*
*Convincing myself that it isn't a human head -- it really is different so there was no satisfying myself -- I place my hands on the door in an attempt to escape. Unable to control my shaking hands, it takes three tries before I can open the door.*
*It's a heavy fire door that is usually open.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*I step inside with my thoughts racing, and the atmosphere changes completely once more. I can hear the sound of the door closing behind me.*
*Inside is a very clean place.*
*The transparent water is tinted a faint blue, and inside float fantasy-like jellyfish.*
*Countless pillar-shaped tanks are spread around, and the water shimmers as it reflects the light of the flourescent lamp, red and yellow, green and purple, the jellyfish's bodies diffusing light like a prism.*
*In spite of the strange space I have reached, I'm fascinated by the shimmering live art display.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*I mutter without thinking.*
*Then I quickly shake my head, erasing the image of Himeno's face that appears in my mind. Earlier, she didn't come after me.*
*After all, Himeno chose on her own to part ways from me. I'm sure it's because she dislikes me now. It isn't something I should worry about.*
*Even at a quick glance, I can see that there are various types of jellyfish in the tank. Having made efficient use of the small space, it's a charming area. It vividly expresses the starry sky, as is fitting of the name Manten Aquarium.*
*Compared to the earlier "Fish of the World Booth," this one isn't the least bit in decay.*
Mayu: "I feel worried about Himeno all of a sudden. I'll go back."
*Upon thinking whether or not it's all right for just me to enjoy something so pretty, I reach for the door behind me.*
*Even though we fought -- Himeno and I are friends. Sharing such a lovely memory will surely help us make up quickly.*
*I step forward, and it is at that moment...*
Mayu: "... Huh?"
*When I open the door and step through, there is no "Fish of the World" booth.*
*Before I can even rationalize the scene before me, I'm pulled in by some sort of swell.*
Mayu: "... Urk!"
*Trying to figure out what's wrong, I realize I am having trouble getting air. I really want air, so I pause for breath. My mouth fills with a fishy stench. Brine -- seawater.*
*I know from the salty taste that I've swallowed seawater. The reason I can't breathe... is because all around me is ocean water.*
*I understand that I am in the ocean, devoid of light. No light and no sound, in the darkness.*
*Wondering why, I move my limbs. At this rate, I will drown. It hasn't been more than a minute since I got pulled under the sea.*
*How many seconds have passed at this point?*
*No, that isn't it. I have to get above water. However, up to this point there has been no light overhead, and I'm forced into an inevitable battle with my despair.*
*I frantically endure my suffocation, swimming higher and higher. Suddenly being thrown into the water hasn't thrown off my sense of up or down as the desire to live serves as an instinct to guide me upward.*
*I still can't see, but I'm all right.*
*Just like that, I seem to have answered my own question. I can feel the clothes I'm wearing become heavy. However, if I have time to shed my clothes then I have time to continue upward. My thinking is that alone.*
*I've heard there is high pressure in the deep part of the ocean that crushes any living thing, but my body is completely fine. That means where I am at the moment shouldn't be that deep.*
*If I swim hard enough, I should make it. I just have to hit the surface and take a deep breath.*
*How many seconds have passed at this point? I have no sense of time whatsoever in this darkness.*
*Even so, I know that some time has passed since I was thrown into the ocean. A minute probably passed awhile ago. Here, my consciousness has begun to fade.*
*Immediately after I see the darkness of death, a small light illuminates the tip of my nose.*
*Because I have been swimming so frantically I haven't noticed, but it seems as though I have gone up quite a ways. It will all be all right. The surface is close.*
*I reach out my hand. I reach out towards that light.*
*That is all that I can do.*
*That's because -- my legs won't move.*
*No matter how frantically I try to move them, my legs won't even quiver. It feels as if something has ensnared them.*
*It has the elasticity of a bottomless trampoline. From the tips of my toes to my rear end, that soft sensation wraps tightly around me.*
*I want to think this is a lie. No, I have actually decided I will not believe it.*
*That a giant octopus has wrapped itself around my legs.*
*Its gum-like suckers are the size of the palm of my hand. I can't even imagine how large its entire body is.*
*The light is getting farther away.*
*My eyes have already closed. For some reason, Himeno's smiling face floats before me. Though it should be her angry face that I see last.*
*Just like that, I'm in darkness.*
*As much as it has tightened up, my legs have lost all feeling. No wonder... my legs are no longer... what they once were.*
*With the depletion of my oxygen supply, my brain has already changed into a machine for merely processing my situation. Within the darkness, it dispassionately communicates my nonexistent will to my four limbs, which have lost all sensation.*
*It won't be long before the only thing I can sense is darkness.*
*That's because I have already become part of that darkness--*
Mayu: "Going back is dangerous... I can't do anything by stopping here... I have to press on."
*And so I start towards the starry sky.*
*I gaze in fascination to the right and left of me. If circumstances weren't what they are, I would have stopped and stared into each and every tank.*
*The tanks lined up all together are beautiful enough, but each one is vivid like the night sky inlaid with jewels.*
*Altogether there are seven tanks. Each one holds a different type of jellyfish. Some with poison, some without, categorized by aspects such as where they live.*
Mayu: "They really are...... beautiful."
*I stop and stand in the very center, between the third and fourth tanks.*
Mayu: "I wonder if the last time we came... these tanks were here."
*Nakanobe Mari. My little sister.*
*I lean my back against one of the tanks and sit down. Whenever Mari's face appears in my mind, I lose all of my strength. If anyone saw me I'd be embarrassed, but here and now in Manten Aquarium there is no one around.*
*I heave a sigh and look up at the ceiling. For some reason, a large fan is revolving there.*
*My memories of the last time we visited Manten Aquarium aren't all bad.*
*It was the first time in a long time all four of us had gone out as a family. Mari and I got along really well, and we always held hands as we walked.*
*We would do things like play house or read picture books aloud. Mari was a bit more of a tomboy than I, so even though I was the older sister, she was the one who gave the orders.*
*Since it was the first time the two of us sisters went to the aquarium, it was natural for us to be very excited.*
*Just as one would expect, the first time Mari and I laid eyes on the creatures of the sea, we experienced fear, awe -- and the sense of being deeply moved. The creatures we saw at that time have stayed with me as vivid memories.*
*-- Happy memories, I still have plenty of them.*
*But on such a special day of all days, Mari and I ended up fighting.*
*The rest proceeded just as I remember.*
*Since then, my parents buy me anything I want.*
*However I changed as well, and I didn't really want anything anymore. Greed itself seemed to decline for me, and I never really got excited for anything.*
*That's why, when my mother bought me these boot sandals... I was happy. I was fascinated by their nostalgic ultramarine color.*
*About one year after Mari disappeared, she stopped coming up in conversation.*
*I don't know if my mother and father had accepted it as a tragic accident, or if the sadness had slowly faded, but my new normal had begun.*
*I had become an only child.*
*But that is nothing more than an excuse I can't escape from.*
Mayu: "Mari...... you're here right?"
*Finally it seems my strength has returned, and I stand up. Even though I asked, no answer will come; the thundering ventilation fan in the ceiling is the only sound I can hear.*
*It was almost at the same time I stood up.*
Mayu: "Ah-- Kyaaaah!"
*A crack appears in the glass of the tank behind me. Since the glass doesn't make any noise when it splits, I realize it's broken when water splashes on my rear end.*
*Water continues to flow out, gradually spreading to my feet. The amount of water from the tank is more than just puddles after a heavy downpour.*
*Along with the water flowing forth, the jellyfish that had been in the tank until just a moment ago float out. In order to avoid contact with the water already soaking my sandals, I step back from the puddles without looking away.*
Mayu: "How...? Why......?"
*As if to play further with my already bewildered mind, the tank behind me also cracks.*
*This time I hear the high-pitched noise of glass cracking. Following that, the tanks are each destroyed in turn.*
*Being stuck in the middle of the passage, water is closing in on me from all sides, leaving me pressed in between. The edge of the water is steadily drawing near.*
Mayu: "What should I do...?"
*The words that slide from my mouth also flow away with the water. The colors of the tanks that had once been so vibrant now seem eerie to me.*
*Among these jellyfish are poisonous ones. Even worse, some of them are fatal.*
*On thinking that, my legs begin to shake in fear.*
Mayu: "No......"
*But no matter how frightened I become, I know I can't sit down.*
Mayu: "I have no choice but to run straight through!"
*I no longer have time for doubt.*
*At a glance, the door on the opposite side is closer than the one I came through. From here, the distance isn't too far to the interior door. Running at top speed should be good enough to reach that one.*
*I hesitate for a moment, then grit my teeth and take off running. It's no more than ten meters. It is a distance that should pose no trouble, even for someone like me who tested below average in physical endurance.*
Mayu: "...... Kyaah!"
*When I had run about five meters, just having passed the sixth tank, I fall full force onto my back. The floor is wet, so one might say this should have been expected.*
Mayu: "No... this is disgusting..."
*The water soaks through my skirt and quickly soaks my underwear. The tank water is in direct contact with the skin on my rear. It is cold and wet, uncomfortable but unavoidable.*
*When I put my hand on the floor to try to stand up, I discover that action is a mistake.*
Mayu: "... Ouch."
*I squeeze my eyes shut at the pain that feels like I've been stabbed by a cold metal needle. No, it doesn't just feel like it. I have been stabbed by something.*
Mayu: "Have I been... stung by...?"
*Shaking, I shift my line of sight to my hands. A jellyfish is covering my right hand.*
*At that moment, I get upset that I can't feel the coldness of the water around my right hand.*
Mayu: "I got stung... What do I do? Someone......"
*The pamphlet introducing the jellyfish has gotten mixed up with pieces of glass from the tank.*
*"Man of War." I notice the name without much of a reaction, but it has to be poisonous to be called that. There have also been incidences of fatalities.*
*-- Fatalities.*
Mayu: "Poison... What do I do if I've been stung? Do I clean it? Is it bad if I don't clean it...?"
*I can feel my pulse quickening. I wonder if it's the poison making its way through me.*
Mayu: "What do I do, what do I do, what do I do......?!"
*When I try to will myself to calm down, my impatience ramps up.*
Mayu: "Someone... someone help me......!"
*Trying to use my pulsating right hand for support to push myself up, I slide in the puddle and can't stand.*
Mayu: "It hurts, it really hurts......"
*I put water on the area where I had been stung in an attempt to rinse it out. However, the opening of the wound stings and startles me... I need fresh water.*
*Trying to apply pressure to the vein, I tightly grip the area where I'd been stung.*
Mayu: "Dad, Mom...... Himeno, Mari......"
*Wanting to be saved, I call out as loudly as I can. However, in reality, my voice has already grown hoarse. The corners of my eyes grow hot, and from them fall individual droplets.*
Mayu: "No, I... I'm going to die."
*My voice is no longer really a voice at all.*
Mayu: "Agh...!"
*Before long, an electric shock runs through my right hand. The subsiding of the intense pain brings my senses back to me.*
*But my voice will no longer come out. Even when I try to think of something, nothing happens except a sensation of pain whirling inside me that fills me to the brim.*
*Unable to control my body, I fall to the ground. First my head... then my chest, from my stomach to my feet. Having fallen on my face, half of my body is submerged in water.*
*My heartbeat quickens. I become acutely aware of the flow of my blood.*
Mayu: "...... Ack."
*Unable to stand it, I vomit. The very idea of being able to endure it no longer occurs to me.*
*Even so, the poison won't be expelled from my body. My entire being becoming numb is proof that my blood has been pumping so quickly it has worn down my veins.*
Mayu: "Huff...... huff......"
*Wanting to rinse out the remaining vomit in my mouth, I suck in water. It tastes like salt.*
*Within my body, I'm struggling with all I have. On the outside, my shell has long exceeded the limits of its stamina. Blood is soaking through the capillaries in my eyeballs, and the puddles spreading on the floor are pierced with red.*
*Gradually, I stop feeling my blood flow.*
*Is it because my heart has stopped? Or because my consciousness has disappeared?*
*Either way, my consciousness stops and fades into the darkness.*
*With the same simplicity that a ventilation fan spews out old air, my breath is evacuated from my body.*
*When I come to, I'm still gazing into the cylindrical tank. Vacantly, thinking nothing other than "I wonder if something's in there"? I just stare into that tank.*
*I have neither the urge to call out nor the desire to walk away. Merely the sensation that my consciousness alone is floating along the rippling sea.*
*Why on Earth am I here anyway?*
*What have I come here to do?*
*Whether I can't remember or there was just no reason from the beginning......*
Himeno: "The jellyfish... are pretty. Especially this jellyfish, its color is so bright..."
*The voice that starts talking right in front of me catches my attention for some reason. I feel like I've heard it before somewhere.*
*Well, who is it? I am unable to remember clearly, seeing only a wavering face that won't come into focus.*
Himeno: "Anyway, Mayu... where did she go...? Mayu......"
*That girl, she talked like she was looking for someone. Poor thing. If she's lost someone here she probably won't find them.*
*Hm, wait a second.*
*Where is this to begin with...?*
*Swaying swaying, softly softly.*
*Drip drop, the sound of drops resonating all around me.*
*Gradually ceasing to care, my consciousness melts into the water.*
Mayu: "No... I can't think of anything..."
*I'm becoming panicked. If I don't calm down... With my thoughts swirling around so frantically, I'm getting even more confused. It's a vicious cycle.*
Mayu: "My sandals... are getting dirty......"
*It isn't the first thing that occurs to me, but regardless it's my only clear thought. The puddles of water are gradually drawing closer, and I can't feel anything from the strange jellyfish.*
*I feel my heartbeat speed up as my chest starts to hurt from nervousness.*
*At this point, I have to run through without worrying about my sandals. Though they are sandals, the soles are strong. As long as the jellyfish don't jump, I probably won't get stung.*
*Little by little, the safe spots are disappearing. I tightly pull my legs together, which makes me stagger.*
Mayu: "Kyah!"
*I let out a small scream and straighten up a bit. With nothing behind me to lean against, I'm just about cornered.*
*I wonder if I'll die of jellyfish poison. Rocked by waves of blended nervousness and fear, I carelessly entertain that line of thinking. The meter gauging my fear must be in full tilt -- I think with cool self-deprecation.*
Hiyoshi: "Y-You... from earlier!"
Mayu: "Hu... huh......?"
*The sudden yell confuses me. Upon looking in the direction of the voice, I see that the door has been thrown open and a man is standing there.*
Hiyoshi: "Shoot... Why are your feet exposed? Ah, it can't be helped!"
*That is definitely Hiyoshi-san. The man we had called names is now gently removing his shoes and tossing them over to me. My mind races as I catch them. They are thick-soled boots.*
Hiyoshi: "Put those on and make your way over here. All of the jellyfish floating there have deadly poison... be careful."
*I take off my sandals and put my feet into his boots just as he tells me to. The size doesn't fit at all and they are way too big, but not so big I can't walk in them.*
*I cautiously take a step and, confirming it's all right, take my second, then third steps toward him with my sandals in one hand.*
Mayu: "Thank you... very much..."
Hiyoshi: "You can thank me later. For now, don't look around, just come toward me."
*I know my breathing is increasingly rough, and I get a little embarrassed.*
*However, knowing some concessions will have to be made, I walk through the water and over the jellyfish. When I step on a jellyfish I feel the squishing sensation through the shoes and shudder.*
*There are shards of glass in the puddles, and that makes me want to thank Hiyoshi-san for being considerate enough to lend me such strong shoes.*
*I can walk the remaining meter. Hiyoshi-san seems fairly close.*
*Adjusting my breathing, I carefully take the remaining three steps.*
Mayu: "Ah...!"
*There is no mistake, in my carelessness I have overlooked a transparent jellyfish at my feet. Even though I can't see it I can clearly feel it, and the baggy boots I am wearing throw off my balance.*
*At this rate I will end up on my butt. If I fall now, all of the help I have received will be for nothing.*
Hiyoshi: "Watch out!"
*However, my body is supported by some strength that pulls me back. Hiyoshi-san's right hand has grabbed onto my left wrist.*
*It was a close one. Just like that, I had been pulled out of the room with the broken tanks.*
*As soon as I was out of danger, Hiyoshi gave a mighty pull and shut the fire door.*
Mayu: "This is..."
Hiyoshi: "In front of the souvenir stand... More importantly, why are you here?"
*Hiyoshi-san steadies me against the wall in front of the souvenir stand. The terror from a moment ago hasn't escaped through the heavy, dark gray emergency door.*
Mayu: "Isn't this...... isn't this Manten Aquarium? Besides...... before I met up with you, Hiyoshi-san, I... there was... so much..."
Hiyoshi: "......"
*Hiyoshi-san just stares at me in my flustered state.*
Mayu: "Uhm, I, uh......"
Hiyoshi: "All right, calm down, Mayu-chan."
*A wry grin appears on Hiyoshi-san's face, and he motions in front of the souvenir stand before sitting down.*
*Today I have nothing but bad memories in relation to the floor, so I merely lean against the wall.*
*The GachaGacha machines are still there, but the whole line-up is new, and I don't know a single one of the products.*
Hiyoshi: "Well then. Have you calmed down a bit?"
Mayu: "...... Yes, thank you very much, Hiyoshi-san..."
Mayu: "Um...... what is it you know about this aquarium Hiyoshi-san?"
Hiyoshi: "... Geez, I told you that you can call me by my given name."
*That wry grin appears again, but there is nothing I can do. I have some hesitation about actually calling an older guy by his given name, and I've only heard his first name once, so I don't really remember it.*
*After that, Hiyoshi-san makes a serious face. I think of him as a lighthearted person, but as his mouth straightens, his face seems very focused.*
Hiyoshi: "Well, it's fine. At any rate. There's only one thing I know... This is Manten Aquarium, but it is not Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "It's not... Manten Aquarium?"
Hiyoshi: "Something like that. I don't know the principle or the cause behind it, but this Manten Aquarium is on a distinct time axis from the one you and I visited earlier, Mayu-chan -- that's what I think. I can't think of it as anything else."
Mayu: "A different... time?"
*"Whether past or present... you could even call it a parallel world," Hiyoshi-san adds.*
*I can't understand what he is saying, not in the least.*
Mayu: "How do you know this, Hiyoshi-san?"
Hiyoshi: "That is..."
*Hiyoshi-san mumbles and snorts. He looks my way... and smiles bitterly.*
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan. Didn't you... meet any friends or acquaintances from long ago?"
Mayu: "-- Huh?"
Hiyoshi: "On the contrary, you didn't see any staff or other customers did you?"
*His words seemed very enlightened. I couldn't think of a single thing. After all, I had just come from that experience first-hand.*
*However, there was something that bothered me as well. Hiyoshi-san had said "long ago." Mari, who hadn't changed since that day... but then why--*
Mayu: "I don't get it, that bit about long ago--"
*Therefore, I rejected it. In doing so, I could affirm the fact that I had reunited with Mari.*
Hiyoshi: "Is that so? It seems I said something a bit strange then."
*As he looks away and says this, I have no response for him. Staying like that for a moment, Hiyoshi-san says, "By the way..." and continues speaking.*
Hiyoshi: "I think what caused the glass in the jellyfish tank to rupture was an expansion of gas from something within the tank."
Mayu: "So the gas expanded...... and ruptured the tank from within?"
Hiyoshi: "If we're thinking about the cause, right. Since there're no staff in a space-time distortion, then it's not strange for the oxygen modulator here to break down."
Mayu: "Then it wasn't...... the work of ghosts."
Hiyoshi: "......"
*To my murmuring, he had no answer.*
Hiyoshi: "Even if we have to force ourselves, we should think positively, right? For us to have come here and there to be ghosts, it's not something I can take."
Mayu: "Can we... get back? To our original time axis, or something like it...?"
Hiyoshi: "We can get back, definitely."
*Hiyoshi-san asserts decisively, without looking me in the eye. Once again, he starts the next topic with a quick "By the way."*
Hiyoshi: "That girl that was with you, the one who seemed into the occult and stuff. What happened to her?"
Mayu: "You mean... Himeno?"
Hiyoshi: "Yeah, yeah, Himeno-chan. Weren't you together?"
*I inflate my cheeks without thinking about it. When he notices, I become embarrassed and look down.*
*I rest my hands on my knees, and moving them up and down say, "I don't know!"*
Hiyoshi: "Ah, you're a pretty easy read, Mayu-chan. Got into an argument, did you?"
Mayu: "...... It has nothing to do with you, Hiyoshi-san."
Hiyoshi: "Still... you do get that there is something foul about this aquarium right? Mayu-chan, you experienced that yourself."
*At his words, a pang of pain surges through my chest.*
*That room with the rotten corpses, the sudden cracking of the tank at the jellyfish booth...... When I think that the same accidents could happen to Himeno, I instinctively leap up.*
*Speaking honestly, I have no desire to see Himeno's face... but knowing that the whole aquarium is dangerous, I can't calm down.*
*What ifs -- imagining possibilities alone makes me want to cry.*
Hiyoshi: "Wait a second. Mayu-chan, you must be tired from all that's happened."
*Moving quicker than I do, Hiyoshi-san's hand holds me back.*
Hiyoshi: "I'll go look for Himeno-chan for you. I'm more knowledgeable about this place. You wait here."
*As he says this, Hiyoshi-san gives a thumbs-up.*
*It was a mystery to me. We had only just met today and had an inconsequential conversation, so I wondered why he had come to rescue me. He was even showing concern for Himeno. He was supposedly a total stranger, so why--*
Mayu: "Um......"
Mayu: "Please, Hiyoshi-san."
*Trusting in his masculine appearance, I bow deeply. In reality, it was as Hiyoshi-san had said.*
