Diary: "That guy, ever since he came back he's gotten weird. How are the fish, how is the ghost...? I'm the only one that's actually listening to him."
Sayo: "From here, the writing gets messy."
Mayu: "It does..."
Diary: "Don't play around, stop messing with me. What's going on? Kozuki drowned to death in one of the tanks."
Diary: "I thought he'd been going too far with these rumors, but he was a good guy. Everyone else has started calling it a 'curse.' What's going on, damn it?!"
*Again, someone died in Manten Aquarium.*
*And he was a person who was active in spreading the urban legends. With that, the rumors would become easier to swallow -- for children and adults.*
Diary: "Something is weird. Lately, deaths amongst the creatures we have on display have increased. After all our work to recover, management is hitting a slump again."
Diary: "The weird rumors aren't going away either. Why can I see the ghost of a girl inside the aquarium? It's so over the top, I don't get any of it."
*The two of us continue to read intently.*
Diary: "Lately, I'm seeing fish everywhere. As if seeking prey, I have a voracious appetite. Come to think of it, I've started to think that rumor in particular might be true."
Diary: "After the closing of the exhibit, I've seen a girl with long hair. Even though it's June, she's wearing winter clothes... There's no doubt about that."
Mayu: "What...?"
*Without my noticing, that word slips out.*
*No, that's wrong -- denial appears in my mind.*
*The girl notices that I've fallen into a strange state and she curtly says, "Concentrate."*
*Either way, I'm already concentrating. I take a deep breath and steady my breathing, squinting to follow the words of the diary.*
Diary: "I've gone crazy, or so many of them say. What's crazy about me, I haven't the slightest idea."
Diary: "This is an aquarium, so it shouldn't be weird for there to be fish everywhere. Look, even as I write this, they swim around me. There's... a clownfish."
Mayu: "This is..."
Sayo: "Probably the same phenomenon as I experienced."
Mayu: "Y-Yeah..."
*Fish swimming about, even in places with no water. It had to be connected to the phenomenon in the underground control room.*
*Even with this girl and I having experienced being attacked ourselves, it feels like I'm still resisting this for some reason.*
Diary: "Lately, I feel like I'm going to drown. I wonder if my body is weakening. Nevertheless, everyone is so cruel. Even if I were to start drowning in this room, no one would help me."
Mayu: "Drowning with no water there, that's..."
Sayo: "Yes, the same phenomenon."
Mayu: "And by 'this room,' maybe..."
Sayo: "The room we're in right now, this staff room."
Diary: "I want to go ahead and quit. I'm at my limits. Fish are swimming around everywhere, no matter where I go I feel like I'm drowning, and no matter what... no one believes me. Ever since Kozuki died... I'm miserable."
Sayo: "The next entry is the last."
Mayu: "Yeah... Let's take a look."
Diary: "I wonder if my past life was as a fish. Recently, I feel no resistance in the water."
Mayu: "I wonder what that means..."
Sayo: "Who knows."
*The girl closes the diary and places it on the table with a dull thud.*
*The last entry in the diary was July 10th. Around the same time of year as now.*
*I wonder... what could have happened to the staff member after that. I somehow know the answer. Surely he met the same end as the one called Kozuki.*
*As I mull over my doubts, I notice a half-full plastic bottle on the table.*
Mayu: "This juice... the expiration date on it is from four years ago."
Sayo: "There was a calendar on the other side, but it's also from four years ago. I thought this room seemed to have been recently in use, but it's the Manten Aquarium from four years ago."
Mayu: "Four years... ago..."
Sayo: "Yes. The prices in the pamphlet left here are also 200 yen cheaper than they are now, so there's no doubt about it."
*Despite my lingering doubts, I believe what she says. Up to this point, I've seen time passing in a weird fashion here many times.*
*Rather than being in awe of the fact that we're in the Manten Aquarium of long ago, I'm more caught up in the realization of how many years have passed.*
Sayo: "Once, four years ago, Manten Aquarium closed down for about half a year. It was right around July. Surely that was when this employee died."
Mayu: "I had... no idea."
Sayo: "It wouldn't have been that big a deal. There are other things for people to do for fun. Anyway, the current urban legends about Manten Aquarium probably came from this period. And they aren't merely rumors."
Mayu: "You're saying there could be others that wound up in similar circumstances to ours?"
Sayo: "Yes."
Mayu: "Moreover, there may be someone who got out of this Manten Aquarium... There has to be a clue somewhere after all...!"
*This diary hints at that.*
*However, this diary holds one more possibility. That three day absence from work -- after that disappearance, the person called Kozuki had an accident and died.*
*Kozuki had returned to the real world, but was unable to live on peacefully.*
*There is a stinging sensation inside of me. The reason we came to Manten Aquarium was because of Himeno's ghost story.*
*Couldn't that have also been someone who returned from Manten Aquarium? The story's content was different, but the result was the same. The full moon and red water could be considered a dramatization of an urban legend.*
Mayu: "Uh, there's something that's bothering me. It's a ghost story I heard from Himeno."
Sayo: "Ghost story?"
*Having caught the girl's attention, I tell her the story as we sit. I forget some of the finer details and mix up the order, but I give her the same general story.*
Sayo: "You heard this story from that girl Himeno? You don't know any more details?"
Mayu: "That's all I heard. Himeno insisted it was a rumor, so she probably doesn't know any further details either."
Sayo: "I see."
Mayu: "By any chance, have you heard that story as well?"
*Some time passes, but the girl still doesn't say anything else on the subject.*
*The girl's expression seems a little different than it has up until now, but I cannot get a deeper read on it.*
*Not allowing the silence to continue much longer, the girl speaks without looking at me.*
Sayo: "Thinking about it won't do any good. Well, I'm going."
Mayu: "W-Wait... uhm..."
Sayo: "Didn't I tell you? I have no desire to run from Manten Aquarium."
*The girl says this over her shoulder, putting her hand on the door to the staff room.*
*According to the map, the side the girl exits from leads to the gate.*
*The girl having turned down my request for company, I can see that she is no longer going to stop to help me, so I don't try to make her.*
*And now I am alone, once again.*
*This room has neither the sound of machines nor that of ventilation fans, so I can hear the beating of my timid little heart.*
*I remember another rumor the staff member wrote in the diary.*
Mayu: "A girl with long hair and winter clothes..."
*The girl was referred to as "a ghost." She wasn't in the story Himeno told.*
*What it may have hinted at was that Mari had already become a ghost. So the Mari that had appeared before me was--*
*One part of me agrees with that thinking, but another part of me rejects it. I am torn between the two, and the inside of my eyes grows hot.*
*But there's no use in thinking about it.*
Mayu: "I have to move forward, if I falter... nothing will change, right...?"
*I am alone again, but it isn't as if I don't know anything. It isn't as if I can't do anything.*
*-- That's right, I will save Himeno.*
*I won't just say that she can probably be saved. Within this aquarium, I have to be determined.*
*That girl had steeled herself. Because she had no doubts, she seemed strong.*
Mayu: "I'll move on."
*Saying this, I reach for the door of the staff room.*
*That was definitely the first time we switched seats in seventh grade.*
Himeno: "Okay! Who~ will I sit next to... uh... um... Nakabe-san, was it?"
Mayu: "... It's Nakanobe."
*When I first entered junior high, I didn't really make an effort to talk to any of my classmates, so I had no friends.*
*Even now I have anxiety about talking to people I don't know, but it pales in comparison to the way I was the year before last.*
*During free time I would read books in a corner of the classroom. During lunch time I would slowly eat my lunch alone. That was how it was every day.*
*I knew how to keep myself occupied.*
Himeno: "Ah, sorr~y. By the way, Nakanobe-san... do you have low blood pressure?"
Mayu: "No... not... really."
Himeno: "Hmm, well, I'm your neighbor now! Let's get to know each other~"
*Himeno was the last one in the row closest to the window, and I was next to her.*
*Himeno ended up with no one else to talk to, so she did all she could to talk to me, or at least that's how I saw it.*
Himeno: "Ah, Mayu, your lunch is ground steak with cheese inside! I just love it~ Let me have a lit~tle!"
Mayu: "Ah... my side dish..."
Himeno: "OK, I'll give you the pork from my yakisoba bread, Mayu!"
Mayu: "No thanks..."
Himeno: "Geez~ Quit being so picky, Mayu!"
Mayu: "Mayu...?"
Himeno: "Yeah, Mayu! You're Mayumi-chan, right? So I thought I should call you Mayu."
Mayu: "You remember my name..."
*This exchange took place two days after our seats changed.*
*I still hadn't opened my heart to Himeno.*
*Undaunted by my curt manner, Himeno would do things like reach for my boxed lunch.*
*Up to that point, I had never told her my first name... but Himeno called me Mayu.*
*After that day, something changed.*
*I would initiate conversation with Himeno, and even after that semester and the next seat change, Himeno would bring her lunch over and eat with me at my desk.*
*Before I realized it, Himeno and I had become friends.*
*The first time Himeno and I ever argued... was in the summer.*
*Should we see a documentary, or a horror movie...? It was a trivial matter, but it was important to us.*
*After that, Himeno and I fought numerous times, but we made up just as many. Every time it rains, the ground hardens.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*I mutter to myself.*
*Even with today's argument, we made up.*
*There was nothing else more important than Tamagawa Himeno, my one and only best friend.*
Mayu: "My best friend... Yes, that's right..."
*No matter what, I will protect her.*
*To save Mari.*
*If I were to guess based on what was written in the diary, then Mari wandered into the closed-down area of Manten Aquarium.*
*If that's the case, I have to bring her home.*
*Even if I flee this Manten Aquarium to save Himeno, I can't just leave Mari behind.*
*There's no way I can prioritize one over the other.*
*Even so, I have to move forward.*
Mayu: "Time to go."
*As I say this, I reach for the door to the staff room.*
*After I leave the staff room and walk for a bit, the entrance gate appears in my line of sight.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*I exit into a different place from the staff passageway I entered with Hiyoshi-san. This aquarium has more back passages than I thought.*
*Thinking it over, I've almost walked the entire aquarium. The only places I haven't visited are places for authorized personnel only... and the closed-off Deep-sea Fish booth.*
*Diagonally across from this area is apparently an authorized personnel only space, the director's office, reachable by going behind the Fish of the World booth. There's a map for authorized personnel to use hanging on the door to the staff room.*
*If I head out from the tunnel tank, I can make something of a shortcut, but... I'm still afraid of the flying cutlassfish.*
*Going through the door will probably cause the state of things to change, but I can't handle things getting as bad as the tank breaking or something like that again.*
*If that's the case, I'll have to go by the Jellyfish booth.*
*In that room, even if the glass is broken, I know I can deal with it. If I walk carefully, I won't fall.*
Mayu: "... I can't just loaf around here."
*Closing up the map and starting to walk, the gate to Manten Aquarium appears in my line of sight.*
Mayu: "I wonder if... I can get out this way."
*The question born from within me has a simple solution.*
*That girl had said this aquarium was isolated in a different dimension, but she had also said that she had not tested that idea.*
*I think I should try tossing something in, so I roll a bucket that had been knocked over. The bucket passes through the gate, and at the very center of the gate it disappears.*
Mayu: "It vanished..."
*From here, I can see the Manten East Building elevator.*
*I prepare myself, then push the door to the automatic turnstile with my hands.*
Mayu: "... What?"
*For some time, my consciousness feels like it's gotten hazy. When my head clears, I can hear the sound of chatter.*
Mayu: "Could I be..."
Lady: "What's wrong?"
Mayu: "Uh... um... this is Manten Aquarium, isn't it...?"
Lady: "Yes, that's right. You can purchase tickets over there."
*Noticing that I look disoriented, a woman speaks to me in a kind voice.*
*Her uniform is the same, but her manner is quite different from Saginuma-san's, it's kind.*
*... Could it be? I have no room for any doubts. Numerous people are entering and exiting through the gate.*
*I can't hear any strange screams either.*
Mayu: "I was able... to get back..."
*But I have no time for relief. There are some problems that remain unsolved.*
Mayu: "I'd like a... return ticket, um, please."
*I hold out my ticket and complete the re-entry procedure. I pass through the gate and find only customers having their fill of the aquarium.*
*The Fish of Tokyo Bay booth is exactly the same as it was before.*
*I am able to pass through the tunnel tank normally. There is no danger of anything like the flying cutlassfish.*
*In the Fish of the World booth, there are tons of brightly colored fish. There are no mummified dead bodies or fragments of destruction.*
*The jellyfish booth is as pretty as ever. However, with no phenomena taking place, this only causes my uneasiness to grow.*
Mayu: "I can't... get back."
*The fact that I came back but now can't return is quite a contradiction.*
*But that's the way it is after all.*
*I want to return to that Manten Aquarium that is full of phenomena. A different dimension, side-by-side worlds, parallel worlds... whatever they really are is irrelevant. I just need to return to Himeno's side!*
Mayu: "What... do I do...?"
*I run through the entire aquarium once. Even so, nothing happens. That Manten Aquarium with the heavy atmosphere, it's gone away somewhere.*
Mari: "Big... sister."
Mayu: "Mari?!"
*I am aware that it's an auditory hallucination.*
*I just wish that she could hear me...*
*After that, I search for Himeno until the building closes.*
*But with a staff member hanging around me and urging me to go home, I am finally forced out.*
*Slumping my shoulders, I tell the police what happened.*
*Five days ago, Himeno's parents filed a missing person's report. Despite my frantic feelings, no news comes about Himeno.*
Mayu: "Ah, a clownfish..."
*On my end, I haven't had any major accidents.*
*In regards to myself, even outside the aquarium I have begun to see fish. I trip over things that aren't there... I start to feel like I'm going to drown.*
*Mom and everyone else are worried about me.*
*For the past three days, it's been getting hard to breathe.*
Mayu: "Ack... urk..."
*It's hard to breathe.*
*Finally, one week later, I'm in a bed.*
*Yes, I've been admitted to a hospital near Manten.*
*While the cause is unknown, my lungs seem to be failing. Now I can't breathe without a machine to assist me.*
*I wonder how long I'll be hospitalized. As much as I love reading, I have grown tired of even that.*
*From the hospital window, I can see the top of Manten East Building.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*Even though I want to speak her name, my voice won't come out.*
*My trachea is just too weak for it.*
*If I can, I want to eat ice cream once more.*
*My beloved cookies and cream, with my beloved Himeno...*
*The test having gone well, it still isn't a guarantee that I can get back to the Manten Aquarium where phenomena are happening.*
*Even if I succeed, I'll be separated from Himeno.*
Mayu: "But I'll definitely get home."
*I mutter to myself. It's strange since it's as if I'm talking to the entrance gate, but it's a declaration of my resolve.*
*As I move forward, I spot the souvenir shop. It makes sense as I'm going back the way I came, but it gives me mixed feelings.*
Mayu: "Huh?"
*It is there that I witness an unexpected sight.*
*The girl from earlier is standing in front of the souvenir shop, apparently lost in thought.*
Sayo: "You. So the coordinates where the doorways lead has to be fixed by someone. Otherwise, there would be no way you would have come across me being attacked by the orca in the machine room."
*I don't really get what the girl, who is reassuring herself, is saying, so I change the subject.*
Mayu: "Um, what's wrong? Did something happen at the souvenir shop?"
Sayo: "Just now, it looked like someone went inside."
Mayu: "Someone?"
Sayo: "I don't know. It was just as they went in. But thanks to whoever it was, the coordinates are fixed on a safe place."
Mayu: "It could have been Mari!"
Sayo: "Your missing little sister, right? I'm sorry, but no. It was definitely an adult."
*I glance over at the entrance to the souvenir shop. Even as I peer through the semi-transparent wall, there are no human shadows.*
*Still, if the person she saw walked through a door and moved to different "coordinates," it wouldn't be strange for there to be no one in there.*
*The girl said it was an adult. Could it have been...?*
Mayu: "Could it be Hiyoshi-san?"
Sayo: "Hiyoshi? Who's that?"
Mayu: "Someone who saved me when I was caught up in the dangers of Manten Aquarium. He went crazy in a staff passageway and... I moved ahead on my own."
*After I explain as simply as possible what happened back then, the girl just nods and says, "Hmm."*
Sayo: "Well, he might have regained his sanity and found an exit, or he might be wandering around, still crazy."
Mayu: "Whichever it is, I think it's worth checking out. Hiyoshi-san seemed like he knew things about Manten Aquarium."
Sayo: "Well, why don't you go? I'll be waiting here."
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*"Aren't you coming with me?" got all the way to my throat before I gulped it back down.*
Mayu: "Either way, if he's not here, we might not be able to go through the door and return to this safe place."
Sayo: "Right. I don't know this Hiyoshi-san's face, so it's better for you to go. Come back in five minutes. If you don't..."
Mayu: "You'll leave me?"
Mayu: "You're mean!"
Sayo: "Don't expect me to take care of you. I'm making the best choice. Anyway, if you die in there, your friend dies too, so make sure to come back."
Mayu: "You don't need to say that, I've already made up my mind to go."
Sayo: "Hmm. Well, I'll wait for you without worry."
Mayu: "... Got it."
Sayo: "Oh, you're so sincere."
Mayu: "I don't think you're mistaken in what you said."
Sayo: "Hmm. Well, I'll wait for you without worry."
Mayu: "Okay."
*Quietly, I open the door to the souvenir shop.*
*The door closes on its own. Not that I had even tried to leave it open.*
*Thinking back on it, no matter what room I entered, the door always shut on its own.*
*The lights aren't on, but I can still see where I'm going by the light shining through the semi-transparent wall.*
*The only thing I can't see is the girl. The scenery visible from this room seems to reflect the time distortion affecting this room as well. The fact that I've become this used to these strange phenomena is a little weird, even to me.*
*The souvenir shop hasn't changed that much from how I remember it.*
*Numerous shelves are arranged so that the purpose of each is visible -- there are some for confectionaries, stationary, and stuffed animals. The keychains and cell phone straps are in a place that stands out.*
*Since I can't find any calendars or magazines, I don't know how many years ago, or from now, this is. Inside the glass balls on the shelves are specimens of shrimp and jellyfish, their prices high.*
Mayu: "Maybe this is the present."
*As one might expect, there is no sign of a shopkeeper.*
*I walk the perimeter of the store in a semi-circle. I study the room carefully, but find nothing.*
*There are so many things that catch my interest, but I can't seem to get interested in picking them up and looking them over closely. That crystal-like necklace, the seal stuffed animal. Right now, I have no interest in orca or jellyfish merchandise.*
*Now I know how a dog being made to wait for a treat feels.*
Mayu: "I should check over here as well."
*I go behind the cash register counter, where I normally wouldn't be allowed to look.*
Mayu: "This is the staff's..."
*It's what you would call a back room. There are boxes full of products, a safe, and even employee uniforms.*
*It looks similar to the staff room where the girl and I saw the diary, only this room is more narrow.*
*Inside the back room, I find a door. Since this is a place for bringing in merchandise, it makes sense that there would be a passageway connecting it to somewhere else.*
*I reach out to see for myself, but it's locked.*
Mayu: "No good, huh...?"
*As I turn back, thinking that this has been fruiltless, I find something.*
*A lanyard with... an ID card. It's been thrown on the ground, so I pick it up and take a closer look.*
Mayu: "This... is Hiyoshi-san's."
Mayu: "This... is Kenji-san's."
*It's the same as the employee ID card that Hiyoshi-san had before we entered Manten Aquarium. There's no picture on it so it's just words, but I remember it.*
*The card is spotless. Unexpectedly, he's showing some propriety.*
*So Hiyoshi-san has been here. Does that mean the one the girl saw was him?*
*Could it be that he headed for somewhere else from this room? But he was able to lock the door.*
*When I entered the souvenir shop, he was already on his way out and I only came after he had locked the door. I had just missed him.*
Mayu: "I don't think there's anything else important here. I'll go back and tell that girl."
*I say that just as I realize it's almost been five minutes. I'm so glad this is a safe place.*
*As if in reaction to the fact that I'm leaving, the sound of a dull thunk echoes.*
Mayu: "Kyah!"
*I let out a small scream. The sound is that of something falling. Even in the gloom, I can make out the outline of what fell.*
Mayu: "What? It's just a frog..."
*Not "what?" There shouldn't be frogs in the souvenir shop. The proof of its identity is the shiny exterior that doesn't belong to an ornament or a toy.*
*It's a large toad. I think it's the appropriately-named giant toad. The reason I know about it is because a TV show was talking about it recently.*
*The story was about how, in Australia, the toad had been imported for pest control, but before they realized it, its population had increased and was disrupting the ecosystem.*
*Characteristic of this species is a gland on the back of its head that looks like a wart, from which it seems they can eject venom. This venom was cited as the cause for alligators and other native species being killed.*
Mayu: "That's right, poison--!"
*What bad luck, I think as I clench my fist. But it's only one.*
*I put a little more space than necessary between us in order to avoid the giant toad. That way, even if there's another lurking nearby, I should be okay.*
*The shelves become a wall for me. Even though frogs have the ability to jump, they shouldn't reach that height.*
*It's really loud for being just one toad, making a "guwa" sound rather than the normal "croak."*
Frog: "Guwa guwa guwa..."
*Although it's poisonous, hearing its cry in a safe place like this makes me think of it as cute.*
*Even though I think that, I can't be careless.*
*A plopping sound resonates once more. The toad cries out, going from a death rattle-like sound to thin breathing, then finally a pause.*
*It soon resumes with an even lower voice than before.*
Mayu: "What?"
*I take a look where the toad was.*
Mayu: "Ah--"
*It is nothing less than ghastly.*
*The toad has been crushed by another frog that has fallen from the ceiling.*
*Its four legs are widespread, its internal organs leaking from its abdomen due to the fall.*
*But that isn't all.*
*The second frog has the white striped pattern characteristic of a certain type, but I don't know its name.*
*When the other frog fell on the giant toad, it stimulated the gland. So naturally, poison leaked out. At the edge of its right foot, its toes have begun melting.*
*Melting...?*
Mayu: "No way."
*The frog's poison isn't acid. It paralyzes or acts as a neurotoxin. If it gets in your eyes, you can lose your eyesight, but basically its for catching prey by poisoning it to death.*
*This poison is different. And more brutal.*
*However, the second frog is even more menacing. As if taking vengeance for the right leg it lost to the poison, it secretes its own venom.*
*The toad's corpse puts up no resistance, its body steadily melting away. I pinch my nose at the mixed stench of the dead body and the acid.*
*Even as my spine grows cold, the room steadily becomes humid, most likely due to the breath of the living creatures in the room.*
*The sight of the frog melting away from the poison is enough to cause bile to rise up in me for a moment.*
*But there's no way I can look away from it. Beyond the fact that I don't know what's going to happen next, I have to pay attention as I head for the exit.*
*I look up at the ceiling, but there's no sign that I'm being followed by any frogs. It's as if they fell from nowhere.*
Mayu: "If one falls on me, it means this place isn't safe anymore!"
*I speed up as I head for the exit. The reason I don't break into a full run is because I know I might trip or fall at a time like this.*
*Plop, plop, plop.*
*A nearby sound fills me with fear and despair.*
*Frogs fall right in front of me. Three... four... it becomes difficult to count them.*
*Just as I panic and attempt to turn back, I hear noises from behind me.*
*Croak, guwa... they begin to speak in unison. The frogs are actually singing in a round, but I hate that they sound so joyful.*
*Hearing the frogs' croaks on either side of me leaves me with nothing but fear.*
*If I step on one of them, the poison will probably dissolve my foot. That being said, there isn't enough room to leap over them, and if I do, one of them may jump up and touch me.*
*As if preparing to attack me while I'm off guard, their numbers increase even further. Crowded into such a small space, they are melting each others' bodies with their poison.*
*I avert my eyes from the damaged frogs. As the danger increases, it's not a tragedy I can continue to watch.*
*As the amount of bodily fluids on the floor increases, the frogs who step in the puddles of poison have their toes melted off. Then they secrete their own fluids, and the cycle repeats.*
*On top of that, there are those whose bodies are melted by their own poison as well.*
*Somehow suppressing whatever is welling up inside me, I finalize my plan.*
Mayu: "Over here!"
*I pull some merchandise off the shelf with my hand and make some space.*
*The original two are still left, but it's better than being surrounded. I step out onto the floor and leave the shelf.*
*To keep them from reproducing anymore, quickly--!*
Mayu: "Nooo!"
*As if they had been lying in wait, numerous frogs fall from every which way.*
*They are small creatures with shrill voices. Red and yellow, green and striped and varied. The intense amount of color saturation is making me feel sick.*
*I know this variety of frog well. Among poisonous frogs, it is the most infamous type.*
*... A poison dart frog.*
*Even under normal circumstances, it carries deadly poison stronger than that used in blow darts. If the strength of that poison has been taken to intense levels like the other creatures here, it could probably instantaneously melt even my bones!*
Mayu: "What... should I...?"
*There is still a distance between the door and I. Three steps.*
*No matter what, I can't take those three steps. There is nowhere for my feet to land.*
*The floor is covered in the squishy bodies of the frogs and their acidic, poisonous fluids, as well as the corpses of those frogs that had melted themselves.*
*Not knowing where they're coming from, there's nothing I can do.*
*Even the sweat falling from my forehead seems like venom to me, as if some kind of illusion.*
Mayu: "Sh......"
*I don't want to see it as if nothing else can be done. But... but...*
Sayo: "You're slow."
*A light suddenly shines through the darkness. The soft breeze blowing in through the crack returns me to my senses.*
Sayo: "Cover your eyes."
*Immediately after saying this, the girl pulls the pin from the fire extinguisher she's holding. The entire inside of the souvenir shop is filled with the smoke from the fire extinguisher.*
Mayu: "A fire extinguisher...! But now we can't jump over the frogs!"
Sayo: "I can see what's going on. This isn't all."
*Almost in sync with the girl's words, the sprinklers activate. A large amount of water immediately sprays us, an amount greater than the strongest rain striking my body.*
Mayu: "Gah..."
*When I open my eyes, the girl reaches out her hand with a firm expression.*
Sayo: "While the water's running, even if you get hit with a little venom, it will wash right off. Don't be afraid, just run for it. If you fall, you'll die."
Mayu: "The floor is soaked, I might slip."
Mayu: "I can't! It's no laughing matter if I fall so... I'll go that way!"
Sayo: "Do you doubt me?"
Mayu: "Huh?"
Sayo: "You get that you don't have time for that, right?"
*The girl speaks in a cold voice and closes the door. As if recognizing my suddenly slowed heartbeat, the rain stops. The sprinklers have been turned off.*
Mayu: "Wha...?"
*No words will come out. Even as I come to understand my circumstances, I cannot say anything.*
*Croak, croak, croak.*
*Guwa, guwa, guwa.*
*Kiii, kiii, kiii.*
*The frogs' canon continues. As they hop freely about, they spray liquid all over.*
Mayu: "Ahh!"
*Frightened, I kick the bright red frog at my feet into the air. He is crushed with a SPLAT, his venom wetting the tips of my toes.*
Mayu: "Hot!"
*I scream this word reflexively. Actually, "ouch" would probably fit better than "hot."*
*Shaking, I look down at my feet. My right big toenail is slowly melting away.*
Mayu: "Ahh!"
*Spray from raising my foot so high scatters about, some of it getting in my left eye. My vision fades in sync with the intense pain.*
*Croak, croak, croak.*
*Guwa, guwa, guwa.*
*Kiii, kiii, kiii.*
*The stench of acid grows stronger once more.*
Mayu: "I have to get to the door...!"
*Enduring the pain, I take a step. I crush several frogs, leaving the sensation of slippery meat. The fluids that scatter about strike me around the knees.*
*As I take my second step, I lose my balance."
*I can't balance on the leg that has melted.*
Mayu: "But it doesn't hurt, why...?"
*I quickly get my answer. My nerves have already been paralyzed.*
*As long as I can't feel the pain. At least not the intense pain of my flesh melting.*
Mayu: "Haha, hahaha."
*I laugh, naturally. Even though my body has begun to deteriorate, I feel no agonizing pain.*
Mayu: "Hahaha, hahahaha..."
*Ahh, how strange. In my fading consciousness, I keep thinking that I should at least try to laugh.*
Mayu: "Got it!"
*I have no time for doubt. I run for it through the rain from the sprinklers.*
Mayu: "Gah!"
*I try to run without looking at my feet. The sensation of squishing the slippery meat continues. The liquid that gets scattered about with a squirting sound is washed off in the rain.*
*Just three steps, that's just a moment.*
Mayu: "Ahh."
Sayo: "Idiot."
*With one last step, I stumble. The girl grabs me with her extended hand and pulls me out.*
*I collapse, but it's outside of the souvenir shop. The door is already closed. There are no frogs anywhere.*
Mayu: "Huff, hahh. Thank you. But it's already been more than five minutes, so why?"
*As I catch my breath, I express my thanks to the girl.*
Sayo: "I just remembered that I owe you a debt."
Mayu: "... You are kind. I might have misjudged you a bit."
Sayo: "You didn't misjudge me. If you had hesitated any further, I would have closed the door."
Mayu: "But you believed in me, right? That I could do it."
Sayo: "You're going to drive me nuts, so stop it with that stuff."
*Expressionlessly, the girl says this in a flat tone. Holding on to this gentle feeling, I prepare to move on.*
*Speaking honestly, I want to rest here like this. My clothes are soaked, and I've used up a lot of my strength.*
*However, it feels like if I were to really relax, I might never get up again. If I don't keep my momentum, I can't save Himeno.*
*I'm able to gain some relief from resting against the wall. The girl who rescued me seems to be resting just like I am.*
Mayu: "Huh, this GachaGacha is..."
*As we are resting against the wall, I realize that the type of GachaGacha situated in front of the souvenir shop have changed.*
Sayo: "How ironic. Five years ago."
Mayu: "What do you mean?"
Sayo: "You said it yourself. That your sister went missing five years ago."
Mayu: "You remembered..."
Sayo: "Of course."
*Inside the GachaGacha machines, there are many keychains. They all have charms with various creatures attached to the droplets.*
*The link between Mari and I, and now the item of my memories that links Himeno and I as well.*
Mayu: "My little sister, her name is Mari."
Sayo: "I didn't ask."
Mayu: "You see, five years ago, we had an argument over keychains. Mari wanted my jellyfish one, but I wouldn't trade with her. She disappeared, sulking."
Sayo: "Still didn't ask."
Mayu: "I have this one."
*I take the keychain that I had received from Himeno out of my pocket. It's the one with the starfish attached to the droplet.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*As I hold it out to the girl, I realize there's something attached to the back.*
*A picture printed on a sticker.*
*It's a pretty old sticker at that. A small girl, and a man embracing her. The two of them are smiling and seem to be having a lot of fun. The man, who is wearing a suit and seems quite serious, is probably her father.*
*The words "manten-aquarium" run along the picture's border. The souvenir shop had a sticker photo booth, so I'm sure they took this picture here.*
*More than anything else, I'm disturbed by the fact that the girl in the picture looks like Mari.*
*It's not just her face, but her long hair, and that smile too. As her big sister, this girl seems like she could be Mari's twin.*
Sayo: "Hey, that's..."
*Pointing to the keychain, the girl speaks. My consciousness is yanked back from my memories.*
Mayu: "What is it?"
*I ask the girl as she shows interest in the keychain, but I receive no response.*
*Her averted eyes tell me, "Nothing."*
Sayo: "Your little sister, she went missing here, right?"
Mayu: "Huh...? Y-Yeah."
*The girl suddenly changes the subject back, leaving me a bit flustered. She doesn't press any further with questions.*
Sayo: "It's the same with my dad."
Mayu: "Huh...?"
Sayo: "But in my case, I know he's dead rather than missing, so it's different. I know he was murdered."
Mayu: "Your father was murdered...?"
*I can't grasp the detached way the girl is speaking.*
*She's talking about her own family member, but the girl is completely apathetic. She has the calm of someone reading a newspaper article aloud.*
Sayo: "Well, I'm going now."
Mayu: "Ah, b-but..."
*I haven't heard all of the girl's story yet, and if things get like they were before, it will be better if there are two of us.*
*The words won't come out right, but the state of panic I'm in should be enough to express what I want to say.*
*However, the girl stands her ground to the end.*
Sayo: "When I'm with you, you hold me back."
Mayu: "Hold you back? What do you mean?"
Sayo: "If dying is what it takes for me to learn the truth, then that's fine. You could never resolve yourself to that."
Mayu: "Fine... with dying? You can't let yourself die!"
Sayo: "Shut up."
Mayu: "S-Shut up... you're awful."
Sayo: "You have that friend Himeno right? If you die, what happens to her?"
*I have no idea whether or not this is her way of trying to encourage me.*
*However, I understand what she's trying to say. She's trying to say that each of us is on our own.*
Mayu: "L-Let's become friends. That way you can't say so easily that you're fine with dying, right?"
Sayo: "That part of you is exactly what I meant when I said you hold me back."
*Without answering my question, the girl pushes me away.*
*Swiftly turning her back on me, the girl walks on ahead.*
Mayu: "What... should I have done?"
*I stay where I am until the girl's back becomes so small I can no longer see it.*
*I pass in front of the souvenir shop, then end up standing in front of the door to the jellyfish booth.*
*I wonder if the girl is already gone. If she ended up in a safe place, she's probably already passed through it.*
*If nothing happens, it takes less than a minute to pass through the jellyfish booth.*
*If by any chance she is still there, we'll surely just meet in passing. The girl is determined to take a different path than I.*
*... I wonder, what will happen to me?*
*I may return to a point before the tank glass ruptured, or I may end up in the jellyfish booth some time after the tank glass ruptured.*
Mayu: "I'll never know if I don't try going in."
*I become cautious. Slowly, I open the door.*
Mayu: "... Huh?"
*At that moment, the air becomes shaky.*
Mayu: "What... is this? It's different..."
*The field of view before me becomes red, and the air becomes so cold it could freeze.*
*This is no longer a matter of dimensions, of wondering what time period this is.*
*Something is different. This jellyfish booth is distinctly in a different place from the aquarium.*
Mayu: "All the water in the tanks, it's red---"
*In the jellyfish booth I know, the transparent jellyfish are illuminated by various colors of lighting. The reds and purples are usually a pretty sight, but this place is different.*
*All of the pillar-shaped tanks are bright red.*
*It's a repulsive sight, as if all of the water has been replaced with blood.*
Mayu: "I have to go..."
*A bit confused, I take a step forward.*
*Despite the fact that the floor at my feet should be solid, I stagger. My sense of equilibrium is off.*
*I proceed one, then two steps. At that point, I start to feel like I might fall. Even so, I head further inside.*
*If I can pass through another door, I should be able to escape this bizarre phenomena. No matter what, I have to hurry!*
Mayu: "Ah...!"
*Suddenly I stumble and place my hand on a pillar tank. The temparature of the tank is slightly warmer than that of the cold air.*
Mayu: "Huh..."
*Just as I am about to move forward using the tank to support myself, I peer into the red water.*
*Where there should be jellyfish floating about, there is something completely different.*
Mayu: "N-Nooooo!"
*I'm unable to stop the scream that bursts from my mouth. An unbelievable sight stops me in my tracks.*
Mayu: "A face, a face... Kyaaaaaaah!"
*Human faces float about.*
*Whatever is below the neck appears as if it's been assimilated into the blood, blending in with the contents of the tank.*
*It isn't just one of them. Just as with the previous jellyfish booth, there are more and more and more...*
*I wobble and break away from the tank, landing on my backside.*
*I don't know whose face it is. It's not as if it would be someone I know.*
*But no matter which face it is, they all seem to be staring at me, here in the heart of the jellyfish booth.*
*Taking a step back, I see that all of the tanks have human faces floating inside them. They have some differences, such as some faces being blank, some have their eyes bulging open, and some have bloodshot eyes, but they are all looking at me.*
Mayu: "What... is this...?"
*That face over there's nose is twitching. This face's lips are trembling and, way over there, that one has a crease in its brow.*
*Ba-da-dum, my heart beats faster.*
*Ba-bum, ba-bum, ba-bum... In rhythm with my heartbeat, I experience a hallucination that the faces in the tanks are drawing closer.*
Mayu: "No... this is..."
Lady: "Heheheh."
Mayu: "Wha...?"
Lady: "Haha, haha, haha, haha!!"
Lady: "Ehehehee..."
Mayu: "No..."
*It isn't a hallucination.*
*I am certain that all of these faces are getting closer to me. The fact that they are getting larger serves as proof.*
*The boundary between the tanks and the air is gradually being removed. This room is becoming all water.*
*The faces are swimming in that water, drawing closer to me.*
*As the faces draw closer, the sound within the water grows louder. Rather than someone's laughing voice, it is the sound of someone sobbing that grows louder instead.*
*Either way is the same to me, wearing away at my mental state.*
Lady: "Haha, hahaha... hahahaha, hahahahaha!!"
Mayu: "Stop iiiiiit!"
Lady: "Ehehehehehehee..."
*A voice unfamiliar to me is sneering at me.*
*Little by little, these unfamiliar faces are closing in on me.*
*Before I realize it, my feet can no longer confirm whether the floor is there or not.*
Mayu: "I have... to go...!"
*So I put all my strength into my knees.*
*I've already learned that time is of the essence. I have to take some sort of action. Should I use force, should I steady myself to move easier? If distance is the issue, then the way I came is just a bit closer.*
*At any rate, I should pull back and get into a good position.*
*I should open and close the door numerous times in order to create a safer environment.*
*My feet unsteady, I swiftly dodge and retrace my steps.*
*One step at a time, feeling as if I'm walking on bubbles, I inch with certainty closer to the door.*
Mayu: "All right...!"
*Finally, I succeed in reaching for the door.*
Mayu: "Just like this!!"
*I throw open the door right away.*
Mayu: "... Huh?"
*At that moment, I am swallowed up in darkness.*
*Whether it's darkness, or water... at any rate, there is definitely no light.*
*Plop. Something touches my back with a wet sensation.*
*Before long, it spreads throughout my body.*
Mayu: "Wha...?"
*Without thinking, I spin around. Before me are a large number of fish.*
Himeno: "They say that fish with human faces swim about."
*I'm reminded of Himeno's words.*
*That's right, human-faced fish.*
*Just like the faces I saw at the jellyfish booth, these are attached to the fish approaching me.*
Mayu: "I'm... an idiot..."
*If I go through the door, the time axis will change.*
*If I do that, the way I came also changes. I have no idea whether or not it will be safe.*
*Regardless, to avoid the immediate terror, I vigorously leap forward.*
*The fish are biting off my clothes.*
Mayu: "They're... fish..."
*Yes, fish.*
*No matter how much their white teeth are like humans', and no matter how much their teeth are arranged like humans, they're all fish.*
*So, it'll be fine...*
*Before I realize it, the clothes enveloping me are disappearing. Blood leaks from the places I have been bitten by those large teeth.*
*Look, red water.*
*The blood flowing from my body mixes with the red ocean.*
*And then they become one.*
*Me and this bright red world--*
*My resolution can no longer be shaken.*
*Even if I go back, I don't know if it will be safe.*
*In that case, I'll have to choose the way with even a small chance.*
*Unable to tell whether I am floating or standing, I frantically move my arms and legs.*
*The faces are closing in, but even as I look up, I'm not afraid!*
*Persuading myself, I move forward a bit. Even if there's no feeling left in my legs, I get the feeling that I actually am drawing closer to the door.*
Mayu: "Just a little more..."
*Suddenly, a loud sound interrupts my movements. That sound is familiar to me. It's a sound I've heard in this jellyfish booth before.*
*The sound of the glass breaking resonates from behind me.*
Mayu: "Still... I won't give up!"
*I refuse to turn around.*
*If I were to see something bad right now, this time my heart may really break. In that case, it may be better for me not to know.*
Lady: "Hihi, hihihi......! Hihihihihi, hihihihihihihihi!!!"
*On the heels of that thought, various voices, all laughing and crying, begin to resonate behind me.*
*They are probably the voices of those faces floating in the blood.*
*The voices steadily draw near, finally closing in on me.*
Mayu: "Fish with... human faces!"
*... They say that fish with human faces swim about.*
*It reminds me of an urban legend Himeno talked about. However, I don't have the time to be in shock over a rumor being true.*
Mayu: "I... won't falter!"
*Human faces and shards of glass are passing by the edge of my field of vision. Occasionally they touch my cheek or forehead, but I move forward.*
*I drift as I move along, grabbing the door with a clack. I encourage myself out loud so I don't lose focus. I have no idea what lies ahead.*
Mayu: "Guh..."
*Almost at the same time as I pull on the door, I begin to feel heavy pressure against my back.*
Mayu: "It's heavy... Why?"
*I can guess what has happened.*
*Surely the fish are stopping me from moving on. This feeling of having my clothes pulled on is proof of that.*
*I never thought fish could be this strong.*
Mayu: "I'm going, I'm going, so......!"
*I bite down on my lip and try to move forward.*
*The harder I try to move forward, the stronger the fishes' resistance becomes.*
Mayu: "... Himeno is suffering even more...!"
*I shut my eyes and grind my back molars, then hurl myself at the door.*
*The door flies open and I fall to the ground.*
*Even so, I muster up my strength and shut the door. There is no longer any pressure against my back.*
Mayu: "Huh?"
*Beyond the door is not the Fish of the World booth. It's dark and narrow, similar to the staff passageway I passed through before. Turning about and looking around, I realize that this is a passageway after all.*
*So the jellyfish booth had at least three doors. The one I initially came in through, the one that leads to the entrance, and this door that connects to the Staff Only passage.*
*A closer inspection shows me that the wall is made of one-way glass. It's been created using something stronger than acrylic fiber.*
*There's water in the space between, serving as a border between the booth and the other side. Like a magic mirror, it's been made so that customers cannot see the staff members moving about.*
*From here, I can see the Fish of the World booth. Somehow, this place feels safer when compared to the Jellyfish booth.*
*It's very different from the first time I visited, with the offensive smell being completely gone. Inside, I can see familiar silhouettes.*
Mayu: "That's..."
*Two females.*
*One is an adult, the other is a girl.*
*The visibility is a bit dicey making it hard to tell, but that looks like the girl from earlier and...*
Mayu: "Saginuma Rieko-san..."
*The female aquarium employee. The heartless one, who said Himeno couldn't be saved.*
*The two of them are in front of the tank I am looking at. The fact that they haven't realized I'm there must mean this really is something of a one-way mirror. Both of them are facing each other, their expressions grim.*
*The sound is cut off by all of the layers, but I can tell they are arguing over something.*
Mayu: "Ah!"
*At that moment, the girl raises her right hand to Saginuma-san and follows through with all of her might. She slaps her with such vigor that it surely resonates with a loud, dry smacking sound.*
*Saginuma-san rubs her left cheek and grins widely.*
*Seeing Saginuma-san grin with such composure after being struck gives me chills. The girl only deepens the crease in her brow.*
*I attempt to leave my safe place, but I cannot find a break in the wall.*
*There should be a door somewhere... Even as I think this, I cannot find a doorknob in the darkness.*
*As I keep my eyes on the both of them, I decide to search the area connected to the Fish of the World booth.*
*No, I don't want to get pulled into this.*
*I reconsider my decision. That girl said it strongly herself, that she wanted to be alone. Surely she would hate it if I intervened.*
*If things escalate it's a different story, but for now I decide to watch over their actions.*
*Their quarrel continues, the girl using hand gestures to get Saginuma-san's attention.*
*In contrast, she merely looks down on the girl. From my perspective as an outsider, it doesn't look like she's even paying attention.*
Mayu: "What could have happened...?"
*What in the world could have happened to bring them to such a climax?*
*Though I have many questions, it's unlikely that I'll get any answers.*
Sayo: "----!"
*A sad expression appears on the girl's face for a moment, then she looks away from Saginuma-san.*
*At almost the same time, the composure leaves Saginuma-san's face. She creases her brow and opens her eyes wide, her nose twitching.*
*Something has touched a nerve, getting her attention. Even without sound, that much is clear to me.*
Mayu: "Huh...?! W-Wait...!"
*Without thinking about it, I pound on the wall. However, this doesn't cause so much as a ripple, leaving me cut off from the two of them."
*This time, Saginuma-san hits the girl. Then she immediately grabs the nape of the girl's neck and throws her into the wall of the tank. There is no sign of the impact.*
Mayu: "N-No...!"
*Having hit the back of her head on the tank, the girl falters and Saginuma-san flips her body over, grabbing the back of her collar. The girl's face is turned in my direction, and I realize that she has fainted.*
Mayu: "She's... unconscious...!"
*A horrible premonition runs through me.*
*With the girl's chest pressed against the wall of the tank, Saginuma-san grabs the back of her head and smashes it against the tank wall over and over again.*
*I can't see Saginuma's face, as it is bathed in light from behind and has become a silhouette. The only thing I can see is her lips, which dangle in the shape of an anchor.*
Mayu: "No, at this rate she'll die!"
*Without thinking, I scream.*
Mayu: "I have to help her!"
*I can't stay quiet any longer.*
*At any rate, I pound on the wall before me."
Mayu: "Come on, come on, please, let it get through...!"
*However, no matter how many times I strike it, Saginuma-san does not look this way, as if the sound isn't going through.*
*Since the sound from the other side isn't coming through, then the sound from my side shouldn't go through either.*
Mayu: "It figures, I have no choice but to go out there!"
*I grope about, trying find a crack in either side of the wall.*
*From top to bottom, right to left... I go along the outer side of the tank and look to see if there's a way to get in from below.*
*A handle of some sort would be great, or anything that could serve as a starting point.*
Mayu: "There's nothing... nothing..."
*I still can't find any way whatsoever to get to the other side. Panicking over what I should do, I frantically search for the door.*
Hiyoshi: "Above you, Mayu-chan!"
Mayu: "Huh...? That voice...!"
*What breaks through my bewilderment is something reminiscent of a man's voice. Looking up, I breathe a sigh of relief at the words piercing through, as I am lifted up in sync with the voice bellowing, "Let's go!"*
*This sensation is different from floating, more like my body is being pushed up. I hear the voice from below say "Oof." just as I'm about to hit the ceiling, and I place my hands against it.*
Hiyoshi: "There should be a handle above you! If you pull that, a ladder will come out!"
Mayu: "Huff... right."
*I look down to give my reply and finally confirm the voice's owner.*
*He's got a firm grip on the left and right side of my hips, raising me several meters in the air. It gives me the stable feeling of a father sitting a child on his shoulders, not letting me slip.*
Mayu: "Kenji-san...!"
Hiyoshi: "It's fine, just open the door!"
Mayu: "All right...!"
Hiyoshi: "From there you can pass through the passageway above and go to the other side of the tank."
Mayu: "I got it."
Hiyoshi: "If you don't hurry... that girl will die!"
Mayu: "Right!"
*I slide my hands across the ceiling, searching for any bumps or grooves.*
*I find something sooner than I expected and give a good pull on that bump. With a clatter the ladder on the other side of the door swings down, and I reach for it before it drops all the way.*
Mayu: "... I've got this...!"
Hiyoshi: "All right! I'll be there in a bit!"
*Inside the ceiling, there's a passageway I have to crouch down to walk through. To the left and right of me are machines with meters attached, surely they regulate the booth below.*
*After ten meters, I have come far enough to have passed the barrier between myself and the two girls. Still bent over, I quickly move on and push open the door at my feet.*
*During that moment, the girl continues to have her forehead bashed against the tank right in front of my eyes.*
*A gash is torn wide across her forehead, her blood clinging to the tank with each hit. The blood sticking to the tank makes a red line as if someone tried to wipe a brush across it.*
*That one line of blood quickly becomes two, then the two merge into one large flow of blood.*
Mayu: "No...! No...!"
*Her eyes are white, and wide open, the blood from the girl's forehead making them seem bright red.*
*From those eyes, I can sense no signs of life.*
*Saginuma Rieko's lips are, of course, smiling.*
Mayu: "Stop this!"
*Unable to stand anymore, I scream.*
*However, it is pointless, merely echoing back through the passageway I'm in.*
Mayu: "Stop... this already..."
*No movement is left in the girl, save for the shaking of her eyeballs. Not just blood from her forehead, but as her entire body goes limp, fluids from all over leak out.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*As the life has fades from the girl, I see a small movement from her lips. I mimic that movement with my own mouth.*
Mayu: "The... truth... of... the... De... ep... Se... a..."
*As these words are unvoiced, I have no idea whether or not that's what she really said. However, to me, that's what it seemed like the girl was saying.*
*Saginuma Rieko tosses the girl. She flops over as she hits the floor, having been reduced to a mere object.*
Mayu: "Ah..."
*My mind goes blank, and I sit down.*
*I couldn't save her.*
*She was right in front of me, at a distance short enough that I could have touched her by reaching out my hand, but I couldn't save her, or do anything to help.*
*Just in case, Saginuma Rieko kicks the girl over. Her heel strikes the hard floor, and that which was the girl's head flops over.*
*Just like that, she leaves the Fish of the World booth behind.*
*At that moment, I see bright red.*
*Without a moment's pause, the girl's corpse loses all traces of life. It decomposes, and goes beyond that to completely dried out.*
*Some new phenomenon is starting, but I can't stand up as I am right now...*
Mayu: "Ah... ah..."
*I'm powerless.*
*I can't do anything.*
*Something hot wells up behind my eyes.*
Mayu: "No... o..."
*I don't want this. I don't... I don't want Himeno to die like this.*
Mayu: "B-But..."
*The image of that girl's expression as she was on the verge of death is scorched into my mind.*
*So hollow, yet so desperate... Her face burning up with rage -- that face keeps appearing within my bright red vision.*
*The Deep-sea -- the truth.*
*Just before the flame of life was extinguished, I think those were the words the girl left me.*
Mayu: "Then... what...?"
*I don't want to just sit here brooding like this.*
Mari: "Big sister..."
Mayu: "Huh, that voice just now..."
*Even though it feels easier just to break down and cry, hearing the voice of my little sister from somewhere brings me back.*
*That was... Mari's voice.*
Mari: "Big sister, come this way..."
Mayu: "Mari! Where are you?!"
Mari: "This way, over here!"
Mayu: "Where... Where?"
Mari: "I said this way, over here!"
Mayu: "It is Mari. Mari's voice... it really is, but from where?"
*Before I realize it, I'm on my feet.*
*Mari's voice echoes about the room like an announcement from a loudspeaker, but I can't find the source.*
*I don't want to lose sight of this one strand of hope that has filled the hole left by my feelings of anxiety.*
Mayu: "-- Let's go."
*I cannot forget that girl's death.*
*Stricken with grief and near the breaking point, I hold myself together.*
*... Thanks to Mari.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*Recklessly coming out of the passageway, I end up right where the tunnel tank lets out.*
Mari: "Big sister!"
Mayu: "Ah...!"
Mari: "Big sister, this way!"
Mayu: "Ma-Mari... Is that you...?"
*I slowly turn in the direction the voice is coming from.*
*Exiting the tunnel tank, I can see a wide wall before me. In front of it stands a girl wearing winter clothes.*
*It's the same place I saw Mari the first time.*
Mari: "Geez, big sister. Did you get lost?"
Mayu: "Mari, I'm glad. Mari, are you... really here...?"
Mari: "Really here? I'm right here!"
*The same color hair as mine, the same color eyes. We were often mistaken for twins.*
*With the silhouette of a doll, she is wearing that warm-looking overcoat.*
*... Mari is right here, looking just as she did that day.*
Mayu: "Mari, why are you here?"
Mari: "Did you forget? Geez, you meanie! We came together to the aquarium!"
Mayu: "There's no way I could forget, none at all... It's just, why... why did you disappear?"
Mari: "You're the one who got lost! I'm here. I've been here all along!"
Mayu: "Mari!"
*Without hesitation, I embrace Mari. She must have caught a chill, as her body is cold to the touch.*
Mari: "Big sister, what's wrong? This is embarrassing!"
Mayu: "It's fine, Mari... We're finally together again..."
Mari: "Geez, big sister. You're such a crybaby!"
*That's right, I'm crying. After Mari points it out, I realize my cheeks are covered with tears.*
*Just as I'm about to stop, I cry again.*
Mari: "Okay, let's go big sister!"
*Mari says this in a cheerful voice as she pats my head.*
*It's a strange feeling, having my little sister rub my head, but not a bad one. I feel my head being enveloped in a soft feeling as if I am floating.*
Mayu: "Go...? Where?"
Mari: "We'll go somewhere higher up. Together, you and I!"
Mayu: "Somewhere... higher up?"
Mari: "Higher, softer. That place is... eternity!"
Mayu: "E... tern... ity?"
*Grinning ear to ear, Mari points her right index finger toward the ceiling.*
*The feeling that I've begun floating upwards grows even stronger.*
Mari: "Yes, there's nothing, yet everything there. It's really fun, very soft, and a place we can always be. A world of happiness!"
Mayu: "Happiness..."
*I don't really understand what Mari is trying to say.*
*But if it's happiness... that might be okay.*
*The fact that I'm able to reunite with Mari like this makes me extremely happy.*
*My thoughts melt together in happiness at being with Mari. I smell something sweet, with a hint of lemon. If that's what the world will be like, we'll surely be happy.*
Mari: "Let's go together, big sister!"
Mayu: "... Mari."
Mari: "Don't get lost anymore!"
Mayu: "... Okay."
*Mari reaches her hand out for me.*
*I sit upright and reach for Mari's outstretched hand.*
*I hear the sound of something falling down with a clatter.*
*I squeeze Mari's hand tight. For a moment, my vision becomes ultramarine.*
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*Without knowing why, I can no longer breathe.*
*Like the ones I've experienced numerous times, it's one of the phenomena that happen at this aquarium!*
*This isn't a situation that I can analyze calmly.*
*Somehow, somehow I have to get out of here!*
*As I think this, I flail my arms and legs about.*
*My body is no longer standing on the floor of the aquarium. I'm in the ocean or a lake, some kind of body of water.*
Mayu: "Mari... Where is Mari?"
*Turning around and around, I search for her.*
Mayu: "Ow..."
*I soon find Mari. Her eyes are wide open, and she floats about lifelessly.*
Mayu: "Huh..."
*In the depths of my heart, I feel as if I'm falling into despair.*
*Just like the girl who was murdered, Mari is dead.*
*... She's dead.*
Mayu: "Nooooooooo!"
*When I scream, I spew out the last of my oxygen, causing it to bubble in the water.*
Mayu: "Urk... Ma... ri..."
*At the base of Mari's throat, there are red scars.*
*Mari didn't just drown to death, Mari was... Mari was--!*
*Even as I realize this, I'm losing consciousness.*
*... Bright red.*
*This place is a world of bright red.*
*Within this bright red world, I wait for someone to come visit me.*
Mari: "You've come."
Mayu: "Yes, that's right."
*Someone has gotten lost.*
*If the person is still living, we have to retrieve their soul.*
*In that way, we maintain this deep red world...*
*Drawn to the sound, I look down at my feet.*
*The keychain with the droplet and the starfish. That precious memory for Mari and I.*
*That unstable bridge connecting Himeno and I.*
*This keychain has a sticker with a picture printed on it attached to it's back so it probably isn't Mari's, but... it has the same shape.*
Mayu: "Sorry, Mari... I've been... regretting not trading you the jellyfish keychain this whole time. I got hung up on something so petty... so..."
Mari: "No, that whole thing, it doesn't matter!"
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*At Mari's words, I withdraw the hand I hold out to her.*
*Just now, Mari said "It doesn't matter," not "Don't worry about it," or "It's not important."*
*She declared it nonchalantly, with that smile she always wears.*
*My heart resounds with a loud thump.*
*At that moment, Himeno's words float through my mind.*
Himeno: "For argument's sake, even if this Mari girl is here, she would have grown in five years, and her clothes wouldn't be the same. It's summer so she wouldn't be wearing a coat... Moreover..."
*All of a sudden, that floating sensation disappears, and instead I feel the impact of falling to the ground.*
*In reality, I had been sitting, so my fall was more like tipping over...*
Mayu: "... This isn't right."
*Himeno's words vividly rush through my mind.*
*Surely, Himeno has saved me, because now I can clearly see the form of that thing before me.*
Mayu: "You're... not Mari. No matter what, Mari was never the type of girl to just say 'It doesn't matter'!"
*Not to mention that the keychain had been so important to us. I can't see her declaring that something like that doesn't matter.*
Mari: "What are you saying, big sister? I'm Mari. I'm... Mari."
Mayu: "When Mari refers to herself as 'I,' she always drags it out."
*As I get up, I announce this to that thing before me.*
Mari: "I'm, Ma......"
*The girl's body begins to become blurry, like a staticky image. I realize I can no longer look directly at her.*
Mayu: "I knew it... You're not Mari after all!"
*Muddy is probably a fitting term here.*
*The thing that had been Mari up to a short time ago begins to melt, transfiguring itself from one clump into countless fish.*
*The fish begin swimming in the air without water. This pattern of behavior is bad. If I get caught up in this bizarre phenomenon, then I am sure to drown in this place with nothing. *
Mayu: "I have to get away..."
*As I mumble this, I search for a door.*
*If I can go through a door, I should be able to escape the phenomenon in front of me.*
Mayu: "Mari..."
*Saying I wouldn't regret leaving them is a lie.*
*But that thing, it isn't Mari.*
*It's just an aggregation of fish.*
*I can't be deceived by them and lose sight of my precious goal.*
*I was... so naive.*
*Fine, even so, it's fine. It happened somehow. If I have time to get down about it, then I just need to not let it happen next time.*
Mayu: "I will save her, no matter what."
*Finding a door as I run away, I throw it open with great force.*
*I think that was something that happened awhile ago.*
*About a year ago, it was something that happened in the summer. During a break, when I was looking over my notes for a quiz or something.*
Lady: "Hey you... keep this in mind!"
Mayu: "Wha--"
*A girl from my class suddenly came to my seat, kicking my desk as she yelled at me.*
*She was unexpectedly strong, the notes and stationery on my desk scattering about.*
*She was like a leader in our class, a part of the largest group. Having almost never spoken to her, I had no memory of ever making her mad.*
*Unable to get mad, I was merely bewildered. Then Himeno showed up, and with her face downcast, picked up my stationery.*
Himeno: "Sorry, it's my fault."
Mayu: "Your fault...?"
Himeno: "A girl from that group invited me to Round One this Sunday, but I turned her down."
*That was the day Himeno and I had made plans to go to the movies. Indeed, I was able to more or less understand what had happened.*
Himeno: "When she asked me why, I told her I already had plans with you. Ah, of course I hesitated to tell her that, you know? But she was persistent."
Mayu: "But... you could have gone."
*That girl's group was big. If there was an event or something, basically the whole class went along with them, but it wasn't strictly a good thing.*
*Their group pride was as great as their group mentality. The girl that had come over a few minutes earlier was particularly striking.*
Himeno: "Ahaha, like I'd go. Mayu, you should know you take priority."
Mayu: "But... if you turn them down, they won't invite you anymore, will they?"
*If their pride was really that high, then that's how it would be. I had heard that, except for that girl as the leader and two others, people were regularly being brought in and thrown out of the group.*
*It wasn't something I cared to hear about, but the bad things girls who had been kicked out said regularly found their way into my ears.*
Himeno: "Yeah. I won't be invited anymore, for sure."
Mayu: "Wha~~! But that means..."
*I saw it as a disadvantage for Himeno, who loved going to events.*
*Like she could have had an advantage getting elected to executive committees for things like the culture festival or the sports festival, or getting votes for a program or something.*
*It may sound like a discussion of politics, but ever since reaching junior high, things have had this kind of mysterious flow to them.*
Himeno: "Anyway, I was the one who turned her down. It's not some form of discrimination, I just didn't like them from the start."
*As she readily declared this, there wasn't a hint of doubt in Himeno's face.*
*This kind of thing happened several more times until now.*
*Cheerful and fun to be with, beautiful Himeno was also popular with our classmates. Since I was always with her, there were plenty of instances of jealousy.*
*But from what I saw, Himeno didn't discriminate against anyone or try to butter them up, just like with the girl from that group.*
*I often asked Himeno.*
Mayu: "Himeno, why are you friends with me?"
Himeno: "Is that really something you need to ask? More importantly, it's not like I'm being your friend as some kind of favor. I'm your friend because it's fun... Okay?"
*Everytime I asked, Himeno laughed it off.*
*I really loved the smile she gave me at those times.*
Mayu: "Hahh... hahh..."
*Once again, I've entered a passageway behind the tanks. I thought it would get me to the Fish of the World booth, but I was probably led astray while I was out of it.*
Mayu: "Anyway, I should get going."
*If another "something" borrows Mari's form and attacks me like earlier, I don't know if I can defend myself against it.*
*Safe no longer means anything, so I should speed through the passageway.*
Mayu: "Um..."
*Upon looking at the map, I see that there appears to be a room at the end of the passage.*
*Just as I expected, there are three doors before me.*
Mayu: "... Maybe this is some kind of reference room."
*Since it's like a reference room, maybe I can get some information.*
*As I think this, I push open the door to the reference room.*
*After confirming that no strange phenomena are taking place, I step inside the room.*
*Inside, there are massive amounts of books and files arranged on metal bookshelves. Looking at the spines of the books, they all appear to be technical in nature. Research data also appears to be mixed in. *
*I've heard that aquariums are also implied to be facilities for research. Seeing this room has convinced me of that.*
Mayu: "I see, it is..."
*It's not like there'd be any materials written about the strange things happening at this Manten Aquarium... Would there?*
*Since this place is, itself, an abnormality.*
*Having come to this conclusion, I notice a book near the entrance on deep-sea fish.*
*"Deep-sea fish" were the last words that girl left me with.*
*Strangely interested, I take the book into my hands.*
*It seems there's a technology called... "nanobubble."*
*An extremely small bubble, less than a micrometer. By suddenly compressing a micro-bubble (a bubble less than 50 micrometers), a relatively stable purification process can be performed.*
*It seems that, due to the properties of vapors in the bubble, various effects can be expected.*
*If ozone is sealed within these bubbles, water can be purified through bacterial processes. Since it's not using detergent or other chemical substances, it seems to be good for the environment.*
*There's also an example of sealing nitrogen inside the bubble so it dissolves down to a finite amount, preventing the oxidation of caught fish, so they can be delivered to cities while still fresh.*
*Of particular note is that freshwater and saltwater fish, using a similar composition in their bodily fluids, apply the bubble's technique to balance the electrolytes in freshwater with their own bodily fluids as a means of coexistence.*
*Freshwater fish cannot live in water that has a higher concentration than within their own bodies, and saltwater fish cannot live in water that has a lower concentration.*
*Furthermore, it seems the microscopic bubbles can stimulate activity at the cellular level. There are cases of the bubbles being used to cure the koi herpes virus, for example.*
*The Deep-sea Fish booth at Manten Aquarium seems to have been set up with this technique.*
*Naturally it would involve a cost incomparable to normal expenditures. In other words, it would take more money. The obvious downside then would have to be pressure for funds.*
Mayu: "... Er, this isn't the time for reading."
*It's all very interesting, but it would be something better researched after I returned to the real Manten Aquarium with Himeno. I return the book to its original spot and leave the reference room behind.*
Mayu: "A storage room... Wonder what's in here."
*On sneaking a peek, there are hula hoops, bowls, and other things for use in the shows. I also catch sight of mops, vacuums, and other cleaning supplies.*
*There seem to be few things of any use, such as books or documents.*
*I close the door, leaving the storage room behind me.*
Mayu: "This is... the director's office..."
*I mumble while looking up at the words written on the sign above the door.*
*This door's appearance differs greatly from the doors of the store room and the reference room, with red ornaments decorating it.*
*Even if there had been no sign, I would have known this room belonged to someone important on first glance.*
*The atmosphere from out here also seems to have a heavy air to it. It's as if I'm standing before the door of a school principal.*
Mayu: "If I don't go in and see..."
*I really won't learn anything just staring in from out here. The obvious truth is that I already possess the courage to go through with this.*
*Keeping an eye out for any phenomena while coming through the door, I push on the heavy door.*
*The door makes a scuffing sound on the floor as I open it.*
Mayu: "Please excuse... my intrusion..."
*It's not as if anyone would be in here, but without thinking, the words come out.*
*An elegant desk and chairs are situated at the center of the director's office, and bookshelves run along the walls without a gap in between.*
*The main wall when looking from the entrance is a giant tank, made of translucent acrylic material. No one can argue that this room isn't also one of the aquarium's displays.*
*The room is perfectly in order, giving me a sense of who the director is from the way their desk is. More than just neurotic, they don't seem to have left even one speck of dust.*
Mayu: "There doesn't seem to be... anything particularly strange, does there...?"
*Just as I mutter this, I notice a map on top of the desk. In this neat and tidy room, only the top of the desk appears to be a work in progress.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*After moving aside a book that seems to be in use as a weight, I find a map of the aquarium.*
*This one is different from the one I picked up back at the stage. The design seems older, and the publication date is from five years ago.*
Mayu: "Five years ago..."
*It's not as if I can ignore that number.*
*It's when everything started for Manten Aquarium, and for me.*
*Inside the map, I discover one defining difference.*
*... The Deep-sea Fish booth.*
*That place that was closed down five years ago... is recorded on this map.*
Mayu: "Could this be..."
*It seems that the Deep-sea Fish booth was just after coming out of the tunnel.*
*However, it's strange. I've been to that area several times, but there's only a long wall. There's no trace of anything else.*
*It seems that when it was closed down, they filled in the wall. It wasn't like that from the start. Questions rise within me as I consider this.*
*If where I am now is five years in the past, then I should be able to get to the Deep Sea Fish booth.*
Mayu: "Huh, this line..."
*As I repeatedly look over the map, I spot one red line. The line extends from the Deep-sea Fish booth all the way up to a circle at the top of the map where there is nothing else.*
*The staff passageway connects to the Deep-sea Fish booth...!*
*My idea seemingly confirmed, I nod without realizing it.*
*The Deep-sea Fish booth is the only place in this aquarium I haven't seen yet. Surely, Manten Aquarium's secrets will be there...!*
*Just a little further, and I can finally reach it.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*As I trace my finger along the map, I mentally position the booths in the aquarium on it.*
*Come out of the tunnel tank and turn right, and the Fish of the World booth should be there. Go in a straight line immediately after turning left at the passageway behind that, then take another left and there's the director's office.*
*In other words, if going straight through as the map says, this office is just past the tunnel tank. And between the tunnel tank and the director's office is situated the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
Mayu: "So then this room with the red circle is... here?"
*The director's office is directly connected to the Deep-sea Fish booth. Since there's nothing written in the map's blank spaces, save for the red circle, I think it's safe to say it's the room I'm in right now.*
*I stop looking at the map and begin to glance around the room. There is nothing but bookshelves along the walls, even though there should be something leading to the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
Mayu: "The tank..."
*Looking at the tank on the interior of the room, I mutter. Surely, there should be something within the tank.*
*I can't swim through the tank and reach the Deep-sea Fish booth. I'll have to look around the room again more thoroughly.*
*Just as I was about to look around the room, I spot a picture frame on the desk. It is of two people standing side-by-side, but it has probably been stretched from its original resolution, making it hard to clearly distinguish the faces.*
*However, they are familiar...*
Mayu: "Ah..."
*As I'm exploring the desk, I discover something of a metal frame below it. About fifty centimeters all around, the parts and materials on the inside are different.*
*On one side of it, I find a collapsible handle.*
Mayu: "This is......"
*I put my hand on the thing that looks like a handle with the intent to pull it out.*
Director: "STOP..."
Mayu: "Huh?"
*Without thinking, I look over my shoulder for the voice that seems to come from nowhere.*
Mayu: "That was... just my imagination, right?"
*Even if not just my imagination, it wouldn't be the first time here at Manten Aquarium. As if mocking me as I try to find an explanation for it, the voice continues even louder with "STOP IT...!"*
Mayu: "I have to... ignore it...!"
*If I want to get away from this voice, I have to keep going. I shut my eyes and pull harder.*
*At the same time, the ground trembles with a clatter. I can hear the sound of the room creaking.*
*I open my eyes and look at one of the legs on the desk.*
*... It's shaking.*
Mayu: "An earthquake...?!"
*In a normal earthquake, I would just stay under the desk, but right now I choose not to. I poke my head out to check on the rest of the room.*
*The bookshelves, everything on top of the desk... even the water in the tank is shaking. I prepare myself for the possibility of the tank rupturing, but it is a needless worry.*
*I become aware of one strange fact -- this shaking isn't a natural phenomena.*
*Because the place where I am standing isn't shaking at all.*
Director: "LEAVE!"
*As the voice echoes, the shaking of the furniture grows stronger. Still, that's the only thing.*
Director: "LEAVE, NOW!"
*The frequency of the voice echoing from out of nowhere is steadily increasing as well.*
Mayu: "I can't... leave... yet."
Director: "LEAVE!"
*I don't know if my words are getting through. As if in agreement, the vibration of the furniture grows more violent as the mysterious voice's strength increases.*
Mayu: "I will leave... I am leaving. But... I'll only leave... with Himeno...!"
*Instinctively, I give voice to my determination. I have no choice but to move on. I sit down once again and reach for the handle under the desk.*
Mayu: "Kyah!"
*At that moment, my body is suddenly flipped backwards.*
*It's not that I tumbled on my own, but it felt more like I was pulled back by an invisible hand.*
Mayu: "I won't give up. I... won't give up."
Director: "LEAVE, LEAVE, LEAVE!"
Mayu: "No, I won't!"
*Once more, my face is pushed close to the floor. My vision is concentrated on the small handle.*
*My hand could easily reach it, but...!*
Mayu: "Kyaah!"
*My body is once again knocked back by this unknown force.*
Mayu: "It doesn't matter how many times, I--!"
*It doesn't matter how many bad experiences I've had up to this point.*
*Surely with just one more step, I can reach this unknown territory.*
*What if there's nothing there?*
*No, there has to be.*
*This force trying to stop me is proof of that.*
Mayu: "Even if there's nothing there...!"
*I will leave here, together with Himeno.*
*I'm no longer the me who knew nothing. Even if I have to drag Himeno behind me, I will find a safe way out, and leave this place, no matter what.*
Mayu: "So I absolutely must... keep going!"
*At that moment, there is a pulsation."
*It isn't my heartbeat, but the entire room pulsating with a loud THUMP.*
Mayu: "What...? Is something else... happening now?"
*At that moment, the shaking of the furniture stops. In exchange, my own heart is pumping blood rapidly through my body with a BA-THUMP BA-THUMP.*
*All the lights in the room go out, and it becomes pitch black. I use my hands to feel for the handle on the floor, but I can't seem to find it.*
Mayu: "This... light..."
*A phosporescent red light begins to shine through the tank inside the director's office.*
*The water inside the tank becomes illuminated with that red color, faintly lighting the whole room.*
*The red light seems to be corroding the entire room, little by little.*
Mayu: "Fish... How...?"
*Fish swimming in the air begin to penetrate the room. They seem to be riding the flow of the light from the tank, swimming inside of the red light.*
*Fish swimming where there is no water -- probably a major misunderstanding I've had here in Manten Aquarium.*
Mayu: "This red light... is water...!"
*The room quickly fills with light. If the room fills with water, I'll drown!*
