*Staying in this room in this state is dangerous.*
*As I fumble about, my body slowly begins to float in the air.*
*This place is already filled with wavering water. Trying to control my body, I reach for the door of the director's office.*
*I have to get out at once.*
*At this rate, I need to pull myself together in a safe place!*
*My fingertip somehow touches the door.*
*I grab the doorknob, and I--*
*Can't move it... The one way in and out won't budge.*
Mayu: "Urk..."
*I cough up the remaining oxygen in my lungs in shock when the door refuses to open.*
*My body is nonchalantly overcome with the sensation of floating.*
*Just like that, I lose consciousness...*
*This is...*
*No longer being of this world, I become aware of something strange.*
*Then my soul is pulled into a large container.*
*Deep red emotions.*
*It isn't just me, but numerous souls, dominated by malice.*
*Now I no longer have my original form, instead becoming nothing more than one part of a whole, molding into a colony.*
*It's all over now.*
*The me that was me... is finished.*
*I can't... give up.*
*Courage springs up from within me.*
*If I'm going to escape from this Manten Aquarium with Himeno, I have to overcome this obstacle. Going from midair to underwater, I have to overcome this nightmare trying to force me to drown to death.*
*I've escaped so many times and been rescued from the brink of death.*
*However--*
Mayu: "I... won't lose anymore."
*The red light is eroding the room and numerous fish are swimming.*
*When I saved that girl, I overcame a similiar obstacle. By knowing something by chance about a creature's habitat, I was able to do something about the situation.*
*However, I can't use the same method here. There are no machines, and there are too many varieties of fish for me to know about them.*
*Moreover, I need to find a more basic solution.*
*That girl... whose name I don't even know, she talked about a "grudge." There were two cases -- a "murder investigation" and a "mass culling" involving this malice...*
*No.*
*There's no mistaking that Mari's disappearance is related to Manten Aquarium, but that isn't what I need right now.*
*The director's accidental death... was caused by the error in the Deep-sea Fish booth that also caused all those other deaths.*
*Either way, what if it wasn't an accident...?*
Mayu: "What if the whole thing was targeted at someone...?!"
*I can see what caused the closing down of Manten Aquarium. The bitter intent against the one who killed them.*
Mayu: "In other words, regret and a grudge--!"
*I bore great fear and anxiety towards Manten Aquarium.*
*But if my way of thinking is correct, then what I should be afraid of isn't the aquarium, but the deceased that have taken on its form.*
Himeno: "Maybe so. There are so many rumors though -- seeing a man's ghost in the tanks, the Deep-sea Fish booth being open only on the day of a full moon, that all of the water changes into blood, or that you can hear crying from the back of the tanks..."
*Himeno's words from that lunch break long ago all come back to me now.*
Mayu: "The director... You!"
*As if agreeing with my conjecture, the red light grows more dense, and it becomes hard for me to breathe.*
*I don't have time.*
*With just one more step, I should be able... to overcome this phenomena.*
*Almost subconsciously, I reach for the top of the desk.*
*My fingertips brush the wooden frame of the picture, then struggle against the water's resistance... and pull it down.*
Mayu: "I've... seen this picture somewhere before... I have!"
*I let go of the picture frame and, steadily floating about, try to gain control of my body and reach into my pocket.*
*Relying on the tactile feedback from my fingers, I search my pocket.*
Mayu: "This is it...!"
*What I pull out is the keychain with the starfish attached. Stuck to the back is the sticker picture of the man and the little girl.*
*Something... on the bookshelf...*
*Hoping against hope, I try to control my wobbling body and reach out my hand.*
*I touch the books, something... anything would be good... I search for something to help me overcome this situation.*
*Not business administration, not architecture.*
*Those aren't the kind of books I need.*
Mayu: "Guh..."
*During this time, air slips from my nose in the form of bubbles.*
*However, I'm still okay. I plug up my nose with my left hand and search the bookshelf with my right.*
*Heavy books, thick books, I fling them all off the shelf.*
*The books float for a second in the water, then sink right away.*
*Once a book falls, I put it out of mind.*
*What I'm looking for is a clue to get through this place.*
Mayu: "Gah..."
*Air is leaving my body. I don't watch the bubbles as they float up.*
*It's not just bubbles, but the equivalent of my life. Watching it vanish could really shatter my mind.*
*If I have even a hint, I'll be fine. To address the director, a hint is all--!*
Mayu: "----!"
*Then a ray of hope appears for a brief second.*
*Just as I think I my air supply won't last any longer, a piece of paper that had been lodged in a book passes before my eyes.*
*On top of the paper are two silhouettes.*
*An adult and a child. A man and a girl. A faded photograph.*
*One of them is a man past middle age, wearing an impeccable suit. The colors of his clothing are the same as Saginuma Rieko's.*
*I'm convinced that this is the director.*
Mayu: "... That's right... Ack...!"
*Having relaxed for a second, the corners of my mouth loosen as well.*
*The bubble that leaves my mouth starts a flow in the water.*
*That slight current of water lifts the photograph towards the water's surface.*
Mayu: "Wa... it..."
*I can no longer continue breathing. That picture was my last clue. My last chance to speak with the resounding voice of the director.*
*I chase the picture, swimming higher and higher.*
*Assisted by the buoyancy supporting my body, swimming up is easy.*
*The picture is higher and higher still.*
*With my right arm stretched out, I follow the picture toward the surface.*
*As high as it can go.*
*... Huh?*
*Was the room always this big?*
*Unable to sense the ceiling, I just keep going up through the water.*
*The picture is very far away, disappearing as it goes higher and higher.*
*... Huh?*
*What was it with that picture in the first place?*
*My vision is nothing but deepest blue, my previous focus no longer visible.*
*... Huh?*
*Is this enough?*
*I should need oxygen.*
*But right now, I don't feel the slightest pain from being unable to breathe.*
*Melting into the blue, my consciousness is freed.*
*That's right, long ago I--*
*Became a part of the wavering water's flow.*
*Where did I begin, and where do I end?*
*Only the things I think are keeping me connected.*
*That too will end.*
*It's mysterious, or so that's how I feel.*
*You, and you, and me?*
*There is no difference between us, we are but one larger flow.*
*The things we can feel are larger than we ever could imagine.*
*Something is here.*
*Carrying with it something I've lost, some sort of form has come...*
*My own body has taken on buoyancy and is floating around.*
*This entire room is now underwater.*
*It doesn't just have water in it, but is in water.*
*As I try to control my wavering body, I search my pocket. My fingers feel something, and I pull it out.*
*It is the keychain with the starfish charm. A sticker photo is attached to the back.*
*My own body is floating in air.*
Mayu: "Director... if you can hear me! If you can... hear me from somewhere, watch this!"
*A bubble as big as my mouth leaps out. The red bubble rises up through the interval between the air and the water.*
*I cover my nose and mouth with my left hand and hoist the keychain up high.*
Mayu: "Ergh...!"
*My lungs are at their limits. The results of my defiance will be life or death.*
*If my conviction is nothing more than a mistake, I die by suffocation.*
*However--!*
Mayu: "Urk..."
*This is... the last bit of air in my lungs.*
*The last bit of air in my lungs. This little red bubble climbing to the heavens.*
*Along with my memories.*
*The inside of my head is becoming murky.*
*There isn't enough oxygen.*
*I'll die.*
*In just a matter of seconds, I will drown to death.*
Sayo: "... Stop this, Father."
*In time with a girl's voice, I feel the pain of my body crashing into the ground.*
Mayu: "Kyaah...!"
*With a thud, my butt begins to hurt. However that pain is proof that the director's office has returned to normal. Without hesitation I feel grateful for gravity.*
Sayo: "It's all right now, Father."
Sayo: "Sa... yo..."
Mayu: "Sayo...?"
*Just like when I tried to open the floor, a voice echoes through the room.*
*As I steady my breathing, I look around me and survey the room. As expected, I cannot find the source of the voice.*
Mayu: "I've heard... this voice before."
*It is a voice I've heard many times in Manten Aquarium. A hollow voice, one that projects well.*
*This isn't a mistake. It's the voice of the girl, the one killed by Saginuma Rieko.*
*I take another look at the picture on the keychain in my hand.*
Mayu: "By Father, do you mean that this is...?!"
*It sounds like she must be his daughter. In other words, that girl... was the daughter... of the director?*
Mayu: "She is! So then... that's why..."
*I realize that she is the daughter of the director whose murder was made to look accidental, which is why she has so tenaciously pursued the secrets of Manten Aquarium.*
*Along with surprise, I feel understanding.*
*A blue light shines through the translucent tank. The ominous red light from earlier is no longer there.*
*In the spaces between the blue light, I can see two silhouettes. Like images projected on a screen they are a bit vague, but I can easily identify them.*
*A middle-aged man and a girl. The two of them face each other in a pleasant manner.*
Director: "Sayo... you've gotten... so big."
*The silhouette that appears to be the director says in a kind voice. I decide to watch the pair's conversation quietly for a bit so as not to interrupt.*
Sayo: "You're making such a big deal of it. More importantly, I found her. The criminal who killed you."
Director: "Sayo, you..."
Sayo: "Saginuma Rieko. I was killed by her as well, but after coming to Manten Aquarium so many times, I was finally able to confirm it on my own, so I am more or less satisfied."
Director: "Because of me, even you were-- Sayo, I..."
Sayo: "Don't say any more. I have no regrets. If you keep going, all of my efforts will be in vain. I lived and died solely for my objective."
*Sayo says in a cool voice. Talking about her objective even after her own death, what a tragic thing.*
*Listening to Sayo's words, the director's mouth becomes a straight line as he looks down, holding back his regrets.*
Sayo: "Your face has changed so much in the last few hours."
Mayu: "Huh... Me...?"
*Sayo shifts her gaze from the director to me. She turns her attention from the director and continues.*
Sayo: "The fact that you're not surprised to see a ghost really isn't normal, but I understand how you feel."
Mayu: "Yeah... that's right. I suppose you could say I've gotten used to it. I wish I could have gotten to know your name. You never would tell me."
Sayo: "It doesn't matter now, since I'm dead anyway."
*Even now, Sayo talks like it's someone else's problem.*
*The director at her side responds to the question about her name.*
Director: "Sakuragi Sayo... that's this girl's full name. I am Sakuragi Isao, Sayo's father, and the director of Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "So you are the director after all."
Director: "It would probably be more accurate to say 'former.' Ah, but it seems I lost my sense of reason for a long time. I wonder why it's only now that I've regained my senses..."
*"Lost his sense of reason," he says, as if that's no longer the case. Nevertheless, his story seems to substantiate the theory that Manten Aquarium gives form to grudges.*
*Having returned to his senses, no strange phenomena should take place.*
*On the contrary, I could say that the earlier poltergeist phenomenon the director caused would also serve as proof, but I don't see any need to blame him.*
Mayu: "Wasn't it seeing Sayo-chan that returned you to your senses?"
Sayo: "Hey, stop adding 'chan.'I have a name too -- I don't like it."
Mayu: "... Eh. Then it's Sayo. I have a name, too -- Mayumi -- so stop with the 'hey' and 'you' stuff."
Sayo: "Sigh."
*Sayo breathes an affected sigh, then continues talking. She looks listless, but somehow, it seems... like she's smiling.*
Sayo: "If your conjecture is right, Mayumi, then I'll probably be glad."
Mayu: "Conjecture?"
Sayo: "That I returned my father to his senses."
Director: "Fool! Is there anything to be happy about? If you hadn't died, you wouldn't have met me. My beloved daughter... dead because of me. Is there really anything to be happy about...?"
Sayo: "Father, calm down. You'll make the aquarium get weird again."
Mayu: "I misjudged you, Sayo. I thought you were amazing because you were steadfast in your determination and your resolve, but I labeled you a cold girl. Really... you were kind."
*Tears faintly gather in the director's eyes. If demons can have tears in their eyes, then ghosts can cry.*
*That's why they call it infectious crying... I suppose. Even as I watch the two of them, the corner of my eye becomes hot.*
*The exchange between the two of them is so natural. It has the rhythm of a conversation between parent and child.*
*It's something that won't happen again for them.*
Mayu: "I'm sorry... I couldn't save you... I... saw you, Sayo... at the moment you got killed... I couldn't save you...... I-I..."
Sayo: "Don't worry about it. This is the result of my own faltering. It isn't your fault, Mayumi."
Mayu: "But, but..."
Sayo: "Don't you have someone you need to save? If so, you can't waver."
Director: "Sayo, you've matured. You've become able to encourage others."
Sayo: "Be quiet, Father! Well, I'm glad you weren't killed by my father."
Director: "... You've grown up, Sayo."
*The air surrounding them warms my heart, but the realization that this is nothing more than a transient moment causes my chest to tighten.*
Mayu: "Huh, you two..."
*Suddenly, the blue light changes to white. It floods the room like a fluorescent bulb, gradually growing stronger.*
*Without realizing it, I narrow my eyes.*
Mayu: "What's happened, all of a sudden...?"
Sayo: "Who knows? Maybe we're crossing over? I am satisfied, knowing who the killer is."
Mayu: "Wha-- But... wait, just a bit..."
*Crossing over. No longer having any attachment to this world.*
*Sayo has achieved her goal. She and the director have miraculously reunited after overcoming space and time.*
*No!*
*I cry out from the bottom of my heart.*
*However, I can't give that scream voice. Because Sayo knows my resolve.*
*I don't want her to see me acting uncool... because even though Sayo is dead, she is cheering me on.*
Director: "Mayumi-kun... was it? Before I disappear, I will tell you what I know. Even so, many events right before and after my death are vague."
Mayu: "This is... much too soon. Will you really disappear?"
Director: "My soul was originally supposed to cross over five years ago. Being able to cross over in and of itself is something to be happy about."
Mayu: "Director..."
Sayo: "I don't want to cause anyone trouble like Father did. I just want to go right away."
Director: "Before our souls are taken..."
*The two have completely reconciled their lives.*
*Souls being taken. It's something I only barely understand. If it happens, just as it did before with the director, then only their grudge and malice will continue to be expressed in this world.*
*When it's time to die, one crosses over. It's probably best to see it from a spirit's position.*
*But then they can't be happy together!*
*I stiffly endure those words.*
*Unconcerned with my own thoughts, the director begins to speak.*
Director: "Five years ago, Manten Aquarium experienced financial hardship. With diverse options for entertainment and a more direct subway system, customer traffic decreased as people were flocking to towns other than Manten."
Director: "The management of the aquarium was seeing large costs from the purchase of exhibits, the large amount of feed required, and the cost of paying for the employees. Also, due to the location of Manten Aquarium, the cost of transporting seawater is high."
Director: "In addition, the Deep-sea Fish booth that served as the centerpiece at that time used the latest technology and required enormous maintenance costs due to its size."
Director: "I was being pressed by many decisions. Letting go of employees, reducing display space, the widening of the target demographic... and the closing of the Deep-sea Fish booth."
Director: "I couldn't bring myself to cast aside anything from my beloved aquarium... I tried to do the impossible in the hopes of producing results, but in the end... it boiled down to a confrontation with Saginuma Rieko and those who stood with her."
Director: "I was eliminated in the most rational way possible. In other words, I was killed. It was made to look like an on-the-job accident."
*The director continues, unperturbed by my trembling.*
*I rise to my feet and reach out a hand to Sayo. She is also listening quietly to the director's matter-of-fact way of speaking. Her form seems so fragile.*
*I am unable to touch the image she is projecting into the light.*
Director: "Despite my position amongst management, I let her know, in not so subtle terms, how I felt about her. This gave her all the motive she needed. I have no doubt that my murder was, in some part, because I pushed her too far."
*Beside me, Sayo lowers her head. However, she listens carefully to the director's monologue. I study the director's face and wait for the rest of the story.*
Director: "Then Saginuma Rieko sent all of the fish in the Deep-sea Fish booth to their deaths. There were 1,217 of them in all."
Director: "There were probably those employees who had their doubts in regards to the recurrence of unlikely accidents. Those that knew even the slightest bit about our management stuck their heads in the sand and just dismissed it as cost-cutting in some way."
Mayu: "That's awful...!"
Sayo: "By all rights, it is something Father should consider as a manager."
Director: "Yes, at times such a thing becomes necessary. Saginuma Rieko held neither reverence nor regret towards the fish she, a human, killed. She probably smiled while killing them, forcing them to bear the brunt of her rage and grudge against me."
Director: "My soul was already here. It's hard to put into words, but I'm what you might call a 'jibakurei' -- a spirit bound to this place. Sympathizing with the souls of all of those fish, I offered up my soul to Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "So then... the grudge you and the fish bore created the world I am in now?"
*The director nods with a serious face. I instinctively swallow.*
Director: "The Red Manten Aquarium... I've taken to calling it that."
Director: "Without regard for when, they took vengeance to dispel their regrets. It didn't matter if it involved people or fish, they merely turned to anger. There was nothing I could do."
Director: "As a human with an independent will and emotions -- my soul was the cornerstone, but the fish were the ones to rule Manten Aquarium."
Director: "There was no distinction for the value of life. The thousands of fish spirits overpowered my own sense of reasoning."
Mayu: "Then why now...?"
Director: "I am sorry, but I don't clearly understand it either. I may be ubiquitous throughout this Red Manten Aquarium, but I am merely a core that bore its grudge, nothing more. There are far too many things I cannot explain."
Director: "But if I were to venture a guess, I would think that my reunion with Sayo has led my soul to stop producing negative emotions. Upon becoming useless, I was probably ejected from the Red Manten Aquarium."
Director: "Theories and such probably don't matter. Mayumi-kun... it's thanks to you bringing Sayo and I together that this miracle occurred. I don't need any further explanation than that."
*The director smiles peacefully.*
*He probably doesn't remember the details of what he put us through. If he did, he wouldn't be smiling.*
*Still, it isn't like the director is directly responsible for his sins.*
*Sins are born from a person's ill will. If I trust the director's words, then that isn't the case here.*
*It isn't in me to bear hatred for something with no form.*
Director: "A cornerstone. Having lost me, the one directing their malice, the fish spirits should also disappear -- they should cross over."
*The director says in a relaxed tone, after some thought.*
Mayu: "In other words... the Red Manten Aquarium will disappear?"
Director: "If my theory is correct, that is what should happen. In turn with my soul's disappearance, the Red Manten Aquarium should begin to crumble. This isn't something I know for sure... merely a hunch, but it seems I am a part of Manten Aquarium after all."
Director: "Please don't lament the fact that I will disappear. It was your courage that rescued us from this place, this Red Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "I... didn't--"
*-- Save you. My words of denial won't come out.*
*Surely, the director is satisfied already. After having his soul bound for five years and squeezed for every drop of malice. Just releasing him was saving him, so I can't dilute that fact.*
Sayo: "Before we part ways, I'll return this to you."
Mayu: "This... this keychain..."
*Sayo hands me a keychain. It is a droplet-shaped keychain, one with a jellyfish attached. It is unmistakably the keychain from my memories.*
Mayu: "Why, Sayo?"
Sayo: "You were running without watching where you were going, and you ran into me. Don't you remember?"
Mayu: "When I was chasing Mari... I ran into someone. That's right, Himeno picked it up in all of the confusion...!"
Sayo: "It was something incredibly precious to me. It never seemed like something someone could mistake. Even now, I still don't understand the mix-up."
Sayo: "I finally realized that you had my keychain when I looked at it, and that the one I picked up was yours, Mayumi."
Mayu: "Thank you. So it wasn't that Himeno lied... The mix-up was because there were two look-alikes."
*I hand the keychain with the starfish charm to Sayo. The father and daughter smile at me from within the sticker picture. This piece of their lost past leaves my hands.*
*As I hand it to her, I am unable to touch her. However, the treasure safely lands in Sayo's hand.*
Mayu: "Why didn't you tell me back then?"
Sayo: "Hope, probably."
Mayu: "What do you mean?"
Sayo: "I won't say any more than that."
*I am about to respond, but Sayo speaks without giving me a chance.*
Sayo: "You know, I'm a bit jealous of that Himeno girl."
Mayu: "Why...?"
Sayo: "Think about it yourself."
Mayu: "... You're so mean."
*Sayo grips the keychain tightly, and shuts her eyes. She takes a small breath, then nods.*
Sayo: "You walked a different path than I, Mayumi. Even now I think so."
Mayu: "Yeah..."
Sayo: "Mayumi, what you've done is resolve yourself to save the both of you. I don't want to think that I'm wrong. However..."
Sayo: "You aren't wrong either, Mayumi. The world is a place with few answers."
*Sayo laughs with a "Haha," then smiles. This seems out of character for her.*
*But Sayo's smile is very natural. Surely this is this girl's true nature.*
*Nah, the cool Sayo I spoke with first is the real deal.*
*Just as Sayo said, there's no perfect answer anywhere. Regardless of the director's choices, there were none that could have lead to a perfect solution.*
*Even so, we all must choose. Even if it means casting aside that which we don't choose.*
Sayo: "I wasn't able to be like you. I was filled with a weakness I couldn't account for."
Mayu: "Sayo, you aren't weak at all. You... saved me."
*Her eyes closed, Sayo shakes her head.*
Sayo: "Mayumi, you have strength that I don't. You won't fall apart at the truth. You will save Himeno, no matter what."
Mayu: "Thank you... I'm sorry... I... in the end..."
Sayo: "Hey."
Mayu: "Huh...?"
Sayo: "Nah, nothing. I don't care for stubbornness after all."
*The light surrounding Sayo grows far brighter than the light surrounding the director. Gradually, Sayo's silhouette grows dim.*
*I understand, this is the light of Sayo's life.*
Mayu: "Bye-bye~"
Sayo: "Yes, bye-bye. I won't say 'see you later' or anything. If you find your way here after a hundred years, I'll just turn you away."
Mayu: "... There's no way I could live that long."
*I am unable to stop my emotions from overflowing. The drops of light spilling over melt into the even stronger light. It's the same for Sayo.*
*She would probably get mad if I say so, so I won't, but--*
Sayo: "Well, Father, I'll go on ahead. This time, you can follow me."
*Essentially, it's decided. Sayo is my friend now. So as much as I continue to struggle, I won't break so easily.*
*By the time I've wiped away my tears, Sayo's light has already vanished.*
*The director closes his eyes and begins to pray. Seeing me stand up, he quietly opens his mouth.*
Director: "I don't know exactly what will happen when this place crumbles. Before it happens, I think it best that you... get out of Manten Aquarium."
Mayu: "I want to ask you... about Saginuma... Rieko-san. She's still alive in this aquarium... isn't she?"
*The woman who killed not just the director, but his daughter too. Her insane smile is etched in my mind and won't disappear.*
Director: "She shouldn't be living. I can't remember it clearly, but I do know this... Manten Aquarium did exact its vengeance on her."
Director: "If she's still here in the aquarium... then she's nothing other than a ghost. She's a captive soul, bound here by the grudge and malice that pervades this place. If so, when this place crumbles, she'll disappear as well. You needn't worry."
Mayu: "I don't need... to worry. Is that true?"
Director: "Those things left behind in time will not change."
Mayu: "I understand. That and..."
Director: "Yes, what is it?"
Mayu: "You didn't see a girl called Mari somewhere, did you? She looks like Sayo when she was a child... Perhaps, like you, she became a spirit and is in this Manten Aquarium."
*I have asked the disappearing director something that has caught his attention.*
*If he is one with Manten Aquarium, then perhaps he does know something.*
*However, that hope is quickly dashed.*
Director: "I... I do not know. Ever since my death, my mind has become rather vague and fragmented."
Mayu: "Is that... so..."
Director: "However, as it is vague, I cannot definitively say that she isn't here. In the Deep-sea Fish booth where I lost my life, there may be some truth even I do not know."
Mayu: "Speaking of which, Director, when you were under the influence of the grudge, you told me to 'leave.' Did you understand somewhere deep down the danger of the place where you were killed?"
Director: "I wonder... Honestly, I do not know. Well then, it seems the time has come."
Mayu: "Director...!"
Director: "I can no longer stay here. I realize it may be irresponsible. However, it seems this alone I cannot fight. In this world, there are powers that go beyond human understanding."
Mayu: "P-Please wai--"
Director: "If I wait too much longer, I won't be able to follow Sayo."
Director: "When I reach the other side, I will pray for you. That you may be saved and blessed..."
*I am unable to stop him as he is wrapped in a brilliant light, just as Sayo was. In the next moment, his form is gone.*
Mayu: "Was he able to cross over?"
*My question is directed at thin air, so naturally, there is no reply.*
Mayu: "I wonder if this is a time when you should say congratulations..."
*Thinking it over a bit, I look up. In that position, I close my eyes.*
Mayu: "Please, find peace..."
*Before I realize it, I'm alone again. But it's all right.*
*Even if something happens, I will not fail. I have prepared myself.*
Mayu: "Hahh... hahh..."
*I've run all over Manten Aquarium, returning to the stage.*
Himeno: "Ma... yu..."
*Having reached the shed on the edge, I caress Himeno's form as she lies on her back.*
*She's alive. For that alone, I'm incredibly happy.*
Himeno: "Hurk...!"
Mayu: "Are you okay?!"
*Her face red and feverish, Himeno seems to be suffering.*
*There seems to be some change. She is no longer spewing out the parasites that were consuming her body.*
Mayu: "Could it have... stopped...?"
*The parasites' strange propogation and assault. Maybe they were linked to the grudge of Manten Aquarium.*
*Now that the director has crossed over, maybe the so-called curse has vanished as well.*
*In other words, at this point Himeno has been freed from this absurdity. Now all that's left is to get her out of here, take her to the hospital, get her on an IV, and restore her strength.*
Mayu: "Let's go. Let's get going... but I can't carry you on my own."
*Physically, there's little I can do. At times like this, I regret my own lack of motor skills.*
*I glance around the room, looking for something that can help.*
Mayu: "This is it...!"
*There is a handtruck for moving boxes. They probably have large tools and such on hand for the shows.*
Mayu: "I can do it. With this we can make it!"
*I call out to the suffering Himeno many times. I bring the handtruck over to Himeno and somehow set her on top of it.*
*From there, I get a vigorous start. We charge through a route with few ups and downs, heading for the gate.*
*The handtruck is slicker than expected, so I push it with all my might.*
*It's only been a five minute dash. Having reached the finish line unharmed, I stand completely still before several turnstiles, one right next to the other.*
Mayu: "Finally, we made it here."
*In just a number of steps, I'll be able to save Himeno. If that's the case, I can keep going.*
*At the moment we pass through the ticket gate, it feels as if we are enveloped in light. Together with Himeno, I head past the light.*
*A week has passed since that dramatic escape.*
*There was no change in my physical health, and the weakened Himeno is in the hospital even now.*
Mayu: "I came to pay you a visit, Himeno."
*Lately, I visit her hospital room everyday. Everytime I go to see her, she is so happy.*
Himeno: "Haha, thanks... Mayu."
*Looking at the sickly Himeno is painful. If I could, I'd get her out of the hospital right away.*
Himeno: "Well, it seems they have to keep me a little longer for observation. So, just wait a little longer."
*Lying on the bed, Himeno smiles at me.*
*I don't remember if it started that day or not. Despite the fact that she is hospitalized, Himeno's condition has deteriorated.*
*Even after a month has passed, Himeno has lost her enthusiasm from just before our escape from Manten Aquarium.*
*As each day passes, her condition worsens.*
*At the suggestion of Himeno's parents, she has changed hospitals, but her circumstances don't seem to have improved.*
Himeno: "......"
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*I don't know how much time has passed. It's probably been half a year since we escaped the Red Manten Aquarium.*
*The amount of words Himeno says has drastically decreased, and it has become difficult for her to eat on her own.*
Himeno: "........."
Mayu: "Himeno, why..."
*I wanted us to eat ice cream together. We had so many fun things ahead of us.*
*My heart overflows from its very core with thoughts and feelings.*
Mayu: "Smile, once more..."
*I don't know how many times I've said these words in this hospital room.*
*However, she shows no hint of a reaction.*
*Himeno's attending physician says, quite bluntly, that the cause is probably something unknown to science at present.*
*Then doesn't that mean I wasn't able to save Himeno?!*
Mayu: "No... I don't want us to say goodbye here."
*It doesn't matter how many years it takes.*
*If I can see that smile again.*
*So, please...*
*I reach for the small handle under the director's desk.*
Mayu: "Wait just a little longer, Himeno...!"
*I have no time, but that means I have to be all the more careful. In a way, this is Manten Aquarium's final puzzle.*
*The closed down Deep-sea Fish booth. Everything will be settled there. There has to be an end to the Red Manten Aquarium.*
*Embracing the vigor in my chest, I pull open the small door.*
*I descend the ladder leading from the director's office, walk along the dark underground passageway for several meters, and reach another ladder.*
*Causing a dull sound to echo, I climb the ladder.*
*As I approach the door overhead, cold air flows through.*
Mayu: "O-Oof."
*Pushing the door up with my head, I've reached the top of the ladder.*
Mayu: "This is... the Deep-sea Fish booth..."
*I mutter, closing the door I came up through. Inside it's pitch black.*
*As if to match the real deep sea, it is a world with no light.*
*My unease in the dark comes from deep down in my heart. However, at this moment, I am able to suppress that feeling. Stretching my arms out to the left and right, I carefully walk on.*
*There must be something here. I am absolutely sure of it.*
Mayu: "Huh, that light..."
*After walking for a bit, I find a light floating in my path. It's neither the red light nor the blue one I saw in the director's office, but rather a golden light, like that of the morning sun.*
*Called by the light, I move forward.*
Mayu: "But I know."
*Having gotten close enough to touch the light, my movements seem to be off. The ball of light before my eyes has begun dodging me.*
Mayu: "A deep-sea fish, eh? That much I can understand."
*A footballfish. Perhaps the most famous of the deep-sea fish. It emits light, even in the depths of the deep sea, slyly awaiting its prey.*
*But I am no longer prey.*
*Passing the light, it's as if something has been blown open, as things begin to flicker throughout the room. Yellow lights, blue lights, even red lights, they all rush about the room like fibers.*
*All of the lights waver like the surface of the water.*
*Thanks to the intermittent light, I begin to grasp the full form of the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
*Fish are swimming all throughout the room. The light itself is the fishes' habitat. This is the sea to the fish spirits that have lost the malice that once guided them.*
Mayu: "I'm not afraid anymore..."
*The director himself said they had all been freed from their malice.*
*Just as he said, the fish no longer have any aggression. The oarfish, the monkfish, the hagfish...*
*There are also ones with scary faces, transparent fish, and fish that glow beautifully, though differently from the footballfish...*
*This room, filled with several deep-sea fish, is more realistic and more magnificent than any other display space.*
Mayu: "I'm no longer afraid. So come on out..."
*Weaving through all of the fish, I touch a wall on the inside of the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
Mayu: "Mari!"
*Just as I call her name, I feel my legs slam close together.*
Mayu: "Huh..."
*I suppose this is what you'd call carelessness.*
*My legs have been grabbed by a large tentacle. It has white skin and pale pink suckers.*
*At my feet is the deep sea. I can see a black shadow, far closer to me than the bottom of the sea.*
*I've seen this creature on the news on TV. It's the largest creature on planet Earth.*
Mayu: "A giant... squid...!"
*My impatience is greater than my fear. Before it can pull me in, I have to escape.*
*But the suction pads are strong, and as I struggle, another tentacle approaches.*
Mari: "Dummy...!"
Mayu: "Huh..."
*That is the voice of hope.*
*A small hand reaches down from above as I am pulled further into the deep sea. A hand of salvation for me, as I writhe against imminent death in the darkness.*
Mayu: "No!"
*As I scream out, I push away the hand being extended to me.*
*Pretending to help me, but really tricking me. This is my logic based on what I've experienced over and over again at this aquarium.*
Mari: "I see..."
*The regretful voice quickly disappears, and in exchange the tentacle pulling my legs pulls harder.*
*I feel intense pain, as if my legs are about to be ripped off.*
Mayu: "Guh......"
*I frantically try to resist, but the only thing I feel is myself being pulled down further.*
*I gradually forget to breathe.*
*Below my eye level, there is an enormous giant squid.*
*As I close my eyes, my limbs are torn away by the thick tentacles.*
*My own body gently floats along the water.*
*No objective, just floating.*
*Before long, the tentacle has wrapped around my slender neck.*
*Just like that, I am pulled even further into the deep sea.*
*At this point, I have already died.*
*It is an instantaneous death, thanks to my broken neck.*
*I am watching the spectacle of my own death from somewhere else. The sight of my death from this enormous creature preying upon me, and my own almost comedic powerlessness...*
Mari: "Big sister."
*I feel like I heard a voice.*
*Unable to find the owner of that voice, I can only look around the room drenched in red light.*
Mari: "Big sister! If you go away before we get to make up, I really will hate you!"
Mayu: "I'm not... going anywhere!"
*First, I grasp her hand tightly with my right hand. At such a miracle, I begin to feel my zeal returning.*
*Next, I grab that little hand with my left hand. I can feel my energy growing stronger.*
*I am pulled up with a great amount of power. It isn't just the owner of the voice's strength either. I can feel the intervention of a miraculous amount of power.*
*With just that, the tentacles that hold my legs and have refused to let go are easily peeled away.*
Mayu: "Ow!"
*Pulled from the darkness, I land on my backside.*
*I don't know how many times today I've fallen down. Please cut it out, the bones in my butt feel like they'll break.*
*This is no time for such carefree thoughts. I've ended up in the Deep-sea Fish booth once more.*
*This room with so many types of fish just swimming about.*
*She is here. I puff out my cheeks, place my hands on my hips, and push myself forward with my palms.*
Mayu: "Mari... really...... I..."
Mari: "Huh? Who are you? I thought you were my big sister, but you're grown up?"
*Looking at me as I lift my head is a girl with long hair -- Mari is looking down at me.*
Mayu: "It's me. Nakanobe Mayumi. Your... big sister."
Mari: "Yup, I'm not wrong after all! You're a grown up, but you're my big sister!"
*It wouldn't be surpising for her not to recognize me.*
*In the five years that have passed, as I have been going through a growth spurt, I have grown quite noticeably. I cut my hair that was, at that time, as long as Mari's, and my looks and my body... have become a little more mature.*
*But Mari is laughing and nodding. She called me "big sister."*
Mari: "I-I've been looking for you this whole time, big sister. Since you got lost, I thought I should find you, big sister. The whole time."
*I realize that Mari's voice has an echo to it.*
*It isn't because of the room that we're in. It's probably due to the light shining through Mari's silhouette.*
Mari: "I-I saw something scary. A lady with a scary face wouldn't let the man who looked worried get out of the water... She was drowning him."
Mayu: "A lady, a man...? Could that be the director and Saginuma Rieko...?"
Mari: "I got so scared, and I ran away. But this lady chased after me."
Mayu: "Saginuma Rieko, Mari, she..."
Mari: "I ran and ran... I hid myself in some sort of box. I thought of how I wanted to see you and stayed there the whole time. Then suddenly, the whole place filled with water, and I... I went to sleep."
Mayu: "Mari... I'm sorry, I couldn't save you..."
*Having witnessed the scene of a murder, Mari ran away within Manten Aquarium as Saginuma Rieko pursued her. Then she ended up in some empty tank that was then filled with water, and then she--*
*Was it an accident, Saginuma Rieko's doing, or could it have possibly been that she slipped into the Red Manten Aquarium?*
*In this world of strange phenomena, Mari herself probably doesn't even know.*
*The one truth is that Mari's life was brought to an end.*
Mayu: "After that, why did you come back here?"
Mari: "I don't know. I drifted, sleeping the whole time. Then I thought that you had come, so I woke myself up!"
Mayu: "Mari... you're dead, aren't you...?"
*I say these words, enduring the pain in my heart.*
*It feels like I have no choice but to say them.*
Mari: "Yes. Sorry, big sister."
Mayu: "The one who should apologize... is me. I had no idea you faced something so terrible, and I wasn't able to save you. Even though you waited here for me, I couldn't save you..."
Mari: "That's right, geez. Because you always get lost... Big sister, are you crying?"
*Mari says this to me in an innocent voice.*
Mayu: "I'm not... crying."
*I am her big sister. Her big sister, five years her senior. So then, I can't cry. Mari is smiling so broadly, I can't let her see me do something as uncool as crying.*
*Finally I was able to see Mari. Finally, I was able to reunite with Mari.*
*No matter what form she takes, this isn't... supposed to be a sad thing, is it?*
Mari: "Well, I'm going now."
Mayu: "To where...?"
Mari: "I don't know, but this cradle where I've been sleeping feels as if it's slowly disappearing. That's why I feel somehow that I'm not supposed to be here anymore."
Mayu: "Mari... thank you, for saving me."
*I was unable to save Mari, but even so, Mari brought me through my own crisis.*
Mayu: "I'm pretty pathetic for a big sister, huh...?"
Mari: "No. After all, you came to see me, didn't you?"
Mayu: "... Yeah."
*Manten Aquarium's final truth.*
*For me, that would be Mari's death.*
*I realized this a long time ago, honestly, but I was unable to accept it and let it go so easily.*
*Little by little, the light enveloping Mari grows stronger.*
*If the director was like a shell for the Red Manten Aquarium, then Mari, surely being one drop amongst the collective, was being released. The thing that held her here is now gone.*
*All around me, numerous other lights are climbing towards heaven. As if the ceiling is no obstacle, pillars of light fill the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
*This other Manten Aquarium is vanishing.*
Mari: "Big sister, I love you. How about you?"
Mayu: "It's the same for me. As it should be..."
Mari: "Well, I'm going then!"
Mayu: "Wait, Mari."
Mari: "What is it, big sister?"
Mayu: "This keychain. It's my treasure, so..."
*I pull the keychain bearing all of my memories from my pocket.*
*I dropped it once but it came back, as if guided by destiny. A true treasure.*
*Just as if she were seeing it for the first time, Mari makes a sad face.*
Mari: "No fair... After all, back then, you told me how important it was!"
Mayu: "It is important... and precious, so I'll give it to you, Mari. I am your big sister, Mari."
*It was such a trivial thing that I regret from that day.*
*I won't make that same mistake again.*
*What it is that's important to me -- I feel like I understand that a little better now than back then.*
Mari: "Hehehe... thank you, but sorry. I gave my keychain to a girl that looks just like me, so I can't trade with you."
*A faint band of light appears in Mari's eyes.*
*Illuminated by the numerous twinkling lights, Mari is so pretty.*
Mayu: "It's all right."
*In sync with my words, I rub Mari's head. I cannot touch her long hair, but even so, I embrace Mari.*
Mayu: "I know that girl. She's a friend of mine."
Mari: "We~ll then, I'll treasure it, no matter what!"
Mayu: "Okay..."
*The light envelopes even me.*
*The warmth is like Mari's own body temperature.*
*In this way, Mari will probably go to heaven. I believe that Mari will definitely go on to a happier world.*
*Because Mari... is a kind girl.*
Himeno: "Do not toy... with me!"
Mayu: "Huh..."
Mari: "Kyaaaaaah!"
*As if an arrow had been shot through the room, all of the twinkling lights stop in sync with the voice.*
*As soon as I hear that voice, the light surrounding Mari disappears, and it is as if time has stopped, leaving me wide-eyed at the small voice that screams hard enough to burn itself out.*
Mayu: "Hi... me... no..."
*No, this shouldn't be. I deny the truth in front of me -- the owner of that voice.*
Himeno: "Don't go deciding it's all over on your own. Sakuragi... going and destroying this place because you say your objective is complete, how convenient."
Himeno: "I'll kill that brat. I'll kill anyone who saw what I did... Then I'll return to Manten Aquarium!"
*The wall of the Deep-sea Fish booth is thrown open, and inside I can faintly see the tunnel tank.*
*Himeno is standing there, lit from behind by the red light leaking out from the entrance. Her face is covered in a distinct shadow...*
*This isn't the appearance of the weakened Himeno, nor is it the Himeno I know.*
*Her breathing rough and her expression furious, she takes a stance like a model would.*
Mari: "No... why...?"
*Mari's gentle expression has changed to one of fear in an instant.*
*Everything is bathed in the red light, and my pulse quickens and grows stronger.*
Himeno: "My..."
*As she says this, the corners of Himeno's mouth bend. Even though I recognize that expression as a smile, it takes me some time.*
Himeno: "Fo~und you."
Mari: "Yo-You... You're that... Noooooooooooooo!"
*Letting out a sudden scream, Mari takes off running.*
*In order to reach the one and only exit in the basement, Mari uses her small body to slide past Himeno's thigh.*
Mayu: "Himeno, why...? Hey, answer me, Himeno!"
Himeno: "Why, my name isn't Himeno."
Mayu: "Huh... What do I do...? Isn't that... Could she be...?!"
*There's no mistaking that as Himeno's face and voice.*
*However, this isn't the first time I've heard the way Himeno's speaking right now. I remember it; it's someone I know.*
Mayu: "Saginuma... Rieko...!"
Himeno: "You have guts to address a lady older than you without respect."
Mayu: "So you were a spirit after all. Then how did you get Himeno's body... Through possession?"
Himeno: "Himeno... this vessel. This girl was shaken, and at the bottom of her heart, she bore resentment."
Mayu: "Resentment...?"
Himeno: "Yes. As to the one who left her all alone -- that would be you. Nakanobe Mayumi-san."
Mayu: "Himeno... resented..."
*Himeno gives a sly grin.*
*No, the one in front of me isn't Himeno, it isn't.*
*B-B-But--*
*But I came all the way here... for Himeno.*
Himeno: "I followed that brat to kill her, but I slipped from a machine and died, so I was unable to finish the job. If I can't get rid of my worries, I cannot rest easy. Sleep deprivation... is the enemy of a woman's complexion."
Mayu: "What...?! But you're already dead, aren't you?"
*If she weren't dead, she couldn't possess Himeno like this.*
Himeno: "It does not matter. I will use this girl's soul and, this time, create my version of Manten Aquarium. There, I will resume my peaceful existence."
Mayu: "If you're dragging Himeno into it... then it's all wrong, isn't it...?"
Himeno: "My soul is nothing more than a droplet within Manten Aquarium. Just as I am released, I am pulled back in. My soul alone can't create Manten Aquarium. I need something not linked to this place or the other souls -- so I'll use this girl's soul."
Mayu: "Then isn't that enough...? Mari is already--"
Himeno: "It does not matter. I will kill that child. If I don't, I cannot move forward."
Mayu: "There's nothing more for you... to move forward to."
*With a twisted expression, Himeno clicks her tongue with a "Tch."*
*No, that's not Himeno.*
*Himeno would never make that face.*
Himeno: "I'll go ahead and say this, but it's because this vessel has its own will that I am like this. The reason I hate you is because of a vector of malice from within."
Mayu: "That's... a lie...!"
Himeno: "Mayu, I hate you."
Mayu: "Wha--"
*Words said on the spur of the moment, words that don't even fill up a second.*
*Those words definitely originated from Himeno's mouth, in Himeno's voice, in the same way of speaking that Himeno uses.*
*Saginuma Rieko spins her body around, and with the sound of her heels going clack clack clack, leaves the Deep-sea Fish booth behind.*
*She begins walking into the Manten Aquarium that is now corroded with red.*
Mayu: "Stupid... Himeno, you idiot..."
*Feeling nauseated, I say this over and over.*
Mayu: "That's why I'll save you, no matter what!"
*I don't care if Himeno does hate me. Because I love her.*
*All throughout the red aquarium, the fish swim about.*
*Rushing out of the tanks that served as containers, they literally migrate into every inch of the area. It has become a big ocean, far greater than just the Deep-sea Fish booth.*
*The cutlassfish that was hopping around the tunnel tank is now just a school of fish swimming about.*
*Occasionally a manta ray passes at my feet, and a small shark swims overhead.*
*The fishes' understanding of this space is probably different from mine. The walls, floors, and ceilings are definitely there, but any concept of up or down, left or right is irrelevant to them. The two perceptions of the world are merging together.*
*Neither version of the world seems to be changing into the malicious aquarium that existed up until now.*
*All of it blends together, a world on the threshold. If what Saginuma Rieko said is true, at the moment Mari and I touched hands, the new Red Manten Aquarium was completed.*
Mayu: "I won't let you...!"
*This is something I definitely will not allow.*
*I can guess where she's heading. I double my speed and head for that place as well.*
*She said that she fell from a machine.*
*If there is a machine high enough to die from falling off of, there's no place that it could be except for the machine room. There was a ladder leading to it from the passageway where Hiyoshi-san went crazy.*
*So that's the way I head.*
*Weaving through the fish, I move along. I'm going to save the people who are important to me.*
Mayu: "... Let's go."
*There is a door combined with a panel on the wall just after exiting the tunnel tank. Upon closer inspection, the words "Staff Only" are written on it. I open it and see that there is a staff passageway that goes to the stage.*
*There are many hidden passageways besides this one in the aquarium. Before I realize it, I've become so knowledgeable about this place.*
*I have no time to get caught up in sentimentality. Stopping Saginuma Rieko takes priority.*
*On opening the door, cool air flows out.*
*Just to be safe, I look all around, but there's no sign of Hiyoshi-san.*
*The time axis may still be changing every time someone passes through a door, but I don't sense any strange phenomena in the dead silence of the passageway. so all that's left to do is to move on.*
Mayu: "Himeno!"
*Though I call out in a loud voice, I get no response.*
Mayu: "Himeno! Mari! Where are you?!"
*As I move further into the passageway, I call out several times. However, there is no response.*
*If so, then Saginuma Rieko has probably already climbed up.*
*I climb the ladder on the side of the passageway. As this passageway is essentially in a sub-basement, the very top is quite high up. I climb it in one go.*
*Looking out, I am on a walkway above the tunnel tank. From here, one could feed the fish in the tank or adjust the machines.*
*Being above the tunnel tank puts me about three stories high.*
*Looking up from below, it's hard to tell how high up this goes thanks to the water. Physically, I get the sense that that's how high I've climbed.*
*It's so deep I can't see the bottom. Even if I fell into the water from here, it would still be dangerous. With my clothes I'd easily sink to the bottom. And if there were no water -- just thinking about it is terrifying.*
*Right now, I can be certain that there is water filling it to the brim.*
*This thin walkway above the giant tank is a bridge about ten meters across. Even missing one's footing by one step could easily lead to them falling.*
*There seems to be a ladder on the other side as well. The metallic footsteps coming from over there hint at that fact.*
Mayu: "Over there are... Himeno, Mari...!"
*I've heard those footsteps before. They are Himeno's boots.*
Himeno: "My, you know this place well."
*From her head down, Himeno's body appears clearly.*
*Directly across from me on the walkway where I currently stand, Himeno... Saginuma Rieko is standing there. She's holding the captured Mari.*
Mayu: "Mari!"
Himeno: "Do not move. You know what will happen if you do, right?"
Mari: "Big sis... ter... I got caught... Ehehe..."
*Saginuma Rieko has Mari by the collar, tightly restraining her. It goes without saying that there is a major difference in stature between Mari and Himeno. She holds her as if she intends to inact violence on her captive.*
*Mari puts a smile on her pained face. The fact that she is forcing her smile is clear as day.*
Himeno: "The order in which one does things is important. After I kill this brat, I'll cast aside the flesh of this vessel and assimilate myself into the aquarium. Once my Manten Aquarium is complete, I'll curse you and end your life. So keep quiet and observe."
Mayu: "Stop calling Mari a brat already. And stop calling Himeno... a vessel."
Himeno: "Is there any meaning whatsoever in all of that? For me, the role one plays takes precedence. And in my world, I am at the center."
Mayu: "How awful... I can't... think of you as a human..."
*Mari alone is bad enough, but Himeno is being held hostage here as well.*
*If Himeno drowns, I can't save her.*
*How can I possibly... save both of them? I frantically search my thoughts.*
*Saginuma Rieko isn't taking her eyes off of me. At the same moment I become aware of her stare, she laughs boldly.*
Himeno: "Mayu, you're the one who's awful."
Mayu: "Wha--"
*With that brief phrase, she's halted my thoughts.*
*Just like the bizarre phenomenon at the Deep-sea Fish booth. Her voice and tone are unmistakably Himeno's.*
Himeno: "Leaving me in that terrifying place. It's your fault that I've ended up like this, Mayu!"
Himeno: "If you had just told me the truth about yourself, we wouldn't have gotten into a fight."
Himeno: "If you had just told me about things like your sister going missing, or that she was here, instead of deciding everything on your own, I wouldn't have ended up like this!"
Mayu: "Himeno, so that's how you saw it..."
*Even though Saginuma Rieko is possessing her, those words just now were none other than Himeno's.*
*I see Himeno as my best friend. Because of that, I know that those words are how she truly feels.*
Himeno: "You know, I thought of you as my best friend, Mayu. But that was just my own misconception. You didn't care what happened to me, did you, Mayu?!"
Mayu: "That's... not..."
*My words of denial come out shrouded in uncertainty.*
*Himeno's expression is a serious one. It's the face I saw when we fought, looking a little like she might cry, but very angry.*
*I came all this way for Himeno. Why are things getting so messed up here at the very end...?*
Mayu: "Himeno, I'm sorry..."
Mari: "Don't give up again, big sister!"
*As I start to look down, Mari's voice calls me back.*
Mayu: "Mari...?"
Mari: "When I've been in an argument, I've lied too! Even though I love you, I said I hated you. This lady is making the same face I made at those times!"
Himeno: "Didn't I tell you not to speak?"
*Saginuma Rieko squeezes the base of Mari's neck even tighter. Mari instantly shuts her eyes, then looks at me through thin slits.*
Mayu: "Mari!"
Mari: "I'm all right... big sister."
*With a pained expression, Mari continues to smile.*
Mari: "I... I love you big sister, just the way that you are!"
Himeno: "I told you to shut up, didn't I, you little brat?!"
Mari: "Bye-bye, big sister..."
*Just as she hinted at with her tone, Saginuma Rieko tosses Mari into the tank water.*
*Even at that last moment, Mari's face was smiling.*
Mayu: "Ma-Mari!"
*It's almost a reflex. I dive into the tank.*
Himeno: "Hahahahahahaha! Serves you right! I hate such juvenile ways of thinking!"
*Saginuma Rieko's shrill laughter resonates from overhead.*
*I swim after Mari as she sinks to the bottom of the tank. Swimming isn't exatly my forte, but no matter what, I want to save her.*
Himeno: "Die, die, dieeeeee! This way I'll finally be free! My soul will finally become one with this Manten Aquarium!"
*I swim deeper and deeper.*
*I notice that Mari's body is beginning to float up little by little.*
*The tanks here in the aquarium contain a great deal of salt. Much like seawater, the force with which something is thrown in disappears against the water resistance, causing it to float.*
*I will save Mari...!*
*I reach out my hand and grab Mari's.*
*I don't know if I actually touch Mari's hand, but I definitely have the sensation that I've grabbed something.*
*Just like that, the two of us float upward. At this speed, we should be able to keep breathing.*
Mayu: "Bwah."
Mari: "Urk, ack..."
Mayu: "This time, I was able to save you... Mari, I was able to save you."
Mari: "Thank you... big... sister."
*I don't think I can consider it saving her... if she's already dead.*
*This time, I was able to hold the hand of my one and only little sister, whom I was unable to save five years ago. The thoughts and feelings that have lingered for five years were finally able to get through.*
*I am unbelievably happy.*
Himeno: "My~ what a pity..."
Mayu: "Huh...?!"
*That transient joy is smashed away in the blink of an eye.*
*Saginuma Rieko has also leapt into the tank. Riding the force of her dive, she grabs hold of us and pulls us towards the bottom of the tank.*
Mayu: "Urk...!"
*Her strength is so great, it's impossible to think of it as human.*
*No matter how great Himeno's reflexes are, there should be no way for her to drag two people underwater.*
*This is... the manifestation of Saginuma Rieko's grudge.*
Himeno: "I thought I would properly curse you to death, but I've changed my mind. You can die here, with your best friend."
*I can hear Saginuma Rieko so clearly, her voice seems to be echoing right through my mind.*
*Despite the fact that we're underwater, it rattles my brain like the clanging of a bell.*
Himeno: "And become a piece of my grudge, giving shape to my Manten Aquarium! Ahahahahahahahahahahahaha!!!!"
Mari: "This old lady is so annoying."
Rieko: "What... did you say...?!"
*Mari's faint voice mixes with Saginuma Rieko's reverberating shrill laugh.*
Mari: "Big sister, I'm so glad, you saved me in the end."
Mayu: "----!"
*Mari is enveloped by a white light spreading out from her chest.*
*The light gradually grows larger, quickly spreading throughout the entire tank.*
Mari: "Take good care of your friend!"
*The light mixes with the water, becoming a faint blue light.*
*Within it, numerous fish swim about.*
Himeno: "What is this light...?! This brat shouldn't be able to..."
Mari: "All of them hate you, lady!"
Himeno: "What are you trying to do...? I'm only trying to get my tranquility... No, stop..."
*Even within the brilliant light, I can still clearly see what's going on.*
*Several fish are opening and closing their mouths, pulling on Himeno's body.*
*No. They're pulling something out of Himeno's body.*
Himeno: "Stop... stop this...!"
*Himeno's body is becoming double-layered.*
*One is Himeno, the other is... Saginuma Rieko's body. Saginuma Rieko's body -- her soul is, little by little, being torn away from Himeno's body by the fish.*
Himeno: "You all, sto... stop thi--"
Mari: "We won't stop. This time, it's my turn to save my big sister!"
*Oddly, even though I'm underwater, I don't feel as if I'm suffocating.*
*There have been various strange phenomena within Manten Aquarium. I have seen many odd things.*
*However, the sight of the fishes' counterattack unfolding before my eyes is by far the most fierce. They are filled with the energy of lives just freed from so much hatred and resentment.*
Himeno: "I'll curse you. Even if I die, I'll curse youuuuuuuu!!!!"
Mari: "Impossible. The fish here are going to eat every last bit of your soul!"
*... What on earth is Mari--?*
Mari: "I don't get it, but this is what I decided to do. So I've done it!"
Himeno: "Could it be that you've become one with the aquarium before I could?! Don't toy with me, stop thissssssssss!"
*Saginuma Rieko's body has already become completely detached from Himeno's. With a look of anguish on her face, her body is being picked at by the fish.*
*The contours of her flesh are gradually becoming distorted as Saginuma Rieko sinks away.*
*In contrast, I am floating toward the top of the tank. It isn't because I am swimming nor because of buoyancy.*
*Orca, sea lions, and various other sea mammals are pushing Himeno and I up.*
Himeno: "You littleeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeeee!!!"
Mari: "This is all that I can do."
*From within the light, I hear Mari's voice.*
*Mari's body is no longer next to us.*
Mari: "So hang in there just a little longer, big sister."
*The past. It's what created the current me. All of the things accumulated from the past, that's what I used to think made me who I am.*
*But Mari's radiance taught me something.*
*I... We... are all surrounded by the possibilities of the future. The past isn't everything I am. It's something that remains in my heart to push me further along.*
*... Big sister, see you later.*
*... Yes. Definitely. We'll meet again, Mari...!*
*This is my farewell.*
*As the light fades, my breathing begins to steady.*
*As for how much time has passed, it hasn't been more than a matter of minutes. I had lost consciousness.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*I've returned to the walkway on top of the tunnel tank.*
*Next to me, Himeno is sleeping.*
Mayu: "He~y... Himeno...?"
Himeno: "........."
*Even as I call out to Himeno, there is no response.*
Mayu: "Huh, wait... could she..."
*I begin to feel apprehensive. It isn't impossible.*
*We were in the water for a very long time. The amount of endurance it took was well beyond the norm.*
*On top of that, she was possessed by a spirit. I can't even imagine how much stamina that would drain away.*
Mayu: "It can't be, it can't be...!"
*Trying not to think the worst, I rest my ear on Himeno's chest. Pressing into the soft feeling of her chest, I concentrate very hard.*
*Ba-da-dum... ba-da-dum...*
*I can hear the sound of her heartbeat, thumping in a slow but steady rhythm.*
Mayu: "I'm... so glad..."
*She's probably just sleeping now, but Himeno is alive.*
*The colony of parasites that were growing within Himeno back at the stage seem to be completely gone now.*
*Their attack on Himeno must have also been a part of the grudge of Manten Aquarium.*
Mayu: "I was able to... save you. I really was... able to save you!"
*A great joy gradually fills my chest.*
*Upon hearing Himeno's pulse, my body goes limp.*
Himeno: "Ta-da, I was actually awake~!"
Mayu: "Hyaaa!"
*Sitting up with great vigor, Himeno spreads both arms wide.*
*Surprised, I lose my balance, and start to fall from the walkway.*
Himeno: "Ah, watch out!"
*Himeno embraces me, pulling me close to her.*
*Realizing I won't fall, I embrace her back.*
Mayu: "Geez... I was so worried... Himeno..."
Himeno: "Ahaha~ I'm a bit embarrassed, but thanks!"
*Himeno breaks away from me and suddenly stands up.*
*I'm so glad, it's not Saginuma Rieko, but the cheerful-as-always Himeno...!*
*Finally feeling a real sense of relief, my tears gradually begin to flow.*
Himeno: "H-Hey! Why are you crying, Mayu?"
Mayu: "Because, you..."
*The thread of tension that was pulled so tight begins to loosen at once.*
*Too much has happened in this Manten Aquarium. Still, somehow I was able to overcome it all. I was saved by so many people.*
*All of it was for this very moment.*
Himeno: "We~ll... where are we?"
Mayu: "Uh... huh?"
*In other words, Himeno still has almost no idea what happened here.*
*The one who was talking to me was Saginuma Rieko to the end. It wasn't Himeno.*
Mayu: "Well... uh... where should I start...?"
Himeno: "Well... how about from the beginning?"
*Just as Himeno tilts her head and looks at me once more, the walkway suddenly begins to shake.*
Mayu: "An earthquake?!"
*Leaving us no time to be surprised, the shaking grows more intense. Unlike the poltergeist phenomena in the director's office, the entire room is shaking.*
*But, this doesn't seem to be a natural phenomena. I grab Himeno's hand and motion for her to climb down the ladder.*
Mayu: "No... It's surely begun to crumble."
Himeno: "C-Crumble?"
*I forcefully bring Himeno, who looks like she wants to tell me she doesn't understand this at all, along with me.*
*The ending has begun for everything that this Red Manten Aquarium held.*
*Despite the fact that we may not understand everything about the grudge or the souls, this space is about to be destroyed.*
Mayu: "We should... head for the exit."
*At this point, I can't think of anything else.*
*The law of Manten Aquarium is that everytime one passes through a door, the time axis changes. The biggest boundary here would definitely be the entrance and exit gate.*
Himeno: "I don't really get it, but I guess at any rate we have to hurry, right?"
Mayu: "Yeah... let's go!"
Himeno: "Mayu, you're pretty reliable."
Mayu: "What are you saying, geez. Let's hurry!"
*Quickly climbing down the ladder, we rush through the staff passageway.*
*Meanwhile, the ground continues to tremble, causing us to stumble as we move on.*
*As we come out of the staff passageway, we are taken aback.*
*The booths in Manten Aquarium themselves look like they've been completely eaten up by bugs and worms.*
*The tunnel tank is cracked throughout and numerous holes are opening up in the floor.*
Himeno: "Uhh... what's going on?"
Mayu: "I think it'd be pretty bad if you fell in one of the holes. Be careful, let's go."
Himeno: "Yeah, okay~!"
*Given the circumstances, our steps speed up.*
*Before I know it, we've reached the Tokyo Bay Fish booth, the booth closest to the exit.*
*Passing through the place we came to first, the gate is right before our eyes.*
*We can see the elevator and reception area on the other side of the gate. However, there's nothing else there.*
*A strange mist hangs in the spaces between the numerous automatic turnstiles. From all throughout the mist comes a bright light.*
*... It's all right, I'm sure this is it.*
*My instinct tells me so.*
*When I realize I'm standing in front of the gate with Himeno, I am deeply moved. I hear the sound of teardrops falling.*
Mayu: "Finally, we made it here."
*Himeno probably just tilts her heads at my brooding words. It's been such a long journey.*
Mayu: "Okay, let's go home... Himeno?"
*I take Himeno's hand and ask quietly, but she suddenly looks down.*
Himeno: "You know, Mayu..."
*Her voice is quiet.*
*As Himeno says my name, her voice has taken on a serious air, one different from when we fought or from our fun chats at lunchtime.*
Mayu: "What's wrong, Himeno?"
Himeno: "I actually do... have memories of when Saginuma Rieko took over my body."
Mayu: "Could they... be from that time?"
*That time. The time when Himeno told me what was in her heart.*
*Himeno told me straight out her true feelings, her dissatisfaction, without hiding anything.*
Himeno: "Yeah. Mayu, you probably... thought that was a trick by Saginuma-san to confuse you, but those were my true feelings."
Himeno: "Without any lie, those were the feelings inside of me, so they came out of my own mouth."
Mayu: "I see. Yeah, your true feelings."
Himeno: "That's right. I... at one time, I hated you! Even though I don't feel that way at all now, I love you... I did feel that way, that I detested you..."
Himeno: "Since you risked your life to save me, Mayu... I can't seriously lie to you! I can't keep hiding that from you!"
Mayu: "Himeno..."
Himeno: "That's why I can't go with you! Mayu... I'm sorry, but I..."
*Still looking down, Himeno lets go of my hand.*
*As the rumbling in the ground continues, the wearing away of Manten Aquarium steadily gets worse.*
*Clearly, it's a sign that before long, this Manten Aquarium will disappear.*
*That thought passes through the deepest part of my heart.*
Mayu: "It could be that, I also--"
Himeno: "Mayu..."
Mayu: "In the depths of my heart, I may also bear some unease and dissatisfaction toward you, Himeno."
*Even for me, it's a surprisingly cool tone I use. Himeno nods and bites her lip.*
Mayu: "I kind of think that... the desire to save you that I got caught up in probably came from my sense of duty and responsibility."
Himeno: "Yeah, that's true... I guess. It's just that I said something so terrible, and thought to myself that I didn't like you, the one who tried to save me with everything she had, so thinking I can go home with you is strange."
Mayu: "Himeno, I didn't say anything like that..."
Himeno: "No, it's all right. If that's how you truly feel, it wouldn't be right for me not to accept it."
*Himeno quickly pulls her hand back.*
*Then she takes a step back from the ticket gate.*
Himeno: "I'll stay here..."
Mayu: "Huh...?"
*Without giving me a chance to respond, Himeno is swallowed up in a black hole as it expands in time with a tremor.*
*Due to the crumbling of Manten Aquarium, this whole world is eroding.*
*Himeno's body disappears into a hole the same size as the others throughout Manten Aquarium.*
Mayu: "No way..."
*The destruction is swallowing up everything, right up to my feet. A floorboard knocks me into the air, launching me past the ticket gate.*
*A week after returning to the safety of our original world, I visit Manten Aquarium.*
*So pretty...*
*I enter the jellyfish booth and admire the expansive cosmos without a second thought.*
*Himeno is not at my side.*
*It seems that Himeno's parents have filed a missing person's report since she hasn't come home at all.*
*However, the police investigation has been fruitless and Himeno has not been found.*
*Of course not. She was swallowed up in all that destruction.*
*And so, half a year has passed since that day.*
*Soon, I'll move up a grade.*
*I visited Manten Aquarium again. Everytime I go, there's no strange occurrences, just an aquarium overflowing with smiles and emotion.*
*One by one, I carefully examine each exhibit.*
*The impatient Himeno would always prod me along with "Hurry, hurry!"*
*But now that Himeno is no longer with me.*
*No matter how fascinating the aquarium it is, it is never enough.*
*It isn't just my enjoyment of the aquarium.*
*If I think back on it now, ever since that day, my daily life has been gray. Having lost the light that was Himeno, I don't even know how to have fun anymore.*
Mayu: "Haha..."
*Having returned home, I laugh emptily.*
*I'm sitting on my bed, holding my knees, not doing anything in particular, just spacing out.*
*Before I know it, I've fallen asleep and the morning of the next day has come. Lying down and getting back up is hard, and every day is depressing.*
*But it can't be helped.*
*This is the result of my choice, and of Himeno's resolve to stay back that day.*
Mayu: "Just a bit."
*Maybe it's selfish to feel lonely--*
*I hear the sound of Himeno weeping. It's rare for her to cry. Ah, but when we see touching movies, she always cries.*
*I wonder, why is Himeno crying with such a brooding face?*
*I don't understand the reason.*
Mayu: "Himeno, you're such a fool..."
Himeno: "Huh..."
Mayu: "We're... best friends!"
*The parts that I like, the parts I don't, friends accept all of it.*
*I tightly grab the hand that she has pulled away.*
Himeno: "Mayu...!"
*Himeno lifts her head, tears accumulating in her eyes, and rushes over to me.*
*The two of us, together, pass through the gate.*
Hiyoshi: "Kick it in!"
*The "it" Kenji-san is referring to is the metal grate serving as an exit.*
*Twisting my body inside the narrow passageway, I kick downward with all my might as he told me to. With a loud clang, it falls to the ground inside the Fish of the World booth.*
Rieko: "What?"
*I can immediately see Saginuma Rieko. Shaken by the wire mesh that just fell, she takes a step back from the girl.*
*I jump down, following the momentum of my kick. It's far from a clean landing, but at least I don't hurt my leg. As I straighten myself up, Kenji-san follows behind me.*
*As she becomes aware of a third intruder, Saginuma Rieko takes several more steps, putting distance between us.*
*Ignoring her, I rush to the girl's side.*
Mayu: "Are you okay?"
*The girl is lying down with her eyes closed, having lost a great deal of blood. Those blank eyes I saw from the other side of the acrylic window are now shut.*
*I place my hand on her chest, trying to find her pulse.*
Mayu: "I can... hear it...!"
*The sound itself seems to be very quiet. Still, within her soft breast, the resonating vibration is definitely reaching the palm of my hand.*
Sayo: "...... Tch."
*As if in response to my voice, I can hear the girl click her tongue in annoyance.*
*She doesn't seem to have regained consciousness yet, but there is no mistake that she is showing her emotions.*
Rieko: "You......"
*Even though I know she's alive now, I still can't lose focus.*
*I listen carefully to the conversation Saginuma Rieko and Kenji-san hold as they stand facing each other.*
Rieko: "Why are you here...?"
Hiyoshi: "Why are you here?!"
*Their words cut each other off, leaving only silence between the two of them.*
Mayu: "Do you know each other?"
*Neither will answer my question.*
*Kenji-san shoves Saginuma Rieko away.*
*With his thick arms and large body, it takes little effort for him to throw her into the wall.*
Hiyoshi: "Because you're here!"
*Before she can straighten herself up, he grabs her by the collar and thrusts her into the wall with a great deal of force.*
*Having slammed the back of her head against the wall, Saginuma Rieko begins to bleed, leaving traces of blood on the wall that look as if someone dipped a cloth in red paint and scrubbed the wall with it.*
*This is quite different from how it was when Saginuma Rieko was trying to kill the girl.*
Mayu: "Kenji-san! Please help me... treat this girl for now!"
*He is most likely trying to protect us. Who knows when Saginuma Rieko, who sought to kill the girl without hesitation, will target me.*
*Honestly, I am afraid of her. I have a terrible gut-feeling about her.*
*Still, in this moment, Kenji-san seems to be acting completely out of rage. That is far more terrifying to me.*
Hiyoshi: "Yeah, you're right, Mayu-chan. "
*Kenji-san slowly straightens his hunched over body and turns back to me.*
Rieko: "Guh..."
*Applying pressure to her head, Saginuma Rieko crawls along the floor, moving away from us.*
*She seems to be quite shaken. I suppose that's not unreasonable.*
*There's no way I can sympathize with her, but at the same time, I don't want it to go any further.*
Mayu: "What is that...?"
*As I watch Saginuma Rieko retreat, I sense that bad feeling once more.*
*I quickly recognize what it is.*
*A red, no... an almost black drop of light leaks from Saginuma Rieko's body. It doesn't quite act like blood.*
*Drops are leaking from the wound on the back of her head, as well as from the skin on her elbows and knees, worn away from crawling on the ground.*
*The drop of ominous light hovers before being absorbed by the aquarium.*
*I feel as if I'm looking at something I shouldn't be.*
*And that something that shouldn't be happening, is.*
*But it's the fact that she appears to be laughing when she leaves the room that bothers me more than anything else.*
Mayu: "I can't get distracted. Right now... I have to save this girl."
*Giving this thought voice returns my focus.*
*Even though I said we have to save her, I have no idea what to do at a time like this.*
Hiyoshi: "At any rate, we have to stop the bleeding."
*Kenji-san takes a facial tissue from his pocket and wipes away some of the blood. He then gently lifts her head and applies pressure to the wound with the tissue. He is pressing hard on her forehead.*
*As the blood begins to dry, the original color is indistinguishable as it has become completely red.*
*Having roughly wiped away some of the blood, he removes his outerwear that has blood on it. He folds the parka he removed in order to hide the blood and lays it on the ground like a pillow.*
*His golden ring looks pretty with his black tank top.*
Hiyoshi: "There's nothing we can do but wait for now. Her wounds are pretty severe, but even with my own limited emergency treatment, it seems we made it in time, so I'm glad."
Mayu: "Will she be okay?"
Hiyoshi: "She took a really intense blow to the head, so she'll probably be in pain for awhile. Her breathing has stabilized, though, so she should be fine."
Mayu: "Thank you so much, Kenji-san."
*Upon hearing his words, a sigh of relief slips from my lips.*
Hiyoshi: "It's no big deal. More importantly, Mayu-chan, you're calling me by my name now."
Mayu: "P-Please think nothing of it!"
*When he went crazy, I called him by name while he was out of it. I thought that "Kenji-san" would get through to him more than "Hiyoshi-san" would.*
*But that change had been practically subconscious.*
*That's why having it pointed out to me is so embarrassing.*
Hiyoshi: "Ah, Mayu-chan, it seems like we've gotten a bit closer."
*He's smiling at me as he says this.*
*In order to hide my embarrassment, I puff up my cheeks and have no choice but to pretend to be mad.*
Mayu: "You're terrible, Hiyoshi-san!"
Hiyoshi: "Hey, go back. I'll stop teasing you, so bring back that good mood."
Mayu: "... It's not like I'm really upset or anything, though."
*Not only did he save me, he saved the girl too.*
*If this were a movie, he would unmistakably be in the position of the hero.*
*Just as I'm thinking I want to hurry up and get off this topic already, it's Kenji-san who changes the subject.*
Mayu: "That's because... I thought it was best."
*When he went crazy, I called him by name while he was out of it. I thought that "Kenji-san" would get through to him more than "Hiyoshi-san" would.*
*It was a subconscious change, but the desire I have to call him by name has not changed.*
Mayu: "I respect you, Kenji-san."
*Not only did he save me, he saved the girl too.*
*Rather than the version of him that changed so quickly, this trustworthy side of him leaves a much stronger impression.*
*Since I rarely talk to adult males, or males at all, I'm not very good at it, but he seems to be strong and kind.*
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, is that a confession?"
Mayu: "Wha... What?!"
*Without recognizing Kenji-san's surprise attack, I turn my face away.*
Hiyoshi: "So how about it? Come on, come on."
Mayu: "I wasn't thinking about anything like that!"
Hiyoshi: "That's so like you, isn't it, Mayu-chan...?"
*Just as I'm thinking I want to hurry up and get off this topic already, it's Kenji-san who changes the subject.*
Hiyoshi: "So, Mayu-chan. This may not be the best time, but who is this girl?"
*Kenji-san asks as he turns his face in the direction of the girl.*
*The girl's eyes are closed, but her soft breathing continues.*
*Her long hair is tied back, and her clothes are more punk than they are high fashion. She looks like she would prefer a live music club over an aquarium.*
*Her round eyes and soft outline somehow remind me of Mari's appearance.*
Mayu: "Actually, I don't know either. I met her after I came to this twisted version of Manten Aquarium."
*I pause for a moment to remember exactly how I met her, then continue speaking.*
*Despite the fact that it hasn't been more than half a day since our meeting, this girl and I have struggled for our lives together.*
Mayu: "She spoke pretty harshly to me when I saw Himeno, whose body was under attack from parasites."
Mayu: "After that, I saved her, and she saved me. Together, we researched the secrets of this 'Manten Aquarium,' including the fact that the director here was the victim of a murder."
Hiyoshi: "So then, you two know about the incident."
*Ignoring Kenji-san's suggestive tone, I continue talking about the girl.*
Mayu: "She wasn't planning to be with me to the end. She steeled herself, saying, 'If I can learn the truth, then it's okay if I die,' and resolved herself to end it on her own."
