Mayu: "Just as I thought that we had reached an understanding, I was wrong. Surely she felt she couldn't rely on me... If she hadn't called out to me, I wouldn't have risen up to save my best friend."
Hiyoshi: "Then this girl wanted to be by herself from the start."
*I hesitate for a moment, then nod.*
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, before you met up with me, you really experienced so much more than I could have imagined. You've come so very close to the truth. Your eyes have taken on such a strong color."
Mayu: "So you did know something after all, Kenji-san..."
Hiyoshi: "I didn't want to scare you all by saying more than necessary. However, it seems I don't have to sugar-coat things anymore. Please tell me about earlier. Your friend, Mayu-chan... What condition is Himeno-chan in right now?"
Mayu: "Himeno's... entire body is currently being invaded by parasites. According to what Saginuma Rieko said, that breed doesn't normally feed off of their host, but they seem to be attacking Himeno."
Mayu: "But this girl said that if I can get Himeno and I out of Manten Aquarium, I might be able to save her because the threat of the parasites only persists while we're inside Manten Aquarium..."
Hiyoshi: "Yeah, I get it. This girl's pretty sharp. That story's half right, half wrong."
Mayu: "Half? What do you mean by that?"
Hiyoshi: "If you escape this 'Manten Aquarium,' the threat of the parasites will disappear, but the curse itself will remain. Little by little, the life energy will be drained from her body and she will weaken... until she succumbs and dies."
Mayu: "Curse? Dies? So I can't save Himeno?!"
*I couldn't stop my voice from getting louder.*
*Kenji-san doesn't make an angry face, but he does answer in a stern tone.*
Hiyoshi: "Calm down and let me finish. This is important, got it?"
Mayu: "I... understand."
*I hold back my overexcitement as I wait for Kenji-san to continue speaking.*
Hiyoshi: "This aquarium is a different dimension, created from the grudge of the murdered director and the numerous fish culled and killed here."
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, you probably remember hearing of it as a parallel world, but it's another world created from negative emotions. It's a world created within the framework of Manten Aquarium."
Hiyoshi: "Grudges and energy from souls, those are occult terms, right? There is definitely some power at work we cannot see."
Hiyoshi: "Even now, science cannot prove exactly how our emotions are created, but the power from that is surely involved in the creation of this Manten Aquarium."
Hiyoshi: "I've decided to call it the... 'Red Manten Aquarium.'"
Hiyoshi: "You and I, Himeno and that girl there, all of us who have come here, are cursed. More accurately, we were lured here by wandering ghosts."
Hiyoshi: "When the Red Manten Aquarium was created, it wasn't by just the murdered fish, but the souls of everyone who died here..."
Mayu: "People who... died here."
*In other words, I could have become one of them.*
*I have been in danger of it so many times. Even now, the possibility is still very real.*
*If I hadn't made it to this point, I might not have believed Hiyoshi-san's story.*
*To borrow his words... having reunited in the Red Manten Aquarium, I could have seen it as a joke at first and lost my life without realizing how serious things really were.*
Hiyoshi: "Something is luring us here. It may only be one piece of a bigger grudge, but for now, let's refer to them as vengeful spirits."
Hiyoshi: "In order to maintain their existence, they need energy. Here, within the Red Manten Aquarium, they are constantly trying to supplement that energy."
Hiyoshi: "But what if we escape? They'll lose their energy supply. If that were to happen, then the life force of a living human... in other words, the soul, would become their energy. What remains in our bodies would only last about a month."
Mayu: "For example, even if we were able to escape, it wouldn't just be Himeno, but all of us who would die within a month... and it's not just that, is it? Whatever it is might contradict everything you've said, Kenji-san."
Mayu: "Could you mean that before our life energy is gone, we have to come back to the Red Manten Aquarium? So that the things possessing us can take some more energy?"
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, you're clever."
*Kenji-san says this with admiration, but he doesn't sound the least bit happy.*
Hiyoshi: "Anyway, you're right. Mayu-chan, you know when it is we need to return, right?"
Mayu: "The day of... the full moon... So the rumor Himeno heard wasn't necessarily mistaken. On top of that, the cycle of the moon lasts for about 28 days. So by coming to the Red Manten Aquarium on the day of a full moon, we won't be killed by the curse."
Hiyoshi: "When there's a full or new moon, the earth, moon, and sun are in conjunction in a straight line. At that time, the moon's pull is at its strongest. You've heard that this is when the ebb and flow of the tides is greatest, right?"
Mayu: "... Yes."
Hiyoshi: "Moreover, there are legends that say the moon gives an illuminating energy to things which normally go unseen."
Hiyoshi: "Whether this is to be believed, or the more scientifically sound idea of the moon's effect on magnetic pull, the point does stand that the path to the Red Manten Aquarium is inaccessible on the day of a new moon."
*What Hiyoshi-san is saying has plenty of guesswork to it, but it's enough to satisfy me.*
*However, it's because of this satisfied feeling that more questions begin to rise.*
Mayu: "If we can reach the Red Manten Aquarium on the day of a full moon, wouldn't more people have reached it as well? Yet that isn't the case, right?"
Hiyoshi: "The difference is probably related to fate. People who are tied to Manten Aquarium, or to the victims of the Red Manten Aquarium, are lured in as if they are guided by the moon's strengthening pull."
*I'm connected to Mari. The girl is connected to the director -- her father. Himeno's connection is surely to someone pulled into the Red Manten Aquarium, me.*
*If I think back on it, everyone has their own ties to Manten Aquarium. Hiyoshi-san also mentioned an old acquaintance he hadn't seen in some time.*
*Hiyoshi-san and who? I almost ask, but stop the words before they come out. Back when he went crazy, he said a girl's name. Maybe it's better for me not to know... and I get the feeling that I don't want to know, either.*
Mayu: "Kenji-san, how do you know all of this?"
Hiyoshi: "My experience and my deductions. Also filling in gaps with the fragments of information you girls have given me, Mayu-chan."
*Kenji-san heaves a small sigh, then continues.*
Hiyoshi: "Well your guesses so far have been on point, though I'm not just trying to maintain my life energy, but find a way to break the curse. That's why I've come back to the Red Manten Aquarium over and over again."
Hiyoshi: "This time I had no luck either... but in order to have a next time, we'll need to get Himeno-chan and escape quickly from the Red Manten Aquarium."
Hiyoshi: "And one more thing that you should know, Mayu-chan. You should know the truth of the murder that took place in Manten Aquarium. The criminal who killed the director was... Saginuma Rieko."
Mayu: "Huh..."
Hiyoshi: "That girl... she's probably the director's daughter. Her primary motive was probably to learn the truth. It was most likely because she found the truth that Saginuma Rieko tried to kill her."
*Putting it like that, perhaps there could be no greater motive for her almost being killed.*
*Erasing her from this world. The ultimate method of shutting her up. On top of that, killing her inside of the Red Manten Aquarium would mean no one would see it and no evidence would be left.*
Mayu: "If she really is trying to kill that girl... that's completely unforgivable!"
*My anger is greater than my shock.*
*A human killing even one person is a sin that cannot be atoned for. Piling on other sins to conceal that first sin... Repeating such a cycle ensures such a person has no future.*
*I turn to Kenji-san and speak with a firm tone. Compared to the cool way he told me all of these facts, I probably come off as angry.*
Mayu: "I'm so sorry. I yelled even though you didn't do anything wrong, Kenji-san..."
*After I say this, I realize that he probably purposely withheld any expression of emotion while he was telling me the facts. That's the difference between an adult and a child...*
*Remembering the way Kenji-san looked when he shoved Rieko-san, I empathize with how enraged he must have been feeling.*
Hiyoshi: "It's fine. From this point on, we'll have to be calm and make our decisions based on facts, so it's better to vent your anger now."
*Kenji-san says this with a stern expression.*
*I understand right away that what he is saying is important, so I wait for him to continue.*
Hiyoshi: "We should... leave this girl here."
*That was not what I expected him to say.*
Mayu: "Huh?! But that's--! Even if you seem to have done what you could for her, her injuries still haven't healed. If we leave her... what will happen to her...?"
Hiyoshi: "Well, we went to all that trouble to rescue her."
Mayu: "If that's how you feel, then why...?"
Hiyoshi: "Try to look at this from the other way around. At the very least, she's received first aid."
Mayu: "I don't get it at all! If we saved her, then we should at least..."
Hiyoshi: "There are two reasons. After hearing what you told me, would Himeno-chan really be able to walk on her own?"
Hiyoshi: "With your physique, it would be impossible for you to take Himeno along and escape. You would definitely run into danger. The only one who would be able to run or jump with Himeno on their back is me."
Mayu: "What if I had this girl ride piggyback on me..."
Hiyoshi: "It's the same thing. Surely... For example, if we were chased by something, you wouldn't be able to run, Mayu-chan."
*He says all of this so curtly, I can't really argue with him.*
Hiyoshi: "There's one more reason."
Hiyoshi: "Do you think this girl really wants to run away from the aquarium?"
Mayu: "That's..."
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, how much do you know about her? She's okay with dying if it means she can ascertain the truth... That kind of resolve isn't forged in a day. Surely this girl has been searching a long time for the identity of her father's killer."
Hiyoshi: "She's finally found it. She might have been knocked down once, but I'm sure that she wants to face the killer once more."
Hiyoshi: "I don't know whether she wants some sort of remorse, or some sort of public punishment, but having finally reached the truth, I doubt she wants to run away."
Hiyoshi: "If she really had decided to go it alone, she probably wouldn't have even accepted the first aid we did provide. In this girl's eyes, we've probably done more than enough."
*Kenji-san's tone of voice is steadily becoming more forceful.*
*So, we've finally reached the heart of the matter.*
Hiyoshi: "It's time for you to make your decision. I won't force you. Do you want to continue?"
Mayu: "Yes."
Hiyoshi: "Then let's go together. We'll leave this girl here, go back to where Himeno-chan is, and the three of us... myself, you, and Himeno-chan, will escape together."
Hiyoshi: "One month from now, the three of us will have to return here, but I've figured out how to get back in. I think it's a pretty reliable method."
Hiyoshi: "If the day of the full moon passes, in other words, when this day ends, the door to the real world won't open again until the next full moon. We don't know how much longer Himeno-chan can last, so I think we should get out of here as quickly as possible."
Mayu: "So basically... there's no time to think it over."
*Kenji-san nods silently.*
*After a brief pause, he continues.*
Hiyoshi: "Don't think of it as abandoning her, but decide based on what is most important to you."
*In other words, two choices have been presented to me.*
Mayu: "I'll go with you, Kenji-san, to save Himeno. The reason I was able to make it this far was because the thought of saving her kept me going."
Mayu: "That's what I want to believe in."
Hiyoshi: "I understand. As things stand right now, I thought that's what you'd say. I can understand... you have a strong resolve to save Himeno-chan."
Mayu: "Yes, I've made my decision and don't want to face any further doubts. I'd like us to go, but... please wait for just a moment."
*As I say this to Kenji-san, I squat down next to the girl on the ground.*
*I take out the small keychain from my skirt pocket. The keychain with the starfish droplet.*
*Mari and I, Himeno and I... and surely this girl and her father. This is the shape these bonds take on.*
*I think rather than me holding on to it, this girl... should have it back.*
*I close the girl's hand around the keychain and clasp both of my hands together.*
Mayu: "If I can, I'll definitely--"
*Wishing with all my might, I stand up.*
Mayu: "Sorry for keeping you waiting."
Hiyoshi: "You okay with this?"
Mayu: "The one who said we have no time was you, Kenji-san!"
Hiyoshi: "... I guess I did. Where's Himeno-chan?"
Mayu: "At the stage. Inside a shed on the stage."
Hiyoshi: "From here... it'll be faster to go through the interior. We can pray that nothing happens to us on the way, but just in case, you should prepare yourself."
Mayu: "Kenji-san, do you know the law governing the fixation of points here?"
Hiyoshi: "...... What's that?"
Mayu: "It's a law that girl and I discovered. If we use it right, we should be able to safely reach Himeno and escape the Red Manten Aquarium..."
*As we walk, I tell Hiyoshi-san about the doors and the phenomena.*
*It seems he hasn't learned the law needed to rebel against Manten Aquarium. He listens intently to my story with a serious expression.*
*We leave the Fish of the World booth behind. The moment of upheaval behind us, I can definitely see that our fate lies directly ahead.*
Mayu: "I'm going to stay here with this girl. She said she was fine by herself, but I want to be her friend."
Hiyoshi: "You just met her today, and you don't even know her name, right?"
Mayu: "Kenji-san, didn't I just meet you today as well? Even so, you saved me... I think it's the same feeling I'm having now."
Hiyoshi: "... It's not."
*Kenji-san murmurs this and immediately continues.*
Hiyoshi: "I get it. Mayu-chan, if your determination is true, then I won't say anything further."
Mayu: "Thank you very much."
Hiyoshi: "You can go ahead and think of me as cruel, but I also..."
Mayu: "There's no need to say anything else. You've come here numerous times just to stay alive, Kenji-san. I can't allow your efforts to be for nothing just because you were looking after me."
Hiyoshi: "Please at least let me tell you my selfish hope."
Mayu: "Hope?"
Hiyoshi: "That the three of us absolutely will escape together. I'm definitely coming next month too. This won't be the end of our relationship. I just went a different route this time. If ever you need me... don't be afraid to rely on me."
Mayu: "Kenji-san, you really are kind after all. I'm all right. I'm not staying behind to die. I'm staying behind... to save her."
Hiyoshi: "Don't lose that resolve."
Mayu: "I definitely won't."
*And so Kenji-san puts the Fish of the World booth behind him. Without looking back, he heads for the exit at a quick pace.*
*I think his lack of attention is Kenji-san's own way of showing his concern.*
Mayu: "Wait for me, Himeno, Mari..."
*I clasp the hand of the girl on the ground. The moment of upheaval behind us has been clearly separated from the destiny that lies ahead.*
Mayu: "Himeno!"
Hiyoshi: "Hey, Mayu-chan!"
*I cry out Himeno's name just as we reach the stage.*
Hiyoshi: "Since the rule of fixed coordinates exists, does that mean we're safe... for now?"
*I run quickly into the shed.*
*Himeno is there, unchanged from the last time I saw her.*
Himeno: "Ma... yu... Urk."
*When she gets choked up, Himeno coughs up parasites.*
*The number of parasites wiggling on the wet floor is even greater than what I saw a few hours ago.*
Mayu: "You don't... need to say anything. I-I'm here to save you, Himeno!"
*Himeno's deterioration has gotten worse.*
*Her eyes are hollow; you could probably say her recognizing me is a miracle.*
*If we had been an hour later... would what have happened been...?*
*It's scary just thinking about it.*
Himeno: "Th... ank... you..."
*Himeno hangs her head, her breathing ragged.*
Mayu: "Let's go. Everything's going to be all right."
*If I touch Himeno, the parasites will probably try to attack me as well.*
*Even so, if we're going to escape, I can't run away from the parasites. Though it's not the best solution, I have to choose what's more important.*
*As I try to lend Himeno my shoulder, Kenji-san intervenes.*
Hiyoshi: "I told you, didn't I? If you don't let me carry Himeno-chan, you won't be able to react if something does happen."
*Kenji-san removes Himeno from the chair and lifts her onto his back. His sense of stability makes me believe that, as he's so easily able to carry her, he could run with her much easier than I could.*
Hiyoshi: "Isn't she a bit heavy? Himeno-chan, you probably need to go on a diet."
Mayu: "She's gonna get mad at you, geez!"
*My heart lightens up a bit as we joke back and forth.*
*I feel a tightness in my chest as I worry over Himeno's condition.*
Mayu: "But... you seem pretty reliable."
Hiyoshi: "Tell me that again later, after we've escaped."
*Kenji-san smiles at me, and we leave the shed. I follow behind him.*
Hiyoshi: "First of all, there's no need to worry. Let's stay calm and get out of here."
*Following what he says, I run after him.*
Hiyoshi: "Somehow, we were able to make it back here again."
Mayu: "Kenji-san..."
*Having come to just in front of the ticket gate at the entrance, Kenji-san stands stock still.*
*Staring far past the entrance gate, he says nothing for quite some time.*
Hiyoshi: "Sorry. Let's go."
Mayu: "Okay."
*As we step into the entrance, we are enveloped by a great light.*
*I think that this light connects the two worlds.*
Mayu: "It really is the outside..."
Hiyoshi: "Yeah."
*The place we've entered is the genuine article, the real Manten Aquarium.*
*The amount of people has increased, and we can hear the sounds of hustle and bustle.*
*With Himeno on his back, Kenji-san is attracting attention, so he moves for the rest area immediately.*
Hiyoshi: "I'm gonna go buy some drinks."
*Setting Himeno down on the sofa, Hiyoshi-san walks off toward the vending machines.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*Himeno is lying down, fast asleep.*
*Looking at her face from the side, I notice something.*
Mayu: "The parasites are gone. They really were a phenomena limited to that place."
Himeno: "M-Mmm..."
*Looking at Himeno's healthy skin color, I'm convinced even more than before.*
*She's been saved... As I think that, something wells up inside of me.*
Hiyoshi: "Like I told you earlier, you can't let your guard down yet."
*Having returned, Kenji-san hands me a canned drink. It's hot cocoa.*
*Kenji-san is holding a bottled sports drink for himself.*
*The hot cocoa permeates my body. It's sweet enough to smother the parts of my heart that aren't yet whole.*
*This hot drink is perfect for a building where the air-conditioner is going full-blast against the hot early summer weather.*
*In contrast to me, Kenji-san, covered in sweat, downs his drink in one gulp.*
Hiyoshi: "Although I guess we should take a breather for now."
*Giving the now empty plastic bottle a good spin, Kenji-san settles himself onto the sofa.*
Mayu: "Um, there's a question I've been meaning to ask... Can I?"
Hiyoshi: "What is it? My age?"
Mayu: "Well, that wasn't it, although I am just a little curious... How old are you, by the way?"
Hiyoshi: "I'm twenty-two. How old are you, Mayu-chan?"
Mayu: "Himeno and I are both fifteen... We're in our third year of junior high."
Hiyoshi: "Hmm. Seven years difference, eh? Well, what did you want to ask me?"
Mayu: "About getting in and out of the Red Manten Aquarium."
*Finishing up our idle chatter, I get down to business.*
Mayu: "It felt like it was not an entrance we passed through so much as we went astray in order to reach the Red Manten Aquarium. Coming back, we passed through an exit. What's the difference? Do you know, Kenji-san?"
Hiyoshi: "At first, I wasn't sure either. In the beginning, I also felt like I had gotten lost and wandered in."
Mayu: "In the beginning? Then, Kenji-san, today you passed through an entrance to get to the Red Manten Aquarium?"
Hiyoshi: "In other words, it's the general concept. I think the way we think about it is the problem."
Mayu: "Can you... give me more details? If I don't have more, I get the feeling that I won't know how to get back in on the next day of the full moon."
Hiyoshi: "No need to worry. Like we talked about before, what you need is a connection. If you aren't rejected by the Red Manten Aquarium, then the way will open."
Hiyoshi: "The problem is the entrance. Thinking of it like an entrance and exit is just an idea we constructed. So the 'door' that connects Manten Aquarium and the Red Manten is really in our minds."
Mayu: "What do you mean by that? If I think of that vending machine as an entrance, then it will become an entrance, is that what you mean?"
*I point at the vending machine Hiyoshi-san bought me cocoa from.*
*I thought no matter what happened, we wouldn't get into that Red Manten Aquarium. It's still just too much for me to comprehend.*
Hiyoshi: "If you are able to believe from the bottom of your heart that it is the entrance, then yes."
Hiyoshi: "Anyway, the border between worlds isn't by nature that distant. It isn't like the wall separating a house from the outside. From the moment we become aware of it, an entrance is created."
Hiyoshi: "It's fine for us to think of it as a door or a ticket gate, even a window or a hole, and if it's not something like that, not be able to see it as an entrance."
Hiyoshi: "We would think it strange to look at a wall and call it an entrance. Only a pure child could think of that as an entrance with all their heart."
Mayu: "In other words, Kenji-san, what you want to say is that because I thought 'this is the entrance,' then any other way in aside from the aquarium entrance disappeared?"
*Kenji-san nods and adds, "Yeah."*
Hiyoshi: "If you don't acknowledge it, it won't exist, but as soon as you do, it does. Maybe ghosts are something like that as well..."
Hiyoshi: "Anyhow, Mayu-chan, you should at least believe that on the day of the full moon, you can get back and forth to the Red Manten Aquarium through the entrance."
Mayu: "Yeah. I think the fact that we got out was proof enough of that."
*I take another sip of my cocoa.*
*At the same time, I hear a loud thud at my side.*
Mayu: "Himeno!"
Himeno: "Ahaha... Sorry, sorry..."
*It's the sound of Himeno losing her balance.*
*In the beginning, I thought she had fallen into a deep sleep, but I guess I was wrong.*
*Himeno is laughing, but her body, which I help up reflexively, feels extremely feverish.*
Mayu: "Himeno! This fever... it's awful..."
Hiyoshi: "What? Fever...?"
Himeno: "I'm telling you, I-I'm fine. Huh, this man is... Hiyoshi-san... right? Why?"
Mayu: "I'll explain later, okay, Himeno? Right now..."
Hiyoshi: "We better get you to a hospital."
Mayu: "I'll call an ambulance."
Himeno: "H-Hey what? What's up with the two of you...? Why are you so worked up...? Yeah, I feel like I've got a fever... but I'm fine... okay?"
Hiyoshi: "Well, she doesn't seem in bad enough shape to need an ambulance. Mayu-chan, we'll talk in more detail the next time we meet. There should be a place nearby where you can get a taxi, so take Himeno to the hospital."
Himeno: "H-Hospital...?"
Hiyoshi: "You're already weakening. It makes sense that you have a fever. Don't dawdle, okay?"
*Kenji-san appeals to Himeno in a soft tone.*
*There's no mistaking that Himeno has no idea what's going on, but she nods at his words.*
Hiyoshi: "Here, for the taxi."
*Kenji-san, seeing us seemingly in agreement, takes a wallet out of his pocket and pulls out a 5,000 yen note.*
Mayu: "Th-That's not necessary... Really, you shouldn't..."
Hiyoshi: "You can pay me back in a month if you want. Isn't it more important for you to get Himeno-chan to a hospital right now? Go look after her."
*Even though Kenji-san is worried, he's not as worried as he was back in the Red Manten Aquarium.*
*That's right. Surely the moment her body, weakened from her deteriorating condition, awakened, fatigue overtook her. Her high fever could easily be explained away as some sort of illness.*
Hiyoshi: "Listen! I want you to promise me this. At the very least, I didn't lie to you. In one month... on the day of the full moon, you two definitely need to come. Around the same time as today, midday works fine."
Mayu: "I got it. Really... thank you so much for everything."
*There are many more things I want to say.*
*Kenji-san, who saved not just me, but Himeno as well.*
*He gave us all this information and backed it up with his actions.*
Himeno: "Uh... I don't really get what's going on... but what's with this atmosphere...? Ugh..."
Hiyoshi: "Look, you need to understand that your body is weaker than you think it is. You can get more details while you're in the taxi."
Himeno: "R-Right..."
*Kenji-san narrows his eyes and chastises Himeno.*
*Watching Himeno actually get talked into something by someone else is somewhat refreshing.*
Hiyoshi: "Well, later. We definitely... won't fail."
Mayu: "Right. Later..."
*Kenji-san heads off somewhere before Himeno and I leave.*
*There's a taxi stand on the basement level of Manten East Building, so we grab a ride from there.*
*Due to her high fever, Himeno staggers about, but unlike in the Red Manten Aquarium, it's not bad enough that she can't walk on her own. With one hand holding the railing and the other holding mine, she is able to gradually make her way on her own legs.*
*I'm sure that when we get to the hospital and she gets an IV drip in her or something, with a little rest, she'll get better.*
*Thinking back on it, this is the first time I've ridden in a taxi on my own. Sometimes my parents use one, but I usually just follow their lead and ride quietly.*
*Raising my hand, getting in, telling the driver my destination. Paying when we reach our destination, saying thank you.*
*As we head for the taxi stand, I review the procedures in my head over and over again and tighten my facial muscles so they don't look down on me or try to take us on unnecessary detours just because I'm a junior high student.*
*If Himeno were in my shoes, she'd be fine, but since it's me, I have to give it my all.*
Mayu: "Um..."
*It's not like someone would hear me from inside the car, but I modestly raise my hand and call out.*
*Without a word, the door opens, then the driver calls out to us.*
Driver: "That girl there, is she not feeling well? All right to head for the hospital? The closest hospital in the area is Manten General. Should we go there?"
Mayu: "Yes..."
*As the driver prattled on, talking very fast, we sat down in the taxi's backseat. Himeno sat further in, and I took the seat closest to the door we got in through.*
*Just as I had rehearsed in my head, we start off without incident thanks to the kind driver.*
*Despite that start boding well, we end up in heavy traffic.*
*The Sunday night homebound rush hour.*
Himeno: "Wow, crazy~"
*But I wasn't impatient. Hoping it might get better as we sat in the car, Himeno, though her breath was ragged, was able to talk to me in the taxi.*
Himeno: "If it's this crowded, wouldn't it be better for me... not to go?"
Mayu: "No it wouldn't! You might become faint like you were back in the rest area."
Himeno: "Mayu, aren't you worrying too much?"
Mayu: "Well... it really was bad, you know."
Himeno: "Ah, that's right, that's right. Give me the details. I don't remember much beyond the fact that I was being done in by bugs. I do remember you showing up and telling me it was gonna be all right though, Mayu."
*Within this car that makes walking seem faster, I tell Himeno what happened after I got lost in the Red Manten Aquarium.*
*Most likely because we aren't talking to him, the driver turns the radio volume up.*
*But that way suits us better anyway, so I decide to start from the beginning.*
*Finding the fish corpses, the tank rupturing in the jellyfish booth...*
*Being saved by Kenji-san, hearing Mari's voice, reuniting with Himeno, meeting that girl...*
*The contents of the journal that staff member left beind, and the secrets of the Red Manten Aquarium that Kenji-san told me, I tell her all of it.*
*Having given voice to all of these things, I'm able to put them in order for myself as well.*
*We reach the point of the choice I had to make in the Fish of the World booth. When I tell her my decision, Himeno's face changes.*
Himeno: "... Driver, could you possibly stop the car here?"
*Himeno clearly says this to the driver, right in the middle of the congestion.*
Mayu: "Hey, Himeno!"
Himeno: "As you were talking I thought... that couldn't be. About Mari-chan, yeah, I think it'd be best to check when we go back in a month. It's hard to say, but it's probably because this is a conversation about dimensions that transcends time."
Mayu: "Himeno, if you talk so much... you'll get worse."
*Himeno ignores me and continues.*
Himeno: "But what about that girl? She's living in a time unchanged from us. Then, Mayu, that choice... you shouldn't make it. I'm happy you saved me. I probably would have died, b-but..."
Himeno: "Is it really okay to abandon someone in trouble right in front of you?"
Mayu: "But I... It was for you, Himeno......"
Himeno: "I know that. I can never thank you enough. Out of nowhere, it's like you became my hero. It's not that I want to argue with you. I don't want us to split up like we did back there, but I can't just go on without saying something."
Himeno: "Mayu, you'll definitely... regret it."
*Himeno's expression in and of itself is a serious one.*
*In her eyes, there's no color change nor hint of weakening.*
*I sit there, unable to respond to Himeno. Himeno smiles a bit, then continues.*
Himeno: "Mayu, if you're tired, then I'll go save that girl. Who that girl is has nothing to do with it. This time, I have to show you my good side."
*I think... No way.*
*Her voice is so strong that it makes me wonder just how much she could have recovered after that little rest.*
Mayu: "Hi-me-no..."
*Timidly, I touch Himeno's forehead.*
*It's so hot, it makes me almost pull my hand back in reaction.*
*It makes it easy to see just how much she's overdoing it.*
*Himeno's desire is real. However, her will alone won't bring her desires into reality. Like going back and saving that girl, for instance.*
*I'm sure she's wobbly from standing up alone.*
*If Himeno's resolution is to come to fruition, then there's only one way to do it.*
*Even though it involves going against my earlier decision.*
*I--*
Mayu: "Driver, please stop there."
*I say this before all my thoughts are even in order.*
*During a break in traffic, I spot the entrance to a subway station.*
*If I take that route, I can get back to Manten Station with no transfers.*
Himeno: "Mayu..."
Mayu: "Don't worry. I can definitely rescue her and make it back on my own."
Himeno: "That's... that's not it!"
Mayu: "If you understand me, Himeno, then I know all about you. You're overdoing it. I'm fine, so trust me."
*The taxi breaks away from the traffic and stops on the side of the road.*
*I leave Himeno the 5,000 yen I borrowed from Kenji-san, and ask the driver, "Please take this girl to the hospital."*
*I get out through the automatic door.*
*Himeno pulls on the hem of my skirt and calls after me.*
Himeno: "No, why are you going alone, Mayu... I'll..."
Mayu: "If you were really that well, you'd jump out of the taxi right away, but you... can't even manage that much."
Himeno: "I'll go... with you......"
Mayu: "Trust me... Wait for me! I'll carry your concern with me, Himeno!"
Himeno: "Mayu... but..."
*I turn back to Himeno and put my all into forging a smile.*
*I don't want to go back to such a scary place. That's how I really feel.*
*But Himeno said I would surely regret it. She's right. Somewhere, deep down in my heart, I know it too.*
*Kenji-san is no longer there. I can't rely on him. I truly am fighting this battle alone.*
*Even so, if it's for Himeno, I can't fail.*
Himeno: "-- Ice cream. We haven't eaten it yet, have we? Let's eat some, together."
Mayu: "Right!"
*The door closes with a hollow slam, and the car returns to the flow of traffic.*
Mayu: "I gotta go."
*Without looking back, I run down to the subway platform.*
*Getting off at the station, I begin walking down the street in the night.*
*It's the same street I walked down mere hours ago, but it seems completely different at night.*
*I couldn't have imagined that this would happen.*
*The full moon floating in the sky.*
*Heading for Manten East Building, it feels like I'm walking towards the moon itself.*
*That mysterious light envelopes the world.*
*Surely that other world is equally illuminated by the light of the moon. Which is why the Red Manten Aquarium exists.*
*My gait seems especially courageous.*
*I believe that only hours ago, I fought, overcame, and changed myself.*
*Manten East Building at night has taken on a new feeling.*
*Thanks to its illumination, the contours of the building are more pronounced. Its sheer size is also more striking.*
*Thinking deeply about the other world being born within this building, I feel that the light seeping out from the signs and windows is eerie.*
Mayu: "I'm home."
*I wouldn't have made such a joke this morning.*
*I smile and giggle to myself... I've gone crazy.*
*The faint scent of salt water.*
*I've returned once again to the deep sea on the surface.*
*Kenji-san said the "path" connecting the worlds only opens on the day of the full moon. In other words, there's a time limit.*
*I sneak a glance at the clock by the counter. It's about 7 p.m. So there are only about five hours left. Around the same amount of time from when I went in to when I came out.*
*At this rate, I should have plenty of time...*
*Completing the process for re-entry, I stand in front of the ticket gate.*
*This is the entrance.*
*If this is physically an entrance, then conceptually it's an entrance too.*
*Closing my eyes, I walk through it.*
*I can feel a strong light from beyond my eyelids.*
*When the light stops, I open my eyes.*
*I feel relieved to see that there are no customers. I think to myself how strange it is that something like that comforts me.*
*I was able to get back in so easily. If this were a movie or a novel, the power of the moon would get stronger when it's full or something to that effect.*
Mayu: "Gotta keep moving."
*I take small steps, one at a time, in order not to get careless.*
Mayu: "... Huh?"
*I become aware of a strange phenomena. I don't know if it's right to call it that though. Anyway, I spot a familiar shadow some distance away.*
Mayu: "Kenji... san..."
*Without thinking, I mumble his name, but he doesn't seem to hear.*
*He might help me out... is what I think for a brief moment.*
Mayu: "What is this...? He's--"
*Strange.*
*There's an intense look in his eyes as he frantically looks around.*
*It's like he's trying to find something.*
*He originally said that he would return one month from now. For him to come back intentionally like this, the only things I can think of are that he forgot something, or that, like me, he came back to save the girl.*
*However, he was the one who said we should leave the girl alone in the first place. It's hard to believe he would come back here on his own.*
*Since he's come here so many times, it's hard to believe he would forget something, or to think he'd come back to retrieve it if he did.*
*I wonder why.*
*My instincts are telling me this is dangerous.*
*Kenji-san, the one who suddenly changed as if he were possessed and attacked me.*
*His eyes are the same as they were back then.*
Mayu: "No, at that time... that was... because of the Red Manten Aquarium..."
*I can't doubt him. I can't embrace these doubts about Kenji-san, who saved me. That was something he couldn't help. It came from a will that was not his own, like a natural phenomena. Just like a typhoon.*
*Which is exactly why I couldn't ask him about that sudden change.*
*The doubts tangled up in my chest make my legs feel heavy. At this point, I didn't expect my own doubts to be like shackles.*
*No, that's not it.*
*Kenji-san is my ally, so to speak.*
*I need to cast off my doubts and open up my heart to Kenji-san.*
Mayu: "Just a little bit more, just a little...!"
*I decide to trust my instincts.*
*When we split up at the taxi stand, the nametag he should have taken off was around his neck. He had told us it worked like a discount coupon. He probably forgot to take it off after he re-entered.*
*Kenji-san enters the souvenir shop.*
*I can't imagine what he's gone in there for.*
*I watch over the souvenir shop from the shadows of the Tokyo Bay booth so I won't be discovered.*
*My heartbeat gradually quickens.*
*The wait feels long.*
*Even though it's been no more than five minutes, thanks to this strange feeling of unease, it feels like an hour or two.*
*If only there had been no change since my return.*
*If only he hadn't had those eyes.*
*I should call out to him, tell him what Himeno said, and have him help me if possible.*
Mayu: "There he is..."
*I pay attention to Kenji-san, who has come out of the souvenir shop mumbling to himself.*
Hiyoshi: "That child. Where in the world is she hiding...?"
*Despite the fact that he's far away, his words shock me so much it's as if they're hammered into my chest.*
Mayu: "That... child...!"
*The Red Manten Aquarium... and a child.*
*... Mari.*
*I can't think of anyone else.*
*The memory of Saginuma Rieko when she appeared by Himeno back at the stage, also saying she was looking for a child, sticks out in my mind.*
*Why would the criminal who murdered the director be looking for Mari? And why is Kenji-san also looking for her?*
*The Red Manten Aquarium.*
*The murder of Manten Aquarium's director.*
*The mysterious girl who was the director's daughter.*
*All of these scattered pieces, could they be part of the same puzzle?*
*I give a loud gulp.*
*These frightening surprises don't end there.*
*I continue to follow Kenji-san with my eyes.*
*He heads for the staff passageway behind the panel we pressed earlier.*
*At the moment he opens the door and is about to disappear...*
Mayu: "Saginuma... Rieko...!"
*There's the slender shadow of a woman there.*
*As soon as I see her, I can't grasp why she'd be together with Kenji-san, the man who shoved her and tried to kill her before.*
Mayu: "......"
*I briefly avert my eyes from the scene before me, but quickly return to looking through the crack in the door.*
*They're putting their lips together. It's what society refers to as a kiss.*
*The two of them... kissing lacks any romance, being merely sinister.*
*I get the feeling I just saw something I shouldn't have.*
*I lose sight of them as I'm wondering what to do and decide my next move. First, I should go over to him, and find out what he was waiting on before. Once I've sorted out what's going on, I should still have time to find the girl.*
*Trying to not make any sound, I head for the staff passageway in front of the souvenir shop.*
*As I'm walking along, my fingertips feel as if they've touched something.*
*It's right in front of the door to the souvenir shop.*
Mayu: "This is... the keychain..."
*Standing still, I pick up the small item.*
*It's one of the prizes from the GachaGacha machine with the small starfish attached to the droplet.*
*Flipping it over, there's a photo printed on a sticker stuck to the backside. A little girl and her father... This belonged to that girl.*
Mayu: "Could she be... in here...?"
*But Kenji-san reacted as if she wasn't there.*
*Which probably means that Mari isn't here. Anyway, the fact that the keychain, which I returned to her at the Fish of the World booth, is here in front of the souvenir stand means that at least she got this far.*
*I take a deep breath.*
*I have to go in and see. I have to go inside and confirm for myself.*
*The fear of the poison frogs dropping down again floats through my mind.*
*One way or the other, if that girl is inside, then according to the law of fixed coordinates, we should be able to get out safely.*
*Even if we get caught up in some phenomena, I won't be careless. Whether going or coming, no matter what does or doesn't happen, I'm risking my life.*
Mayu: "She's probably inside..."
*I'm concerned about Kenji-san and all, but I should start by investigating nearby.*
*Embracing my worries, I enter the souvenir shop.*
Mayu: "... It doesn't seem like there are any frogs."
*As soon as I enter, I take a look around.*
*There's no sign of the ghastly sight of melted frogs.*
*Still, an air as if something bizarre and grave is afoot enters my mouth, causing my tongue to curl. I place my hand on my chest and strengthen my vigilance.*
Mayu: "This is... a bloodstain...?"
*It's hard to see in the dark, but the deep red blood is spread out here and there across the floor.*
*It goes all the way to the counter, like a path.*
Mayu: "Something's... here."
*That alone I can be sure of.*
*From within the dark, something may jump out at me.*
*As I take a step, I look around, left and right, using such great caution one might call it excessive.*
*Clack, clack, clack.*
*The echoing of my own footsteps provides me some relief.*
*On closer inspection, the blood circumvents the counter and continues around the back.*
*As I follow it, I notice something in the garbage can by the counter.*
Mayu: "This is..."
*One nametag has been thrown away. It catches my eye because it looks like Kenji-san's. The cover for it is worn down and dirty.*
*He had his on when he left the souvenir shop, so it's not like he threw his away.*
*But contrary to my expectations, the name "Hiyoshi Kenji" is on it.*
*Having my doubts, I flip it over, and immediately drop it.*
*On the back of the nametag, written in big red letters with magic marker,*
*Murderer.*
*The letters themselves are like a curse.*
*I don't understand at all what this means, but I don't have any desire to take a closer look to find out. Having probably thrown it away when he was here earlier, I satisfy myself with the thought that it is just a prank.*
*Following the bloodstain takes precedence.*
*Remaining cautious, I peek behind the counter.*
Mayu: "There's nothing... here..."
Mari: ".... Ter."
Mayu: "Kyaah!"
*As I suddenly hear someone's voice, I shout without thinking.*
*There's no one below the counter. Just two boxes left there.*
*There's no one behind me, nor next to me.*
*Could it be above!*
*As I consider this and look up at the ceiling, the only thing there is the sprinkler.*
*Appearing as if it hasn't been used in some time, it seems this place is in a different phase than it was before.*
*But more important than that is the voice's owner.*
*Without thinking, I narrow my eyes and look all around.*
Sayo: "Hey, don't make a sound."
Mayu: "Huh!"
Mari: "Big sister! Big sister!"
Sayo: "Huh?"
Mayu: "Huh, that voice? Mari?"
*The cardboard box at my feet skitters about.*
Sayo: "We have to be sure. What do we do if he's with her?"
Mayu: "That voice... is it that girl? By 'him' do you mean that Kenji-san who was here before? It's fine. I'm here alone."
Mari: "Yeah!"
Sayo: "Wait. It might be a trap."
*As if the girl's voice didn't even reach Mari, she topples the large cardboard box over.*
*Mari immediately jumps out and clamps on to my leg."
Mayu: "Mari...!"
Mari: "Big sister!"
*I embrace Mari's tiny shoulders in return.*
Mayu: "Mari, Mari! Why are you here? How-- We can see each other? Is that really you?"
Mari: "Hey!"
*Saying this, Mari twirls around once. That innocent behavior, there's no mistaking that it's her.*
Mayu: "... Hahh..."
*I'm finally able to trust that the girl before my eyes is Mari. I'm surprised. It's the first time that I've truly understood how people who are shocked are left speechless.*
*I'm able to see Mari... I hug her again to confirm that it's actually true. I'm able to actually feel Mari's body on the other side of that bulky fabric with my own flesh.*
Mayu: "Mari, were you really that small...?"
Mari: "Big sister, you got so big you don't seem like yourself!"
*Mari rubs her plump cheek against me.*
*The difference in our height after five years is clear.*
Mari: "Even though I was younger, I was a bit bigger, right~?"
Mayu: "Hey, that's not true. We were the same height."
*I'm older, so even us being the same height is nothing to boast about, but this stubbornness itself is vital to my memories.*
*I hug Mari again, clenching even my fingers tight around her.*
*And then I realize it.*
Sayo: "Sorry, I know you're having a touching reunion, but..."
*The girl was inside of one of the boxes, despite how difficult it was to get in, and seems to be struggling to get out now.*
*Finally, she breaks out of the box by tearing it open, and not the least bit bothered by it, she speaks in a cold tone.*
*The blood spattered here and there appears to be hers. It seems to have stopped now, but surely it was from the wound on her forehead having opened.*
Sayo: "That girl, she's already dead."
*It's something I'd already realized. That's because I couldn't feel Mari's body heat.*
*That girl had put it into words, as if it were no big deal.*
*I had prepared myself and my heart, but on actually being able to embrace Mari, that difference causes my chest to tighten.*
Mari: "Big sister, I'm sorry."
Mayu: "That's not something to apologize for, Mari."
*Mari looks at me with an anxious expression on her face.*
*I rub Mari's head. Her hair is silky, a fact that doesn't change whether she's alive or dead.*
Mari: "Sayo-chan. Can I tell my big sister about it?"
Mayu: "Sayo-chan...? Is that this girl's name?"
Mari: "Yup! She told me!"
Sayo: "That's right. Though Mayumi, please drop the 'chan.'"
Mayu: "Sayo... is it? Okay, nice to meet you."
Sayo: "There's nothing nice about it."
*What a cold response.*
Mari: "Sayo-chan, are you a friend of my big sister?"
Sayo: "Not particularly."
Mari: "Do you not like her then?"
Sayo: "I don't particularly dislike her. Mayumi, what's so funny?"
*Their exchange is so funny, I start snickering.*
*Because it's funny to see Sayo wavering at Mari's barrage of questions.*
Mayu: "Um... so Mari, what was it you wanted to tell me?"
Mari: "Um, well..."
*Mari glances in Sayo's direction.*
*Sayo tilts her head silently.*
Sayo: "Wait a second. Mayumi, why are you here?"
Mayu: "Why, you ask..."
Sayo: "Didn't you take that man's advice and leave me behind? I didn't see it as you abandoning me and felt your actions were correct, but I do want you to tell me your reasoning."
Mayu: "You heard all that?"
Sayo: "At that time, I couldn't move at all. Not even my lips, but I was conscious. 'The Red Manten Aquarium'... what a dumb name. Anyway, that was the only part of your conversation I caught."
Mayu: "There's nothing to hide from you then."
*Somewhere inside, I feel relieved.*
*My guilt at having left Sayo comes back, making me fully aware of its presence.*
*But she said what I did was right. I don't think Sayo's the type who would lie about that.*
*The decision I made at that moment was definitely not wrong.*
*I explain those feelings between Himeno and I that, without worry over whether they were right or not, brought me back here.*
Sayo: "So stupid. You should have just left me alone."
Mayu: "It was my decision... I can't let things be anymore."
*No gratitude... or apology.*
*I don't see it as us having grown closer, but Sayo is accepting me just a little bit more.*
*Unlike when I'm with Kenji-san, and when I'm with Himeno, it's the first time I've truly appreciated this feeling of relief.*
Mayu: "Now it's my turn. Sayo... why are you with Mari?"
Sayo: "That story's a long one. Where should I start?"
Mayu: "First of all... how about telling me about when Saginuma Rieko tried to kill you?"
Sayo: "You heard that from that guy already. She's the criminal who killed my papa. She's already dead, a spirit out for vengeance. She's merely a shadow, only able to exist within Manten Aquarium."
Sayo: "When I pointed that out to her, she suddenly attacked me. She probably doesn't wanna acknowledge that she's dead. She's looking for Mari."
Mayu: "So... she was looking for Mari after all, but why? If she's already dead, why is she...?"
Mari: "Her feelings have gotten really big, like a balloon. She can only think about that one thing, so much so that she's forgotten everything else."
*The one who answers my question is Mari.*
*Mari, likely unable to stay silent any longer, gets between Sayo and I, and speaks while spreading both her arms out wide.*
Sayo: "I also heard that. In other words, she has lingering attachments from when she was alive. Vengeful spirits and other ghosts usually do. I don't believe in such things, so it's just theoretical."
*Sayo says this while leaning against the counter.*
*I can see she's settling in for a long conversation.*
Mari: "I've always wanted to see you, big sister... This whole time, it's what I've wanted."
Mayu: "That's why... you called out to me over and over again."
Mari: "But I wasn't feeling like myself. With no body, I was just fuzzy! I couldn't even come see you!"
Mayu: "F-Fuzzy?"
Mari: "Right. Fuzzy!"
*I think I'm missing something fundamental in what Mari's trying to say.*
*I wonder if Sayo's even listening, but when I look her way, her eyes are closed.*
Sayo: "She's your little sister, Mayumi, so you get what she's saying, right?"
Mayu: "You say that, but you don't get it either, do you?"
*When I give her a hard time, Sayo sullenly puffs up her cheeks.*
*And soon after, she clicks her tongue with a "tch," and looks away.*
*Even if she wanted to tell me she didn't get it after all, she can't.*
Mayu: "Well then, how do you feel right now?"
Mari: "Now? Hmm~ Normal?"
Mayu: "Normal? Hmm~ Anyway, let's set that subject aside."
Mari: "But you know, I remembered lots of stuff! After I met Sayo-chan... Huh, maybe it was before... Until now, all I could think about was seeing you, but I did remember something important."
*Mari looks a little saddened.*
*I still don't understand the reason why.*
Mayu: "Is that what you wanted to tell me earlier?"
Mari: "Yeah. Sayo-chan, I can tell my big sister, right? She's not an 'enemy,' right?"
Sayo: "It should be fine. She's just too soft is all."
Mayu: "... Sayo, what are you filling Mari's head with...?"
*Sayo gives me no response.*
*I look back at Mari and wait for her to continue.*
Mari: "You see..."
*Mari takes in a small breath.*
Mari: "I saw a man get hit until he couldn't move. That scary man, he did that with a scary lady."
*Mari quietly tells me the truth.*
*That way of telling the story is unmistakably fitting for Mari.*
Mayu: "That's..."
Sayo: "That was probably when the director -- my papa -- was killed."
*I understood that. However, Sayo readily reiterates it.*
Mayu: "The woman was the criminal, in other words, Saginuma Rieko, right? Then the man was..."
Mari: "A scary man who came after me!"
Mayu: "Who... do you mean?"
Sayo: "You should already know. That's why I had to be sure earlier, Mayumi."
Mayu: "Kenji-san would never do such a thing. Mari, you must be mistaken..."
Mari: "Big sister, I'm not. I may be more grown up than when I saw it, but that man keeps coming after me."
Mayu: "That's... because of the curse...He has to come back here at regular intervals."
Sayo: "It lines up with what the diary said, so it's probably true, but that's not all."
Mari: "He came here with many different ladies, and then... did something scary. All of them screamed."
Mayu: "What does... that mean?"
*With a frightened look on her face, Mari shakes her head.*
*Even though it's a scene she witnessed herself, Mari doesn't seem to understand the meaning behind it.*
Sayo: "It's that 'life energy' he was talking about. If the number of people possessed by this place decreases, then don't you think the mere existence of the Red Manten Aquarium is using that energy up?"
Mayu: "That's why I'm asking what it means!"
*I yell suddenly.*
*I become aware of the fact that, through all the things that have happened here, my own perception has gotten sharper.*
*Talking to Mari and Sayo is causing connections to be formed in my mind, allowing me to guess some things.*
*I'm sure I yelled because I'm trying to deny the thoughts floating about in my own mind.*
*However, Sayo is not so gentle.*
Sayo: "They need lives, or something like that. Essentially, he's bringing people here and killing them. Even if he doesn't kill them, he can probably at least steal their life energy. Don't you remember that rumor about the number of missing people increasing?"
Mayu: "Kenji-san said something similar, but Himeno knew that rumor as well!"
Sayo: "People were saying rumors like that around me as well. If you want to spread a rumor, you gather as many idiots as possible to do it. He knew that."
Mayu: "Just come out and say it!"
Sayo: "I've been trying to say it the whole time."
*Sayo heaves a sigh and continues.*
Sayo: "Treating the rumors like bait that Manten Aquarium scatters for its prey is kind of ironic. The one spreading those rumors is that man. To prove it, I let him pick me up. It was creepy."
Mayu: "Kenji-san... I thought him hitting on girls was normal. Him spreading rumors is a completely different thing."
Sayo: "I came here to look into some things and found someone who had escaped. She doesn't seem like she'll ever wake up, and it's probably because of having her energy exhausted."
Sayo: "Before she came to the aquarium, some man called out to her. I have no doubts it was him."
Sayo: "If he's Saginuma Rieko's accomplice, then it's not odd for him to help preserve the Red Manten Aquarium. Even if we refer to them as the worst of humanity, they probably don't want to disappear. Even now, they're probably still working together."
Mayu: "But Kenji-san's alive!"
Mari: "Geez, I'm not done with my story yet!"
*Mari spreads both arms and starts bouncing up and down.*
Mayu: "Mari... Yeah, sorry. I lost my cool for a bit..."
Sayo: "Speaking of which, I haven't heard this yet either."
Mari: "After I saw the man fall over, I was chased by that scary lady. It wasn't anything like a game of tag. I climbed this ladder and hid inside a giant tank."
Sayo: "In the top part of the tunnel tank, right?"
Mari: "The lady glared at me. I was so scared, but I tried to get her to leave me alone by running away. That was when that man showed up!"
Mayu: "Kenji-san did..."
Mari: "He was really, really scary. First he got rid of the lady, then he turned something that looked like a handle and then water started pouring in, but from there, I don't really remember anything. It all gets kinda fuzzy."
*Before Mari can finish, I break in.*
Mayu: "So then, that means that... Kenji-san, Mari, he--"
Sayo: "He killed her along with Saginuma Rieko. I think we can all agree he was the only one still living."
Mayu: "No, no, it's a lie, it's gotta be... Hey, Mari? Did you... maybe mistake what you saw?"
Mari: "Sorry, big sister. I... said something awful, didn't I?"
*Mari looks at me with large eyes.*
*I'm sure she doesn't completely understand the horrific misfortune that befell her body. That's why she has been able to maintain her pure heart.*
*I'm a terrible big sister.*
*Of course Mari is the one I should believe over anyone else...*
Mayu: "I just can't believe Kenji-san would do something like that!"
Sayo: "Sorry, but I'm also a bit confused. He's definitely the accomplice to the one who killed my papa, Saginuma Rieko. So, then why would he kill her? Moreover, I can't grasp the reason they would work together now."
Mayu: "Sayo, you also saw the two of them together?"
Sayo: "That's why I'm saying this. Seeing that those two were after Mari, she and I ended up together like this. By 'also,' then you mean you saw them as well, Mayumi?"
Mayu: "I saw Kenji-san meet up with Saginuma Rieko in the staff passageway after he came out of the souvenir shop."
Sayo: "Tch. If you saw it too, why don't you believe us?"
Mayu: "Because Kenji-san saved you, me, and Himeno as well!"
Sayo: "Ah, geez, I don't get you."
Mayu: "I don't get you either."
Mari: "Geez, don't fight~!"
*It's Mari's words that stop the heated quarrel between us.*
*We both look at Mari. She is puffing up her cheeks and her face is red.*
*Looking at her face, even more strength leaves my body.*
*Being so close to her, I had forgotten that Mari is already dead.*
*She's aware of her own death.*
*At this point, the futility of it all makes it hard for me to stay standing.*
Mayu: "I'm sorry, Mari... I really am..."
Mari: "I don't really get it, but I forgive you. I don't want to ever fight with you again, big sister!"
*I've sunk to the ground, and Mari begins proudly rubbing my head.*
*Her hand has no heat, but I can feel a slight warmth.*
*Sayo puts some distance between us, standing a little ways away.*
Sayo: "Having reached a conclusion, I think the only thing left is to see what lies on the other side, but it seems I haven't reached the truth itself just yet."
Mayu: "I also want to know... the truth. We have to find it, no matter what."
Mari: "Big sister, do you feel better? Sayo-chan, are you okay too?"
*We both nod.*
*As I stand up, Sayo and I look at one another.*
Mayu: "Speaking of which, Sayo, you dropped this."
*Thanks to our eyes meeting, I'm reminded of the keychain tucked into my pocket.*
*After taking the keychain from me, Sayo looks at the back.*
*She sees the sticker photo of two people.*
*She says a brief, "Thanks," looks up, and continues.*
Sayo: "I'm going to see my papa. To be more accurate, it's probably his spirit. Papa is likely related to the creation of this closed-off world. I'm sure the truth lies there. I suppose there's no need to ask the two of you at this point."
Mayu: "... Right."
Mari: "Right~ Sayo-chan, I wonder what your father is like. Our father..."
*That was a slight change.*
*Sayo is no longer alone.*
*It may have been Mari who brought about this change.*
*No matter what truth lies ahead of us, I get the feeling that if we don't forget what it is we believe in, we'll be all right.*
*Talking with Sayo, we decide to take the route to the director's office that passes through the tunnel tank, the Fish of the World booth, and the back staff passageway.*
*This small aquarium supposedly has a magnificent director's office.*
*Supposedly to reflect Sayo's father's preferences, expensive, foreign-made furniture is situated around the room.*
*It seems that, when the Deep-sea Fish booth was open, one could see through the tank and watch the deep-sea fish from the director's office.*
*I'm a little excited that I'll get to see such a magnificent room.*
Mari: "Wow~ Pretty!"
Mayu: "It's pretty, but why is it so calm?"
Sayo: "Probably because they're among companions."
*The "tank" part of the tunnel tank no longer has any meaning.*
*As if all of the air is filled with water, the fish are swimming all about.*
*Like the bizarre incident I witnessed with Kenji-san with the cutlassfish, I'm left completely confused.*
Mari: "Yup, there, there."
*Mari gently pets a ray that passes beside her.*
*A group of sardine pass above our heads, casting shadows that look like bullets, and Mari follows them with her eyes until they pass.*
*The flat fish that passes by my legs is a...*
Mayu: "Um, a right-eye flounder?"
Sayo: "It's a large-tooth flounder. On the left is a large-tooth flounder, on the right is a right-eye flounder. Mayumi, you thought they were the same, didn't you?"
Mayu: "Th-That's not true! The large-tooth flounder is so crisp and crunchy, it's delicious as sashimi. The right-eye flounder is more soft and flaky, so it's better boiled in soy sauce. I know the difference."
Mari: "Big sister, you're such a glutton~!"
Mayu: "E-Even you, Mari!"
*"Geez," I say and puff up my cheeks.*
*At any rate, being able to actually enjoy the aquarium like this is refreshing.*
*We are enjoying it, but the feeling is different. If nothing scary happens, this "unreality" isn't bad.*
Mayu: "Ahhh!!"
*Straight ahead of where I'm walking and spacing out, the nose of a large shadow brushes past me.*
*I fall on my backside in surprise.*
Mari: "Geez, don't get so scared! It's because you get scared that they all react!"
Mayu: "Yeah, I'm all right. I wasn't scared, just surprised... Was that a sunfish just now?"
*The large fish with the shape of a shuriken is an ocean sunfish.*
*My feeling on being so close its nose could brush against me was that it was honestly grotesque. With its hollow face and its sleek-to-the-touch skin, it's like the face of an alien swimming about.*
Sayo: "It's because you're scared that they react. They react to our negative emotions."
Mayu: "Just because these creatures are here, there's no indication that they'll attack. Right now, I think that... they're enjoying it a little."
Mari: "Hm~ I don't really get it, but because I get along with them, they might not attack us?"
Sayo: "Even back when you came with me to the souvenir stand, no matter how many doors we passed through, nothing happened. Maybe you're special, Mari."
Mayu: "Mari, you're amazing."
Mari: "Ehehe!"
*Mari proudly folds her arms.*
*On the other side of the tragic fact that the fish, like Mari, are no longer living, is the optimistic fact that they are not alone either.*
*Manten Aquarium, when it's not experiencing phenomena, is smaller than I expected. We've probably walked for about fifteen minutes.*
Mayu: "This is... the director's office?"
*Sayo nods, her lips pressed together.*
*The door is solemn, bringing about an odd atmosphere. It's something of a mismatch with the tranquil aquarium, leaving a heavy impression.*
Mari: "I'm a little scared, big sister."
Sayo: "We took a look around, but Papa was nowhere to be found. So he must be here. This was his favorite place."
Mayu: "Is there any chance that he's gone crazy? That he might have been taken in by his own hatred or malice..."
Sayo: "There is, but, Mayumi, you went back to someone who lost it before, right? There's no reason I can't either."
Mayu: "In other words, you're prepared for whatever may happen?"
Sayo: "Yes, I have been from the start."
*Taking the lead, Sayo places her hand on the door.*
Sayo: "No, why?"
*After entering the director's office, that's the first thing Sayo says.*
*Just as Sayo told us, the director's office has expensive antique furniture, and one of the walls is a tank.*
*The director's chair looks soft and comfortable to sit in.*
Sayo: "Why is he sitting there like normal?"
*The director is sitting there calmly.*
Mari: "Sayo-chan, I think something is different."
*A pale blue light is flowing out from the tank.*
*Looking inside from the entrance, the form of the director sitting there has the transparency of a projection on a screen, wavering about.*
Mayu: "Let's... have a little look."
*Sayo, who was trying to make her way forward, stops, her shoulders shuddering.*
*Seeing her father, who had passed away, right before her eyes, it's natural that she would want to get closer. I understand such a feeling well, but I also understand that we can't lose our cool either.*
*I stand the furthest forward of the three of us, my focus on the director's desk.*
Sayo: "It's the director, Sakuragi Isao. There's no mistake, that's my papa."
Rieko: "Director..."
*On the screen of light, one more character appears.*
Mayu: "Saginuma... Rieko... But this is like, a video, or something."
*Here she appears a little younger.*
*Without even glancing our way, she faces the comfortably-seated director.*
*Saginuma Rieko has a frantic look on her face and places her hands on the desk.*
*The director wipes away the spot where Saginuma Rieko had placed her hands on the antique desk.*
*She glares at the director for just a second before calming her expression.*
Rieko: "Why did you reject my plan? The greatest number of visitors to Manten East Building comes from women in their teens and twenties. There are also many couples."
Rieko: "If we partner with other tenants or offer paired tickets, as well as attach ourselves to the image of a fashionable nightlife, we can expect a set minimum number of customers."
Rieko: "We could also partner with magazines for coverage. Other aquariums aren't being touted as date spots yet. If we follow this course, it could be extremely advantageous to all involved."
Director: "It goes without saying. An aquarium isn't some flashy attraction. Our purpose through and through is to display living things. Having foolish young people point and laugh, where is the respect for living things in that?"
Director: "In that case, shouldn't we convey to young people the splendor of marine life and teach them how precious it is, as well as the smallness of humanity?"
Rieko: "Then what about this application? Saying nothing to me, throwing several million yen into an addition for the stage. Children would love that. The number of visitors would temporarily increase, but does it serve any other purpose?"
Rieko: "You employ animal trainers to train them from scratch. We don't have the know-how for this. Moreover, given the scope of Manten Aquarium, how great of a show do you think we can offer?"
Rieko: "A show on a stage won't win against our competition. Let's make that clear. If people get bored soon after, all we're left with is a balance in the red."
Director: "It won't end up that way if the staff as a whole works together for that end."
Rieko: "Director, you understand nothing. What we need is a new measure that will yield fast results. As management, you at least should grasp the gravity of the issue, right?"
Director: "And all your plan concerns itself with is profit in the immediate future. It is not money that keeps us going, it is living things. If we forget our feelings of respect for them, Manten Aquarium has no future."
Rieko: "If we're shut down, many of them will be killed. Money absolutely supports Manten Aquarium, us, and the living things. Why can't you see that?"
Director: "It seems our ideas are fundamentally mismatched."
Rieko: "Even so, I'm not merely thinking of profits in the immediate future. Couples nowadays will likely be married in the future. If they continue to enjoy Manten Aquarium, then ten years from now, they may bring their own children to Manten Aquarium."
Director: "A result in one year is better than waiting ten years for one, is it not?"
Rieko: "That's not what I'm trying to say!"
Director: "Rather than focusing on couples and the like, shouldn't you worry about first getting married yourself? You're already 27, right? Normally, one would be well on the way to having children at that age, wouldn't they?"
Rieko: "Hahh...!"
Director: "If you have nothing to say back, then you lack any persuasive ability. If you have time to think up nonsense plans, then you have time to think of ways that the plan I'm pushing for can generate profit."
*The two forms waver and disappear.*
*Nothing remains but the light leaking from the tank as I replay the conversation in my mind.*
*My image of the director has completely changed, but he could have never said something like that in front of Sayo. Even Mari looks like she has something she wants to say, but she just bites her lower lip and holds it in.*
Sayo: "Horrible... Papa, you're the worst."
*Sayo is the first to put her feelings into words. I guess I should have said something after all.*
Mayu: "His way of thinking was... completely wrong. Still, that was no reason to kil--"
Mari: "Big sister, it's not over yet."
*Mari says, breaking through my words, and points towards the desk.*
*An image once again appears in the light.*
*Similar to before, it's those two again. The director seems to have more grey hair this time around.*
Rieko: "Director, please take a look at this."
Director: "What is this?"
Rieko: "I have finalized the proposed budget for plans to alter the jellyfish booth. This is so we can change it to be more cognizant of the lighting, keeping true to the creation of a universe befitting the name Manten."
Rieko: "Ibaraki's aquarium is saying they are making their own alterations and plan to decrease the number of jellyfish, so we could cheaply inherit those jellyfish."
Rieko: "The architect we've requested is popular as the artist who designed the monument in front of Manten Station. Using connections through an acquaintance of mine, I plan to make use of him at a discount."
Rieko: "As a prerequisite, I would like your word that if this plan succeeds, we can continue to request alterations to Manten Aquarium. I anticipate better synergy than I saw with the plan of mine you rejected two years ago."
Rieko: "Although it's just fine if you don't think about it at all. Just giving your stamp of approval is also more than enough."
*This Saginuma Rieko's tone of voice is different from the previous one's.*
*In those two years since she spoke to him, something must have happened to increase her confidence.*
Director: "What nonsense. It's precisely because I've guided us towards appealing to children that our customer base has increased in the last two years."
Director: "Bringing your plan back into play now would only put us in the red once more. So what, you just want to treat the living creatures we obtained like cheap objects?"
Rieko: "The money we lost on the addition to the stage was recouped by my plan to collaborate with talent agencies. Do not forget that."
Director: "The one who decided to make an addition to the stage was me. It was due to my intuition that we experienced success. Do I need to repeat myself? Your plan is rejected. From here on, we focus on additions to the Deep-sea Fish booth."
Rieko: "Even if you reject it, all of the other employees are in agreement. Under the circumstances, even if you don't approve it, my stamp would be more than enough. In order for you to save face, Director Sakuragi, I am humbly requesting your approval."
Director: "How conceited. I've always thought you were too brazen, ever since you came here on an internship in graduate school."
Director: "You had no real business management experience, yet you somehow find nothing but fault with Manten Aquarium, to say nothing of the fact that you decided to present it all to me in the form of a report."
Rieko: "You have a point about my position as a graduate student at the time, but should I not tell you when it's relevant?"
Director: "Even if you had been an employee, the proper procedure is to address these things one by one with management... I'm at my limits with you. Listen, Manten Aquarium is my aquarium. Commit that to memory."
Rieko: "My... Didn't you say something about paying respect to living creatures? You seem to be contradicting yourself."
Director: "I'm not making any sort of contradiction. It's because of your impudence that you can't seem to marry!"
*Without even reading it, the director tears up the plan in his hands and throws it away.*
Director: "At any rate, I will reject that plan every time you bring it to me. Do you have anything else you need to say to me?"
*No matter what the director says to Saginuma Rieko, she boldly smiles back. Possibly because she's trying not to show the fear she feels inside, Mari grabs on to my fingers.*
*In contrast, Sayo silently watches the replay shown in the light without looking away.*
Rieko: "That's right, Director. It seems you've been taking the train to work lately."
Director: "Yes, that's right... The company car is in for repairs."
Rieko: "My, how unfortunate. There are some who speak badly of you, saying how you used funds to buy that car, but that seems to have quieted down, just as you might have hoped."
Director: "Did you come here to expel sarcasm? I received approval from accounting before making that purchase. There's nothing to blame me for."
Rieko: "Indeed. We mustn't forget to get the approval of our management and accounting. And you have a child, so you have need of a car."
Director: "That's enough. If you have time for such idle chatter, you have time to bring me a plan I can actually agree to."
Rieko: "Yes, yes. About half a month ago, I was on my way home from work, walking down the street at night, when I was almost hit by a car."
Rieko: "That late at night, they were likely pressing their luck by speeding. Luckily I was able to avoid them and they lightly hit a telephone pole, but it was just barely."
Rieko: "With there being no people out so late, it could have been bad if I were involved in a hit-and-run."
Director: "Y-Yes..."
Rieko: "It was dark, so I couldn't see the license plate number, but I knew the model of the car. It was... yes, yes, a foreign sports car."
Director: "That's because it's a fast car. There are probably lots of idiots who drive fast late at night..."
Rieko: "I believe that it was about half a month ago when you started taking the train, wasn't it, Director?"
Director: "Do you mean to say that I did it?"
Rieko: "Don't be absurd. I was merely seeing if you had any thoughts, Director. I wouldn't dare bear such doubts in my heart."
Director: "Get out... Get out now!"
Rieko: "My pleasure. Director Sakuragi, you should also be careful on the streets at night... Heehee..."
*The projection on the light begins to fade with Saginuma Rieko's unsettling laughter, finally disappearing altogether.*
*The remnants of the light become droplets and are drawn into the ceiling.*
*Until the last of it disappears, a silence remains over the three of us.*
Mayu: "That's... unacceptable!"
*Unable to bare it any longer, I'm the first to open my mouth.*
Mayu: "The director probably did try to run Rieko-san over. He may have even killed her, but if hate is returned with hate, the cycle will never end."
Mayu: "Anger can beget nothing but anger, hatred can beget nothing but hatred... and malice will be returned with nothing but malice. At that rate, people can do nothing but argue and fight."
Sayo: "Well, Mayumi, if that Himeno girl were killed, would you be able to go on without hating, for example, the one who created this Manten Aquarium, my papa?"
Mayu: "I..."
*Trying to think of a rebuttal I shut my mouth, but Sayo won't let me off that easily.*
Sayo: "Poison is something that exists to protect one's own body. Those dark feelings people direct at one another are the same."
Sayo: "Because people know that someone else carries that poison known as anger or hate, they basically won't come any closer than necessary, nor will they attack."
Sayo: "Still, once one bares their fangs, there's nothing left but to fight. In order to protect oneself, they'll return poison with poison. In that case, it won't end until someone dies."
Mari: "Sayo-chan, that's scary."
*Mari says this in a serious tone, but Sayo doesn't look in her direction.*
*She merely bites her lip slightly.*
*I pat Mari's head and say to Sayo.*
Mayu: "There is the... possibility of a light that can end the chain of hatred... Right, Mari?"
Mari: "Big sister, what do you mean?"
Mayu: "Nothing, I'm just saying how great you are, Mari!"
Mari: "I don't get it at all, but I got complimented, yay~!"
*Seeing her innocently cheered up so easily, I smile a bit.*
Mayu: "But I wonder what was up with that projection earlier."
*We proceed further into the director's office.*
*There's no projector or anything of the sort.*
*There's no explanation but that, just like the impression I got, the images were projected on the light leaking out of the tank.*
Sayo: "Who knows? You said you wanted to see this truth with me, so maybe the aquarium reacted to that desire, or something like that. No matter how ugly or terrible it is, we have reached the truth."
Sayo: "All we have to do now is find Papa. If we want to bring an end to this world, we have to end the curse."
*The Red Manten Aquarium will disappear... That's probably what's "on the other side" of the truth Sayo seeks.*
*At that time, what will happen to Mari?*
*I am unable to give voice to the questions floating about in my heart. I'm sure this girl will easily give us those answers.*
Rieko: "What a shame. What you've arrived at is nothing more than facts... We still hold the truth in our hands."
Mayu: "Saginuma Rieko...!"
*Saginuma Rieko stands at the entrance to the room, the door having been left open.*
*Having seen her argument with the director, my animosity toward her has softened.*
*Even murderers have reasons. I can't be sure, but could the reason that she didn't directly lay a hand on Mari mean that she was trying to protect her?*
Mayu: "I was just thinking that I wanted to have a little chat with you."
Rieko: "My... What an honor."
Sayo: "You jerk. Stop acting so high and mighty and tell us about this truth."
Mari: "No, that is her, but... it isn't her!"
Rieko: "That whole projection was true. In light of my confrontations with management, the director tried to settle things by taking my life, since there was no place for him anymore."
Rieko: "That's why I... decided to kill him."
*At Saginuma Rieko's words, the temperature in the room drops.*
*The air becomes so tense at those few words that it's almost painful on the surface of my skin.*
*No matter what truth she might be hiding, I'm sure that her murderous intent is pure.*
Hiyoshi: "Mayu-chan, Sayo-chan, run away! Leave this to me..."
Mayu: "Kenji-san..."
*The voice continues from behind Saginuma Rieko.*
*It's a frantic voice, but a powerful and dependable one.*
Mayu: "How do you know Sayo's name...?"
*Kenji-san's eyebrow twitches.*
Hiyoshi: "That's because I heard you guys call her that..."
Rieko: "That's enough, Kenji-kun. There's been a change in strategy."
*A silence flows through the room.*
*By my own discovery, the final line has been crossed.*
*But I can't fall apart here. I have Mari by my side.*
Rieko: "You've gotten so big, Sayo-chan. My, didn't I say so earlier? You've done a nice job surviving."
Sayo: "Shut your wrinkled mouth. We know that you two are working together."
Rieko: "Insult me all you like, but I wonder if that will be enough to absolve your hatred?"
*Kenji-san walks to Saginuma Rieko's side and loudly cracks his neck.*
*The three of us are facing the two of them head on.*
Mari: "Big sister, what do we do? Those people were looking for us, right? They had the same look... when they killed me!"
Mayu: "It's all right, it'll all be fine."
*I place my hand on Mari's head, and I can feel that she's shaking.*
*She's afraid.*
*It's not impossible that Mari would remember the moment she was killed. When she saw them, she probably remembered the fear she felt back then.*
Mayu: "Anyway... there are too many things we don't understand. Please tell us! Like, when you pushed Saginuma Rieko, Kenji-san, you were trying to save us, weren't you?"
Rieko: "Kenji-san... you said. Heehee, how funny."
Mayu: "What is?!"
Sayo: "Mayumi, calm down. It's not good for you to let her rile you up."
*Just as Sayo says this, I glare at Saginuma Rieko.*
Rieko: "Even though she's realized she completely misjudged you, this girl continues to trust you."
Hiyoshi: "Well, that's not impossible. That's what I led her to believe. Didn't you say we needed to restore that one piece of the aquarium?"
Sayo: "What do you mean?"
*Saginuma Rieko responds to Sayo's question by snickering.*
*As if he's mimicking her, Kenji-san also snickers. The pair's actions are frighteningly similar.*
Mari: "Big sister, something's not right after all."
Mayu: "I know. This isn't... the Kenji-san I know!"
Mari: "No, that's not what I meant. The two of them are weird, but they're the same! They're fuzzy!"
Hiyoshi: "Mari-chan, so you've realized it after all."
*Hearing him speak so lightly to her, Mari glares hard at him.*
