*All that can be heard are her footsteps. I don't turn around because, if I do, I'll surely chase after her.*
*Finally, even her footsteps fade away.*
*I can hear the sound of rippling waves.*
*As I walk along, I am enveloped in light.*
*Within that gentle, transparent blue light...*
*My consciousness begins to return, and I can feel how hard it is to breathe.*
Himeno: "... I......"
*There is pressure on my neck. My breathing is becoming shallow.*
Himeno: "... On... e..."
*Something is pushing on my throat with great strength. The flow through my carotid artery is being stopped up.*
*Despite the fact that I'm finally waking up, my consciousness is starting to waver again.*
*I wonder what is happening to me right now.*
*I open my eyes in thin slits, and a faint shadow floats in my vision.*
*Long hair. An outline that I've seen before. Soft-looking skin.*
*Slender arms are stretching out toward my neck. Putting all their strength into wringing my neck.*
*When I realize who it is, the words I couldn't understand before become clear, as if a fog has been lifted.*
Himeno: "Go on and die."
*The one wringing my neck is Himeno.*
*Trying to grab my neck with both of her hands, I can feel the pressure of her weight.*
*Even as she wrings my neck, she has a frantic look.*
*The proof is also in the fact that she doesn't kill me, even in my weakened state.*
Mayu: "Wh... y... are... you?"
*... Is this not Himeno?*
*Because of the pressure being applied to my throat, I can't finish saying what I wanted to say.*
*Himeno looks down at me with her blue eyes.*
*That isn't the color of Himeno's eyes.*
Himeno: "How... did... you... wake up?"
*Her voice raises doubts in my mind. Still, I clearly grasp that this is not Himeno.*
*With just that small change in tone, I get it. Since I'm her best friend, after all.*
*Well then, what is going on here?*
Himeno: "Nakanobe Mari has gained the ability to withdraw power from Manten Aquarium. This is because she maintains her individuality while her soul is still connected to the collective Manten Aquarium."
Himeno: "I attempted to seize the power she had amassed. However, her power was so great that I could not force her to yield to me."
Himeno: "Since she was so pure, tainting her was quite simple. In order to create a world with only the two of you, that child squandered all of the power here."
Himeno: "Power... within this Manten Aquarium, that's souls, or we could call it life energy as well. If it is the thing keeping this world stable, then if it disappears, wouldn't the world itself begin to crumble?"
Mayu: "Y... o... u... are..."
*Somehow I maintain my fading consciousness and manage to desperately summon some sort of voice.*
*As I listen to whatever it is that has taken on Himeno's appearance, I realize that I've heard this tone of voice before.*
*As soon as that person's face appears in my mind, my feeling changes to certainty.*
Himeno: "Sayo-chan... what a pity. In order for me to continue to exist, I had to use her as a source of nourishment."
*... Saginuma Rieko.*
*Just as I realize it, there is no strength left in my body.*
*Due to my shallow breathing, there is no way I can make any brash movements. With no blood flowing to my brain, my thoughts are equally unfocused.*
Himeno: "I was able to find this body merely by chance. However, with this body, destruction is unacceptable."
Himeno: "My final hope is you. When you escaped from Nakanobe Mari's birdcage, it was as if a miracle had occurred and I was very happy."
*Mari's birdcage.*
*That's right, intuitively, it is like that. Being locked away for one's own good.*
*But it's different. That was the world of Mari's heart.*
*Of course it was sad and lonely. She's been alone for five years.*
*All she could do was continue to pray, and to make the ungrantable wish that she would see me again.*
*I want to reject Saginuma Rieko's words. However, my body won't allow me to do so.*
*I think I'm getting close to my limits.*
*Little by little, I'm unable to keep my eyes open...*
Himeno: "When the world of the birdcage disappeared, that energy itself did not. All of it... flowed into you."
Himeno: "Perhaps Nakanobe Mari has already disappeared. I no longer sense her presence, and if she were here, she would try to interfere."
Himeno: "When the two of you parted ways in that world, surely she gave you her energy."
Himeno: "That's why... I'll kill you, and then I will obtain that energy... along with your body!"
*I have no idea if she gave me her energy. What she did entrust me with was her gentle wish.*
*But at this rate, I won't be able to keep my promise to Mari.*
*At this rate, I'll die.*
*... If Saginuma Rieko's words are true.*
*No, surely this is real. If it weren't, there'd be no need for her to kill me in this situation.*
*There's no mistaking the fact that the only reason I'm not dead is because she hasn't delivered the finishing blow yet.*
Mayu: "I'm... going...... home..."
*At that moment, I decide to believe in myself.*
*I close my eyes and surrender to my fading consciousness.*
*I did it.*
*As I look at the small palm of my hand, I confirm this.*
*In lieu of a mirror, I look at my body reflected in the tank before me.*
*It's one of the cylindrical tanks in the jellyfish booth, and it clearly depicts my entire body.*
*Nakanobe Mayumi's body.*
*Compared to Tamagawa Himeno, she's strikingly less sturdy, but reflexes and firmness aren't important at this time.*
Mayu: "Ahahahahah...! Hahahahahahahaha!"
*This is a feeling I haven't experienced until now.*
*The sense of completeness throughout a body.*
*To use an example, it's a feeling close to the pleasure of having sex. Being able to derive pleasure from sex in the body of a virgin, though, is something of a contradiction.*
*... I have the feeling that something other than myself is encroaching upon me.*
*All that's different is, rather than a climax, there is the feeling of being able to exercise one's own power.*
Mayu: "With this girl, I am certain I'll be able to expand the Red Manten Aquarium..."
*She bears the emotional strength to support the foreign contaminant flowing through her body. That is... her strong will to get home with her friend.*
*In Nakanobe Mayumi's case, she is likely undergoing a time of great emotional maturation.*
Mayu: "Well then, there's no time to be fooling around..."
*Using the strength that Nakanobe Mayumi -- my body -- has accumulated, I shall reconstruct Manten Aquarium. I need no further plan than that.*
*It only needs to be one area. I'll switch my location to that in the real world. Finite, yet long, I will maintain my soul with the grudge as my storage.*
*I close my eyes.*
*My pose is irrelevant.*
*My intuition is telling me a piece of me must cross the aquarium using this power alone.*
*When I was merely a soul, that was simple. Rather, it was merely a hardship necessary for self-preservation.*
*Well then, I will make my wish.*
*For the world that is best for me.*
*... A world without the likes of you.*
Mayu: "Huh..."
*At last, I open my eyes. I hear a voice from somewhere.*
Mayu: "Where is that...?!"
*... You should know already.*
Mayu: "Do you mean the soul is listening...?"
*Not from the ears.*
*It isn't that I hear a voice. It's more of a cognizance.*
*... You are already dead. No matter how hard you wish, that fact will not change.*
Mayu: "Which is precisely why I told you I am using this body...!"
*... Mari entrusted me with her wish. I swore to her that I would grant that wish.*
*... What is it you want to do in the real world? What is your unfinished business?*
Mayu: "To live."
Mayu: "Merely to continue existing in this world. To extend my hand into eternity."
*... If that's the case, then you as a person are already dead.*
*... I think there's no meaning in living merely for the sake of living. If you have a heart, you have a place to call home.*
Mayu: "For example, though her life was like the blink of an eye, Mari lived with all she had, never letting go of her feelings."
*... M-My control has...*
Mayu: "I was waiting for the moment when you would try to unify yourself with the aquarium. At that moment, I was sure cracks would appear in your heart."
Mayu: "I am what Mari has entrusted to this world. Therefore, I will now make my wish."
Mayu: "Please disappear."
*... I will hate you with all my life.*
Mayu: "Isn't that more human anyway?"
*I think that the foreign substance within my body has definitely disappeared.*
*As I can no longer hear that voice.*
Mayu: "I am sorry."
*Turning towards the empty space, I mumble to no one.*
*I am standing in the tunnel tank room.*
*This room is all that remains in this world. In this world already destroyed by Saginuma Rieko, it seems she only left one area.*
*She said it was reconstruction, so this area will probably disappear soon as well.*
*Himeno and Sayo have already disappeared. It's not that they died.*
*If they had died, their bodies would remain. They just DISAPPEARED.*
Mayu: "So, it's all right."
*Now that Saginuma Rieko has disappeared, I can feel some sort of different power.*
*This is... what Mari entrusted to me.*
*Mari had held onto it, in the hopes that it would make it to the end.*
Mayu: "Just one, please, a miracle."
*I say in a small voice, and I close my eyes.*
Mayu: "Haha... Hahaha......"
*At that moment, I laugh.*
*How did things end up like this? I have no idea what caused it.*
*All that I can say is, this means I was unable to create a miracle.*
Mayu: "Himeno..."
*I speak to Himeno, who sleeps next to me.*
*There is no response.*
Mayu: "Sayo..."
*I speak to Sayo, who lies on the ground.*
*There is no reaction.*
Mayu: "How did it end up like this...?"
*There is flesh.*
*There is space as well.*
*If it goes as Kenji-san said, at this rate, if we reach the exit, we could return to the real world.*
*It's just, there is no heart.*
*This isn't a corpse.*
*There is a fever. There is body heat. When I place my ear to the surface of her body, I can hear vibrations.*
*She's like an intricate doll.*
Mayu: "Hahahahaha..."
*I laugh.*
*Having no clue what else to do, I can do nothing but laugh.*
*I'm already broken.*
Mayu: "Haha..."
*This is the end of me.*
*The three of us rot away together, as good friends do.*
Mari: "Hey, big sister."
*... In this dark world, I hear a voice.*
*I open my eyes in the hopes that something has changed.*
*Nothing has changed in reality, with Himeno and Sayo still lying at my side with their eyes closed.*
*Nothing is being shown to me visually.*
Mari: "Big sister, from the bottom of your heart, you wanted me to come back too, didn't you?"
Mari: "Because of that, in the end, I was able to see you again, just for a little bit."
Mari: "I feel just a little happiness."
*I can hear a tiny little voice.*
*I can hear it from far away, like the sound of rippling waves.*
*My eyes still closed, I strain my ears.*
Mari: "This time really is 'bye-bye.'"
Mari: "Surely, sometime--"
*No longer able to hear the voice, I don't open my eyes.*
*The moment I fall asleep feels more like taking a nap, but it's a pleasant feeling.*
Himeno: "Whew, I bought them, I bought them! That necktie was so cute, and I found a strawberry-scented candle, and..."
Mayu: "You really did buy a lot... You won't get in trouble?"
Himeno: "It's fine, it's fine! Ah, that's right, we haven't looked at shoes yet today. Well, let's go!"
Mayu: "H-Hey, wait a second, Himeno... I'm a little tired. How about we eat some ice cream and take a break?"
*It's been one week since then -- Himeno and I are visiting Manten East Building again. My mother was a bit huffy about me going out for a second week in a row, but I got my way in the end.*
*Even my nagging mother was surprised that I wouldn't back down, but I guess I've changed a little bit.*
Himeno: "It's all right if we get ice cream... but don't mess up your stomach like you did last week, okay?"
Mayu: "I told you I'm fine already!"
*After a week has passed, that terrifying aquarium is like a memory or a dream, and we have returned to a relatively unchanging daily life.*
*That day, I eventually did eat three scoops of cookies and cream and my stomach started to hurt, so I couldn't go shopping with Himeno after all.*
*As compensation, we came here to get our fill of fun at the aquarium.*
*... That's right, Manten Aquarium.*
*There's a reason we came, without hesitation, to this place stained with trauma.*
*I don't want this to be a place filled with only bad memories.*
*This is the place I last played with Mari.*
*The place where I met Sayo, the place where we became friends.*
*The place where it was etched into my heart, just how important Himeno is to me...*
*There are a lot of painful memories, but on the other hand, it's a place I really love.*
Mayu: "Well, to relax... should we head on in... to the aquarium?"
Himeno: "Mayu, aren't your legs still a bit too tired?"
Mayu: "It's fine! I'm... totally fine."
Himeno: "All right, then let's go!"
*Carrying her shopping bags with both hands, Himeno energetically heads for the elevator.*
*Since we've been walking for a bit, the cool breeze from the air conditioning is a nice feeling. The window through which one can see all of Manten gets me excited. An upbeat song I wouldn't normally listen to is playing in the background.*
*Manten East Building is totally fascinating.*
*But the deep blue of Manten Aquarium is incredibly soothing.*
*The beauty of the water and the light.*
*The pulse of life and of the future.*
Himeno: "What are you doing, Mayu?! Let's go!"
Mayu: "Okay!"
*I speed up to catch up to Himeno, who has gone on ahead.*
*From here on, many beautiful things await me in the future. If I try my best, I can overcome all of the challenges awaiting me.*
*That's why this is... my farewell.*
*Surely, this is my sound of drops...*
*A quiet rain.*
*Pitter pat, pitter pat.*
*The drops make a small sound as they land on the girl's umbrella.*
*The droplets of water bursting open mixes with the scent of the trees lining the street and the asphalt, making Himeno, who is taking deep breaths, aware of the smell of rain.*
*She had thought it would be a good idea to come on a day there was no club, but today just happens to be a rainy day.*
*Since it would have been a simple training and meeting, Himeno would have been done and heading home within an hour.*
*Turning down her clubmates' invitation, Himeno headed for Manten on her own.*
*She has plans with Mayu again this weekend, so today is her only chance to come alone.*
*In order to distinguish herself, to take action as Mayu's best friend.*
*Himeno finds a flower shop she read about in a magazine, and closing her umbrella, heads inside.*
*There are several flowers reminiscent of the summer season -- cute, yellow safflowers and hydrangeas adorned with the melancholic color of rain.*
*Even though Himeno enjoys visiting different shops, she has little experience with buying flowers.*
*With such a sweet smell wafting through the small store, Himeno decides to come here again with Mayu.*
Himeno: "Um~ I have a question about these flowers..."
*Having finished her shopping, Himeno once again walks through the rainy Manten. Her destination is none other than Manten East Building.*
*As she had thoroughly checked the map yesterday, she is able to reach her destination without issue.*
*Heading through the lobby that is crowded whether it's raining or not, Himeno rides the elevator.*
*Manten quickly shrinks away from Himeno's sight.*
*Going through the entrance, Himeno buys a "student" ticket.*
*The lady at the reception desk is the same one that was there yesterday, with her dark brown hair pulled back in a bun.*
*Her lips, lightly painted with pink lipstick, form a smile, and she asks, "You came yesterday as well, didn't you? You're alone today?"*
*Seeing a junior high schooler alone is probably a rarity. Himeno is pleased that the lady remembers her face.*
Himeno: "Yes! It was really fun, so I'm here again today!"
Lady: "Thank you so much. Well then, please enjoy your visit."
Himeno: "Of course!"
*Returning the lady's smile, Himeno politely declines the year-long pass the lady has offered her. Visiting more than three times in one year earns a free visit, but it would be better to get the year-long pass when Mayu is with her.*
*Himeno passes her ticket through the ticket gate and once again enters Manten Aquarium.*
*As it's already evening, most of the customers are adults, particularly couples.*
Himeno: "How nice..."
*A boyfriend says "I'm sorry," to his girlfriend, after being over an hour late due to working overtime. Although this has left her in a foul mood, as they walk around the aquarium, a smile slowly overtakes her whole face.*
*Finally they end their day at a restaurant with a view of the town, clinking their wine glasses.*
*While aspiring to such an adult romance, Himeno sighs at the notion of having no such prospects in the present.*
*Of course, after staring into the tanks for awhile, one loses awareness of the other customers. Manten Aquarium is one little universe that Himeno is familiar with.*
*After taking a brief look around, Himeno leaves the aquarium.*
*Though Himeno thinks she'll head on after a meal at a cafe on the second floor, she ends up having reservations about going in alone and, distracted by her hunger, gets a smoothie made from dark leafy greens from the juice bar.*
*Once again riding the elevator, she heads for Manten Aquarium. The night sky visible from the elevator reminds her of another starry sky.*
Himeno: "Huh, it's just nothing but the cosmos."
*Putting the name Manten to shame, Himeno sees the starry sky in various places. In the aquarium and the night sky... even in golden nail polish and in aromatherapy candles. But the real vastness of the starry sky is something Himeno still doesn't know.*
*However, Himeno isn't worried about such a thing. Each day spent with her best friend sparkles, and Himeno now knows how precious that is from the bottom of her heart.*
Himeno: "Uh, I forgot something, haha~"
*Manten Aquarium is now closing. All of the customers are heading for the elevator.*
*Heading for the lady who is about to close the reception area shutter, Himeno calls out to her with a pained smile. It's the same lady with the dark brown hair.*
Himeno: "I'll come right back, so may I please go get it?"
Lady: "You forgot something...? Half of the lights have been turned out, shall I accompany you?"
Himeno: "N-No, that's all right. I'm coming here tomorrow as well!"
Lady: "Ahaha, I see. Well then, we're closing up, so please hurry back."
*Himeno quickly passes through the ticket gate, exits the Fish of Tokyo Bay booth, and heads for the tunnel tank.*
*Just as the lady said, the inside is much darker than it was in the middle of the day.*
Himeno: "... I'm here."
*Himeno stands at the center of the tunnel tank and rests her hand on the tank glass.*
*It isn't as if there is anyone else around, but Himeno looks up and speaks in a gentle voice.*
Himeno: "You probably can't hear me anymore, but I have something I think I should say."
*... Mari-chan.*
*In the blink of an eye, Himeno is brought to tears, her voice shaking.*
Himeno: "It's my fault Mayu had to face something so scary. I didn't know anything about her past... I hadn't even tried to... Even though I talked so much about ghosts and all... I could have never imagined..."
*Himeno leans on the tank, looking down.*
*If she doesn't, her body will give out completely. It seems as if the strength to hold up her body is leaving in turn with her overflowing emotions.*
Himeno: "Since you were there, Mari-chan, we were able to come back. I was able to make it out... without losing something precious to me..."
*As she says this, Himeno pulls a flower from a shopping bag. It is a blue rose.*
Himeno: "It's only one, but it holds... my feelings..."
*Leaving flowers here would, of course, be in the way of others. But she at least wants to get her feelings across. So, just one flower.*
Himeno: "Thank you so very much, Mari-chan. Somehow, rest in peace..."
*Her words serve to accompany the flowers, then Himeno puts her hands together.*
*It is probably only several seconds that pass in real time.*
*However, time is not the problem. For Himeno, it is the depth of what fills that time, of her very precious sentiment.*
Himeno: "...... Okay, I'll probably get in trouble."
*Himeno wipes away the tears that have gathered at the corners of her eyes and jogs toward the exit.*
*She never hears any sort of reply. Therefore, this is probably nothing more than her satisfying herself.*
*However, this is Himeno's farewell... and her decision.*
*A farewell to her best friend's little sister, and a resolution to properly watch over Mayu and to support her.*
*Saying thank you to the lady at the reception desk, Himeno heads for the elevator once more.*
Himeno: "Someday, we'll--"
*Grateful that she is alone in the elevator, Himeno gazes up at the nightscape and murmurs.*
*Tomorrow, life will go on as it always has. However, for Himeno, she'll begin to move forward just a bit each day...*
Mayu: "Huh, this is..."
*Staggering, we come out of the gate.*
*Everywhere we look, people, people, people... Customers and employees, there are innumerable amounts of people.*
*Fish are only swimming within the tanks, the color of the water isn't red, and I can't sense any abnormality anywhere.*
Himeno: "Mayu... this is it, isn't it?"
Mayu: "Yes... we've come back. We were able to get back."
*As my eyes settle on the information desk at the entrance, a sudden wave of relief washes over me.*
*Too much has happened since we came here. Himeno and I may have some minor injuries, but we've returned to the real world more or less unscathed.*
*I can't take anymore -- I'm so happy.*
Mayu: "Himeno... we're back..."
*Exhausted, I can no longer hold back everything welling up inside me.*
*My eyes suddenly feel hot, and large droplets overflow from them.*
Mayu: "W-Wahhh... I'm so glad..."
Himeno: "H-Hey Mayu, if you cry in a place like this..."
*I know.*
*The people around us have no way of knowing what we've been through.*
*I'm just a junior high schoolgirl who suddenly started to cry. I'll definitely be seen as a strange girl.*
Himeno: "Uh, wanna find some place on the edge of the room? I'll go buy some cocoa."
Mayu: "No... let's go together. I don't want to be apart..."
Himeno: "Okay, okay. You're such a baby, Mayu-chan."
Mayu: "You idiot...!"
*We sit in the rest area, and Himeno pats my back.*
*Just as I start to calm down, I glance at my watch.*
*According to the time, it's about eight o'clock at night.*
*Our whole ordeal wasn't even a full eight hours.*
*Even so, it was the most densely packed period of time in my life.*
Himeno: "Say, Mayu..."
Mayu: "What's up, Himeno?"
Himeno: "I love you!"
*Saying this in a conspicuously loud voice, Himeno winks at me.*
Mayu: "Hey, that's a bit sudden."
*Averting my eyes, I push Himeno away, but I can't keep a smile off my face.*
*I don't know which of us is more embarrassed.*
*I wanted to protect this smile -- and I did. A bubbling warmth begins to boil over from within.*
Mayu: "... I feel the same."
Himeno: "Well then, let's go get some ice cream!"
Mayu: "N-Now? Won't we be late getting home...?"
Himeno: "It's all right, I can help you with your excuse!"
Mayu: "In that the case... I'll go shopping with you."
*Having pulled my hand away from Himeno, I board the elevator. The me up to this point would probably have just gone on home.*
*Coming home late once in awhile is probably okay. Right now, I want to live in this very precious moment.*
*Manten Aquarium.*
*That place that takes up no more than one corner of Manten East Building, is somewhere I can never forget.*
*A sea of stars. A river of lights. Everyone is everyone, just one of those lights.*
*Twinkling frantically, the light of life.*
*It doesn't matter if it's fish or people. For both, it's an equally harsh world.*
*Meetings and farewells.*
*A resolution to overcome the farewell that pierces my heart.*
*My heart drips endlessly, just a powerless little drop.*
*However, that sound is surely resonating somewhere.*
*The first day of summer break. I'm leaning against a pillar in Manten Station.*
*Of course, the reason is because I'm waiting for someone. Having come before the appointed meet-up time, I'm spacing out standing against the third pillar from the left.*
*Doing this makes me remember that day. The day I visited Manten Aquarium, the day of farewell, where I faced destiny.*
*It makes sense, but beginnings are always so normal.*
*There are five minutes until our meeting time.*
*At that time, Himeno came early, but I wonder how today will go. Come to think of it, this is our first time going out since then.*
*Just as I lose awareness of my surroundings, my memories are brought back as if in reaction to my situation.*
*The world of fear and horror, the Red Manten Aquarium. We're talking about a world that's long gone, but right now, I'm being clearly reminded of it.*
*That's right, it's...*
Mayu: "Kyaah!"
*In an instant, my vision is dyed pitch black.*
*Even though my eyes are open, I can't see a thing. Something cold to the touch is touching my face. It's become hard to breathe.*
*However, I don't feel afraid. Rather, I feel irritated.*
Mayu: "Hi~me~no~!"
Himeno: "Hehehe~ That's the second time~!"
*Himeno pulls away the hand that had covered my eyes and mouth, and suddenly swings around in front of me, holding up two fingers and laughing.*
*I respond by puffing up my cheeks at Himeno, who seems to be enjoying this from the bottom of her heart. She's right on time, so I guess her prank is her way of making things even.*
Mayu: "Geez, you're so mean. You scared me."
Himeno: "How do I put it? That's a staple of my routine."
Mayu: "You know this isn't good for my heart..."
*Today, Himeno is wearing a pink one-piece dress and an emerald green cardigan. It's the first time I've seen her in these clothes, so it may be that she got them especially for this day.*
*Along similar lines, I also actually have brand new clothes on today. They're very summery, and the color matches well with my beloved boot sandals, so they're perfect for going out.*
*Himeno, of course, seems to have noticed, and we talk for some time.*
*During our conversation, my eyes drift to the big Boston bag Himeno is once again carrying.*
Mayu: "Himeno, why do you still have that bag?"
*Himeno puffs up her chest with pride and begins fishing around inside of it.*
Himeno: "You se~e..."
Mayu: "Ah, geez, it's fine! Could it be that you wanna include that in your routine too?"
Himeno: "Oh, have I been found out?"
Mayu: "Geez, Himeno... Speaking of which, earlier you said something about 'being faster than me,' what was that about?"
Himeno: "Hmm, you wanna know, Mayu-chan...?"
Mayu: "When you say it like that, it piques my curiosity!"
Himeno: "But I can't tell you yet~ You're not ready, Mayu."
Mayu: "Aw..."
Himeno: "Geez, don't get mad at me. Next week, I pro~mise I'll tell you!"
Mayu: "Why's that? I'm gonna wanna know today."
*Next week, then it'll have been a whole month that she's been stalling, and now she says I have to wait another week. What's the point in putting it off for that long?*
*Having thought that far, I place my hand to my mouth with an, "Ah."*
*When I have a hunch, my face immediately becomes hot. From Himeno's point of view, I'm sure there's mistaking the redness in my face.*
*Next week... That's my birthday.*
*I start to say, then close my mouth. Himeno looks at me, somewhat bashfully. She's really not good with surprises and the like...*
*Rather than being annoying, I find this to be so like Himeno and charming.*
*When our eyes meet once again, we both become strangely embarrassed.*
Miku: "Well, well, what a pleasant mood. Sayo-chan, shall we get them to let us join in?"
Sayo: "Huh? I don't get it at all."
Miku: "You're not very earnest."
Mayu: "Sayo! Miku-san!"
*Their timing is matched with the arrival of a train. Almost in sync with the flow of people, the two of them arrive. Our meeting time, on the dot.*
Sayo: "Hello."
Miku: "You can leave out the honorifics. We're all Sayo's friends and all, and I'm calling you Mayumi-chan, after all."
*When I greet her, Miku always returns my greeting in a relaxed tone.*
Himeno: "Miku-chan, long time, no see!"
Miku: "Himeno-chan, it's been almost a month."
Sayo: "You two are getting on well."
Mayu: "Sayo, you too."
Sayo: "Tch."
*That brusque way of clicking her tongue at what I say hasn't changed. It hasn't been more than a month leading up to today, the first day of summer break, so it's not likely for her to change that much.*
*The four us together is also rare, if I do say so myself.*
*From the incident at the Red Manten Aquarium onward, before we realized it, we had become friends. In place of Sayo, who doesn't care for emails or phone calls, Miku has served as the go-between, leading to the creation of the plan for us to hang out today.*
*I quickly became friends with the self-paced, sociable Miku. I felt it was somewhat wondrous that even I could become friends so easily with anyone, but even in class, I feel like more people are talking with me.*
Himeno: "Okay, let's go!"
Mayu: "Yeah!"
Sayo: "I haven't heard anything about what we're doing today though."
Miku: "Didn't I tell you we're doing karaoke?"
Sayo: "Wha-- Karaoke? I'll pass. I don't care for it."
Himeno: "What? Are you bad at singing?"
*As if she were waiting for it, Himeno was broadly grinning. When she talks with Sayo, Himeno is lively in a different way than she is in class.*
*According to Miku, in the beginning, Sayo had the upper hand, but after several days, there were signs that this was being reversed.*
Sayo: "I'm not bad."
Himeno: "Are you embarrassed?"
Sayo: "That's enough. It's fine if we go. I'll say this though, I don't know how this will turn out."
Mayu: "What does that mean...?"
*It's not like I come to karaoke frequently, but I know enough to know that it's not a place where something could happen.*
*At something said behind someone's back, Miku is grinning for some reason.*
*With Himeno at the helm, we finish checking in, order each of our drinks and a snack for us to share.*
*Sayo and I sit towards the middle, with Himeno at my side and Miku next to Sayo.*
*Just as we've finally settled down, it's Sayo who is the first to move. Nimbly operating the touch-pad remote, she leads the charge with the first song.*
*Standing up straight, she uses the loading time to face the microphone and chat.*
Sayo: "I am merely entering songs I like. Please add to them as you see fit."
*Once Sayo finishes saying this, intense drumming reverberates from the speakers.*
*A rock number in a genre I know nothing about is unfurling from the speakers. Maybe it's the same for Himeno.*
*Sayo begins to sing the first line just a little bit late. I have a feeling I know why Miku didn't tell her until the day of that we were going to karaoke.*
*As the ringleader of the cajoling, Himeno looks like she wants to say something.*
Miku: "I'll sing too~!"
*Taking a microphone from the table, Miku pops up.*
*Unexpectedly, considering her appearance, Miku apparently likes hard rock as well.*
*Sayo seems a little dissatisfied, but when the song starts, the both seem to be enjoying it.*
*Compared to Sayo, Miku is a good singer. She accurately sings even the most intense songs. I thought Himeno was a good singer, but this is even better.*
Himeno: "Hey, Mayu! I don't know how to get into this!"
*Just as the second melody ends, Himeno whispers in my ear. Even so, her voice is loud enough not to be drowned out by the microphone.*
Mayu: "I-I don't know either... I don't listen to this kind of music, either."
Himeno: "Right... Though it's not surprising that Sayo-chan and Miku like this type of song."
*Himeno and I have similar taste in music; both of us like pop music.*
*If we sing them in front of Sayo and Miku, they might get mad, but when neither of us chooses a song, Sayo puts another one on.*
*The band is different, but the song is the same.*
Mayu: "Anyway, should we throw up our hands?"
Himeno: "Agreed~! Let's thrust out our fists~!"
*Somehow I try to elevate the mood in the room, and it goes better than I expected.*
*Glancing at us, Miku starts to snicker.*
Miku: "You two wanna sing?"
Mayu: "Sayo is getting pretty pumped up..."
Himeno: "It seems wrong to interrupt!"
*Sayo immediately makes a displeased face and tosses the microphone.*
Sayo: "If you wanna sing, then just say so."
Mayu: "But I don't know this song."
Sayo: "It's fine, just sing."
Mayu: "Wha~!"
*Even as I push the mic back towards her, Sayo won't take it.*
*Maybe she didn't think that I wouldn't know this song.*
Miku: "Hmm~ Well then, Himeno-chan, you'll sing too?"
Himeno: "Nah, I don't know it either."
*In the end, the three of us end up in a shape similar to a game of Oshikura Manju.*
*Miku doesn't seem like she's completely enjoying it, though.*
*Despite the jumble and disorder, I really enjoy it.*
Sayo: "Tch, then what do you guys know?"
Mayu: "Well~ there was the theme to that recent detective drama..."
Himeno: "I like that song!"
Sayo: "I don't listen to those kind of songs."
Miku: "I know it."
Mayu: "Well, then the three of us can sing it!"
*Sayo sticks out her lip like she's pouting.*
*After that, it wasn't really karaoke in the traditional sense.*
*I found watching Himeno and Sayo's squabbles to be particularly charming.*
*During that time, Miku and I chat while drinking our beverages.*
*Since this is still during free time, we can stay as long as we want.*
*The fun times will continue on and on.*
*For example, even when today's done, my time's not up.*
*From here on, there will be many more good and bad times.*
*I'll probably have many more arguments with everyone.*
*I'll probably have times where I hate myself.*
*Even so...*
*In the end, I want to laugh and take it all in.*
*Because I think that first step, it's a happy one.*
Himeno: "Mayu, why are you spacing out?"
Sayo: "I can understand why with this song playing."
Miku: "There aren't enough mic's, but let's all sing together anyway."
Mayu: "... Right."
*When I stand up, a single drop trickles out and falls.*
*I was just reminiscing a bit on all that's happened.*
*Just one last drop, and then I can no longer hear the sound of them.*
*I try to sing with all my heart, and somehow, it feels really good.*
*On one holiday.*
*We're gathered at a coffee shop.*
*With no plans about where we'll go and no particular objective.*
*There's only the feeling that we want to get together.*
*It's a day off with no club activities for Himeno, so we all gather spontaneously.*
Himeno: "Hey, where should we go today?"
Sayo: "Anywhere."
Miku: "We went to karaoke last time, so anywhere's fine by me."
*Having finished our drinks, we're finishing lunch and sitting idly.*
*Even though it's a holiday, seats with customers in them are sparse, so there seems little need to leave hastily.*
Mayu: "... Manten Aquarium."
Himeno: "Hm?"
*Somehow even though I intentionally whispered it, Himeno asks me to repeat myself.*
*It's not as if I had a particular thought in mind.*
*As an only child, I don't have many memories of my family going out to do much other than eat.*
*We often come to Manten to have fun and have gone shopping in Manten East Building many times.*
*We've never gone to Manten Aquarium, even though it's in the same building. It mostly seems to attract people with families or couples, so we've avoided it.*
Sayo: "That sounds fine, doesn't it?"
*Sayo is the first to agree for a change.*
*Sayo always complains the longest. In the end, she's more like the chaperone we drag along.*
Miku: "If Sayo-chan's in, I'm in too."
*Miku says in a light tone.*
*Apparently she carelessly fell from her apartment balcony and was hospitalized some time ago.*
*When I heard that story and saw Miku laugh and say, "That was so dumb," I felt like she was just sweeping it away with carefree words and gave her a strained smile.*
Mayu: "How about you Himeno? Do you like Manten Aquarium?"
Himeno: "Hmph~"
*When I ask her, Himeno closes her eyes and looks down.*
Sayo: "Well, I'm fine with anywhere."
Himeno: "I totally agree~! It sounds fine, let's go! Hey, how come we didn't think of it until now?"
Mayu: "H-Hey, why are you grabbing on to me?"
*Himeno, sitting next to me, grabs on tight to me.*
Himeno: "When I was a kid, we always went to aquariums. I've seen Manten Aquarium in magazines, it looks fun!"
Sayo: "Then hurry up."
*With an unpleasant look on her face, Sayo bites down on her straw.*
Miku: "Sayo-chan, maybe we should hug too?"
Sayo: "You're nuts, aren't you?"
Miku: "You're so mean..."
*Himeno and I are a bit spread out.*
*I think to myself, she's so developed after all, I'm jealous.*
Himeno: "Okay, it's decided, let's go to the aquarium!"
Miku: "That's right. If we can, I wanna take my time looking around."
*Without giving any reply in particular, Sayo turns her gaze my way.*
*As the four of us chat, we head for Manten Aquarium.*
Miku: "Right, later."
Sayo: "Later."
Himeno: "Bye-bye. That was fun~"
Mayu: "See you later."
*The two of them are apparently heading to a live music club.*
*It's not that I'm not interested, just that it's a problem if I get home late, so Himeno and I decide to leave.*
*Those two love music, so after we hang out, they often go there on the way home. It seems they have a particular club they like.*
*I dimly think that the two of them seem so adult-like, probably from living alone.*
Himeno: "The aquarium was fun, eh~ It wasn't all that big, but the jellyfish place and some of the other booths were really pretty."
*Going to the aquarium for the first time in ten years was more fun than I expected.*
*So, it might be fun to go from time to time.*
Mayu: "Let's... go again."
Himeno: "Let's get a year-pass~ The case for it is cute too, after all."
*I was thinking the same thing. The ticket that lets you in as much as you want in a year also has a case with a really cute design.*
*The only problem is the price.*
Mayu: "Maybe with my New Year's present..."
*As we walk home making idle conversation, suddenly, the rain begins to fall.*
*It was sunny this morning, so I didn't watch the weather report, but it's a pretty heavy rain. I guess it's a sudden evening shower.*
Himeno: "Mayu, let's hurry!"
Mayu: "Right... Ahh."
*As I start to run, I spot a flower shop just ahead of me.*
*A faint light has filtered along this dim road.*
*Mysteriously, it catches my eye.*
Himeno: "A flower shop? They sure are open late. It's nearby though, so let's wait out the rain there."
*Himeno, who has followed my gaze, takes my arm and runs with me.*
Himeno: "Hello~ Ohh~ How pretty~"
*The interior of the store is overflowing with brightly colored flowers in full bloom.*
*Tulips and anemones... It's difficult to settle my eyes on any particular one.*
Mayu: "Ahhh..."
*My gaze stops on a single, eye-catching blue rose.*
*A blue rose that seems to be filled to the brim with sadness.*
*Mixed in with the red roses, it stands out, blooming like something from a dream.*
*I can't take my eyes off of it.*
*I don't understand the reason myself, but I can feel a single teardrop on my cheek.*
Himeno: "Mayu...?"
*The shopkeeper tells us the meaning of the blue rose in the language of flowers.*
*Miracles, blessings of the gods, making dreams come true -- and then, "Would you like one? Sounds like the shopkeeper is willing to lower the price."*
*I'm so taken by it, I forget to wipe away my tears.*
*Staring at it the whole time, a sense of nostalgia overflows from me.*
*Himeno waits for me the whole time. It seems like such a long time until we can no longer hear the intense sound of the rain.*
*I think that this flower may be tied to one of my earliest childhood memories.*
*I put the flower shop behind me.*
*Droplets of water drip from the awning above the entrance.*
*In the end, I didn't buy the blue rose.*
*I was going to buy it, but then lost any sense of what I had originally seen.*
*It's probably because it continues to bloom within my heart.*
Himeno: "Was it all right for you not to buy it?"
Mayu: "Yeah. I'm happy as I am. For now and always."
Himeno: "What's up with you being so earnest?"
*Himeno takes my hand as she smiles.*
Himeno: "We have so~ many fun things ahead of us. So we will always be happy."
Mayu: "Right."
Himeno: "Well, we'll probably have a lot of hardships too. Like entrance exams."
Mayu: "It's fine!"
*I can no longer hear the sound of drops.*
*We take a step out into the vast, clear sky...*
*Before she realized it, the girl was standing before an imposing door.*
*The girl occasionally came to Manten Aquarium alone. She watched the manta rays... she watched the jellyfish... and finally she decided it was time to go home.*
Miku: "Where is this...?"
*She had her doubts, but there should be nothing else there except for the aquarium. She assured herself that she had probably wandered off onto a side path.*
*The door before her had a large padlock hanging from it. Just as she had heard, it was unlocked and merely dangling there.*
Miku: "... Gulp."
*The girl drew in a breath.*
*Being locked would usually mean that it was a place she shouldn't enter. Being open just meant that a staff member had forgotten to lock it, so going in was out of the question.*
*Her curiosity piqued, she was past the point of persuasion. Her imagination ran wild and her mind raced at the prospect of what might lie beyond the door.*
Miku: "Just... for a little bit."
*She opened the door and saw lots of glass tanks.*
*Several types of fish hailing from the deep seas, from footballfish and oarfish to pelican eels and barreleyes, were swimming about.*
Miku: "Why isn't this an exhibit?"
*She thought it over, wondering why this would be in a closed-off area. Behind her, the door shut on its own.*
*The girl spaced out, staring at the deep-sea fish swimming about. They would leap from the tank and swim in mid-air.*
*The entirety of the room was the fish and their ocean. Though it was an unnatural occurance, the girl treated it as quite normal.*
Miku: "Hm, wasn't there originally a deep-sea fish booth that was shut down?"
*She suddenly remembered. At that moment, her surroundings were dyed a deep red.*
*The water in the tanks, the fluorescent lights, all of it was red. The girl's vision also became red--*
*When she opened her eyes, the girl was back in her own room. It was well into the night. She couldn't remember how she had gotten home after all of that.*
*But, more importantly, she had a bad feeling about something else.*
Miku: "It isn't... red here."
*She became aware of how hard it suddenly was to breathe. No matter how hard she gasped, she was unable to get oxygen into her body.*
*Like the fish who couldn't breathe anywhere but in the water, she couldn't breathe unless she was inside that red color.*
*She was convinced.*
*On impulse, she threw open the window, leaned out, and thrust a hand outside. She couldn't inhale the wind flooding her room. She could only smell its milky, unpleasant scent.*
Miku: "More--"
*At that moment, the girl's body was tossed into the air. For just a second, her body seemed to float like a fish in the sea.*
*Her room was on the 12th floor of her apartment building.*
*In her fading consciousness, the girl watched her blood flow endlessly from her body.*
Miku: "It's red..."
*Only then could she breathe. Now everything would be fine.*
*The girl smiled.*
*The full moon in the night sky smiled too.*
*Drip, drip.*
*The sound of falling drops.*
*The moon was concealed behind the clouds and all of the lights in the town were covered in water, the sound of it the only thing left to resonate.*
*She couldn't tell whether it was hot or cold, merely that the humid air was encircling her.*
*Drip, drip.*
*Wherever they were falling to, wherever they were coming from, the drops just continued to trickle out.*
*That single drop.*
*I stopped it with both hands.*
*The sound of that solitary red, red drop--*
*Tonight's concert is taking place at 7PM at the Manten live music club "Kagerou."*
*The indie band "Black Rising Crows," being promoted by "Koro," the bassist from the major label band "Izayoi," is on the schedule for tonight and a popular topic of conversation.*
*It's not that I'm not interested, but rather than hype, I prefer substance. And anyway, my aim tonight isn't the concert.*
*In contrast to the dazzling fashion buildings, this live music club is in a back alley with an underground feel to it.*
*It was not long after I was born that one might say Manten really began to be developed.*
*In sync with improvements to it, the station itself became an unquestionable hub for the subway and national railways, and Manten Station was quickly reborn as a terminal stop.*
*The symbol of the town, Manten East Building, was built in the center of the business district. It became an entertainment hub for students, adults, and tourists alike.*
*The stronger the light, the deeper the darkness. Unlike the east side, bustling with visitors from a wide age range, the west side developed as more of a pleasure district. There are various bars and other businesses focusing on night-time entertainment.*
*Although cases of illegal immigrants coming to do manual labor have increased, their higher-ups remain concealed within this forest of people.*
*Their gang-like unions have strengthened their pull, forging something of an unspoken agreement with the police.*
*Inevitably, the public order has deteriorated, and this town has become dense with both light and dark.*
*It is within the basement of a building in a back alley in the deepest part of this pleasure quarter beyond the west exit, that the live music club "Kagerou" lies.*
*A girl walking alone at this hour runs the risk of being hit on, but with strict rules being enforced these days, almost anyone who learns that the person they're talking to is a student would back off. Anyone looking for an easy target is just spineless.*
*Occasionally, there are those who won't give up, the perverted creepy types who are thrilled that I'm a junior high student. There's no merit in talking to them, so I ignore them. Well, to be honest, the others are just as worthless.*
*Especially considering I didn't come here to pick up guys in the first place.*
*As far as the denizens of this back alley are concerned, they seem to have learned to recognize my face and so conversation attempts have decreased. Ignoring people is quite draining, so I am grateful from the depths of my heart.*
Sayo: "Yes, if you add this to the cost of the drinks, it should be exactly 3,000 yen."
Staff: "Uh, yeah. But you're a junior high student, right? Sorry, but after 6 p.m., people under 16--"
Sayo: "Tch. Must be a newbie."
*I click my tongue and look at the staff member. I have no recollection of seeing him before. There's a large amount of turnover among live music club staff, but still, these kinds of exchanges are troublesome.*
Sayo: "I've paid my money, so hand over my ticket. This place has never been a stickler for rules. They give minors alcohol and cigarettes all the time. I'm not here for either. I'm just here to listen. If you don't hurry, I'll report this place."
Staff: "I-I got it... Here, this is it."
*He falters as I quickly talk him down and hands over my ticket without incident. It's not as if someone like me would make a report, but saying so is more than enough to get him to cave.*
*He probably feels the same, seeing me as troublesome and wanting to end the conversation as quickly as possible, which leads to him being careless.*
Sayo: "Tch. Stinks of cigarettes."
*I come here often, even though I hate the stench of tobacco. Lately many places have separate non-smoking areas, but this "Kagerou" place has an old-fashioned mentality, so the inside is filled with smoke.*
*I often hear that it's good to be retro, but most of the ones saying this are people over thirty. These days, non-smokers have come to hate the second-hand smoke.*
*Since there is a show tonight, the club is packed. The eyes of customers anxiously awaiting the start of the show are gleaming. I weave through the crowd, most of whom aren't paying attention to their surroundings, and reach the drink counter.*
Sayo: "Club soda. In a bottle."
*I take my drink from the bartender. Usually this would be mixed with alcohol, but I like to drink it with a little lemon, so I drop a slice in. The faint, refreshing scent of citrus tickles as it mixes with the stench of tobacco in my nose.*
Sayo: "This is a good spot."
*I lean against a pillar near the bar and touch the bottle to my lips, sipping the carbonated water. The tingling sensation it gives hits the spot.*
*I finally feel relaxed. I probably stand out amongst all of these other high-strung customers, but I'm not really paying them much attention.*
*The reason I came here is to gather information...*
*... On rumors regarding Manten Aquarium.*
*Surely any junior high schoolgirl in the metropolitan area has heard these stories.*
*Like that on the day of a full moon, the water in the tanks turns to blood, or that fish with human faces swim about, or that those who get lost in the closed-down Deep-sea Fish booth never come home; worthless stories written on the internet.*
*But I can't simply dismiss these stories as meaningless.*
*A murder that took place at Manten Aquarium.*
*Of the many rumors, it's that story in particular that gives me a sense that something is amiss in the real world.*
*The reason why is because the director of Manten Aquarium, my father, Sakuragi Isao, died at Manten Aquarium. Yes, I know. The police records list his death as accidental. In reality, his death is a suspicious one with its cause unknown.*
*There's only one way his body could have ended up torn to pieces in a tank at the aquarium.*
*It's been about five years since then, so I was about ten at the time. Despite how young I was, I didn't believe it was an accident, but with nothing to go on, I had to let it go. However, lately, I've begun hearing these strange rumors.*
*When the rumors started, it was strange that there was no origin to them. I began my search for a source about two months ago by hanging around here after hearing that someone from the club "Kagerou" worked part-time at the aquarium five years ago.*
*But nothing's come of it so far. If my efforts are fruitless this time, a night when there should be the most people here, then I've resolved myself to changing the location of my investigation.*
Miku: "Oh, this is a surprise."
*Someone speaks to me in the Kansai dialect, coming off as overly familiar because of it. She doesn't speak extremely fast, so she's probably from the Kyoto area or somewhere around there. It sounds more elegant than the Osaka dialect.*
*Looking at the source of the voice, I'm a bit surprised. It's a girl younger than I. I thought she might be a female otaku with no one to talk to, but really, she seems quite intriguing.*
*That being said, she seems to be looking my way with an odd expression.*
Sayo: "What? You're creeping me out."
Miku: "Don't say that. I'm Minato Miku. Nice to meet ya."
Sayo: "Tch."
*She's not the one I'm looking for. That person had worked part-time five years ago, so it couldn't have been this kid. You can't be employed until your first year of high school.*
*Minato wears an outfit covered in frills that seems difficult to move in. She's dressed like my polar opposite. Her outfit is girly, with white as its base color. There's an 80-90%% chance that someone dressed like that sees herself as cute.*
*She's probably about ten centimeters shorter than I am. Only upon sitting in one of the high counter's chairs is she brought to my eye level.*
Miku: "Hey, Sakuragi-san, right?"
*Minato greets me by my last name.*
*In that moment, I become even more wary of her. I haven't told her my name. I don't like giving my name to people I don't know, so not even the staff or customers here know it.*
*I glare warily at Minato.*
Miku: "Don't make such a scary face. Do you not recognize me? Is that why you're looking at me like that?"
Sayo: "Of course not."
Miku: "We're from the same school. You're from Class B at Mitsuba Girls' Junior High, right? I'm in Class A. We're next to each other."
Sayo: "Huh?"
*Mitsuba Girls' Junior High is the abbreviated version of Private Girls' Academy Mitsuba Junior High. Known throughout the city as a school for "rich girls," it is part of a combined junior and high school. Just as Minato says, I am a student there.*
*Since I haven't made any friends, I don't know any of my classmates. Even though it might be uncommon that some of those classmates are people who like bands enough to come to a live music club, it's not abnormal.*
*More than that, the fact that Minato is a classmate -- is the same age -- surprises me. From her feet to her head, I look over Minato once more. To be sure, her outfit is unique and brand name, and her fluffy blonde hair shows scrupulous grooming.*
*Putting aside her annoying appearance, there are definitely many girls at Mitsuba like her. Still, she's so small I can't see her as anything but an elementary school student.*
Miku: "Do you doubt me?"
*I want to nod my head, but there's no malicious intent in her having learned my name. I can't see any point in her lying to me, so she must be telling the truth.*
Sayo: "So, what do you want?"
Miku: "I don't want anything. I was just happy seein' a classmate at this hour, in a place like this, and wanted to talk to you."
Sayo: "I see. Sorry, but I don't feel like chatting with you right now."
Miku: "Sayo-chan, you're like that at school too, right? You're never with anyone."
Sayo: "So? It's no problem if I don't have friends. More importantly, drop the 'chan.'"
Miku: "You're the same as me, eh?"
Sayo: "Huh?"
Miku: "What kind of band are you here for, Sayo-chan?"
*Without going too deep, Minato changes the subject.*
*She continues using "chan," and since I'm already struggling with her Kyoto dialect, there's a pushiness to her quiet demeanor that's hard to describe. Coupled with her tendency to do things her way, it's troublesome.*
*As I remain silent, Minato puts her lips on the straw in her strawberry milk and looks at me with upturned eyes.*
Sayo: "I'm not after one particular band. If the songs are good and the band is good, I'll listen, but I don't follow any of them."
*The fact that she doesn't look away until I answer is a bit frightening. I have no choice but to speak. While I do have my preferences, I'm not following one specific band, it's just a matter of those bands that I hear and consider good.*
*Whenever I go to the live music clubs, it's usually popular bands that aren't very good.*
*I can't stand the ones who just rely on looking good, having a good MC, or acting stupid instead of having real substance. They're obnoxious. There's no point in declaring your success based on anything other than your music.*
*That's why I dislike these visionless fools at live music clubs, getting their egos inflated by morons and prattling on about their dreams.*
*I dislike the morons who gratefully listen to those fools talking about their dreams even more.*
Miku: "Well then, I have a band to recommend. You should give a listen to the one scheduled to go on right before the main act."
Sayo: "No."
Miku: "Then why did you come here? If you were just here for a certain band and left after, I'd understand, but if you're just vacantly listening, then you should stay 'til the end so you can learn which bands are good."
*In reality, I was planning to stay here until the end of the performance to see if anyone stood out to me, but I think I can be forgiven for not wanting to spend extra time with Minato.*
Sayo: "From the looks of it, you and I don't share the same interests."
Miku: "That's fine. It was worth a try."
*I finally confirm that despite what I say, Minato isn't listening at all.*
*In contrast with her appearance, she's more stubborn than I thought. Since moving from here would come off as running away and might offend her, I stay put.*
*Whether she's satisfied or not, she sits down softly. I thought she would talk to me about many things, but she doesn't speak any further. She seems gloomy, giving the feeling that she was disappointed.*
Miku: "Sayo-chan, they were good, weren't they?"
Sayo: "So-so."
*After the show, we head back to the station.*
*I didn't really want to walk with her, but trying to convince her not to follow me is too much effort.*
*The nightscape is terribly bright.*
Miku: "No way. They had amazing rhythm."
*Silky Strawberry.*
*Since Minato recommended them, I thought it would be a goth-rock band or something, but they were actually a classic rock group.*
*The guitarist and vocalist in particular were the best I saw today. The main act, "Izayoi," wasn't bad, it was just a major difference in their level of skill.*
*Because of that, I sort of feel that it wouldn't be so bad if they were here the next time I came.*
Sayo: "Those lyrics though. I can't get into them."
Miku: "Hmm~ Everything else was good though. Are you really sticking with 'no good' and 'bad' and 'they suck' for your impressions of the other bands though?"
*Minato is more knowledgeable about music than I expected, telling me her impressions during breaks in the performances. The only thing she takes away from my comments are those three thoughts. There's no value to what I said, so it's understandable.*
*Whenever there is a break in conversation, I realize that Silky Strawberry has come up every time.*
*Thinking back on it, the most unusual thing is that Minato and I don't talk about anything but music. It is more mysterious than suspicious.*
*Speaking of which, I don't particularly initiate most of the conversation. Instead, I'm sulking over my inability to gain any information about Manten Aquarium.*
*My silence probably persists for five minutes. Minato is a slow walker and I thought about leaving her behind, but even I don't know why I didn't.*
*The one to break the silence, though quite suddenly, is Minato.*
Miku: "You know, I experienced something weird today."
*I don't respond, continuing to ignore her, but she continues her story anyway.*
Miku: "You ever hear of Manten Aquarium?"
Sayo: "I have."
Miku: "Before the event tonight, I used a year pass and went there, but..."
*Year pass is the abbreviation for year-long passport. It's a ticket that allows you to enter as many times as you want. At 5,500 yen (tax excluded), you can get one after going to the aquarium three times in one year.*
*Furthermore, five years ago they started selling a pass-case set with an illustration and a photography kit. It's a premium version sold at 10,000 yen before tax.*
*It's fairly expensive, but the illustrator is popular with apparel companies and the design containing all the fish is popular with young women, giving such goods a great deal of value.*
*As additional perks to getting the premium version, you get the ability to visit the Manten East Building observation deck as much as you please, as well as a 5%% discount at some of the restaurants within the building.*
*With so many perks, the year pass has become quite a hit. It was the idea of a Manten Aquarium staff member, but rumor says that they've since left the company. It's not unthinkable that it has some relation to the director -- my father's -- incident.*
*With that in mind, I wait for the rest of Minato's story.*
Miku: "That Deep-sea Fish booth was supposedly closed long ago, right? But I... was able to go in. Moreover, there were fish inside. That's not all. The fish were swimming outside of the tanks."
Sayo: "Huh? Are you stupid? The Deep-sea Fish booth is closed, like you said, so there shouldn't be any fish."
Miku: "Which is why I said it was weird from the get-go. I thought I was daydreamin'. I got caught up in that feeling and even spaced out for a bit, so I don't remember everything from that time clearly."
*Minato is saying something strange.*
*It's frustrating, but that's exactly why I can't ignore it. The story Minato is telling me is part of those ridiculous urban legends I'm investigating.*
Sayo: "Who did you hear this story from?"
Miku: "I didn't. It happened today, but I don't have anyone I thought I could tell."
Sayo: "Really? Sometimes there are people who like spreading those kinds of rumors."
Miku: "I told ya. I don't have anyone to talk to about that kind of stuff."
Sayo: "Hmm."
*In other words, Minato isn't the person I'm looking for -- the one who spread the rumors about Manten Aquarium.*
*On the other hand, Minato's observations are in fact giving the rumors some credibility.*
Sayo: "You're serious?"
Miku: "I guess it's natural that you would doubt me after all. It's such a weird story, so I can forgive you for that. I find it weird myself, but I thought you might believe me, Sayo-chan."
Sayo: "It's not that I believe you, but it isn't that I don't, either."
*After I say this, the conversation ends.*
*We walk on without a word, and although the distance to the station is short, it seems as if we go on forever.*
Miku: "Well, time to go home. I'm going this way."
*It seems Minato's going the other way.*
*As we're about to part, I call after Minato.*
*Ever since she told me about the bizarre thing that happened to her today, I've been wondering what I should do, and I decide I should choose the most efficient option.*
Sayo: "Are you free tomorrow night?"
Miku: "Yeah, why?"
Sayo: "About the Deep-sea Fish booth you say you went to. I can't believe you if I don't see it with my own eyes. If you really did see it, then take me there."
Miku: "So you mean we'll go to Manten Aquarium together?"
Sayo: "We're not going to have fun. We're going to do research. I'll be waiting at the coffee shop at Manten Station at five."
Miku: "Ocean Cruising, right? Okay, I'll go along."
*For some reason, Minato looks happy.*
*Even though I've asked her to be my guide, she probably isn't inconvenienced by it.*
*And so, that's how I parted with Minato.*
*As I gaze out the window of the train, I suddenly remember that, oh yes, tonight is the night of a full moon.*
*The next day, I enter the coffee shop at 5 p.m. I come right on time, but there is no sign of Minato.*
*Since I don't participate in any clubs, I still arrive in plenty of time even after stopping off at my house.*
*I take a seat by the window and order a club soda. The new sandwich posted by the door looks delicious, but as I'm not hungry, I don't order one.*
*I wait for her for about five minutes.*
Miku: "Sorry I kept you waiting. It took a little while to get changed."
*I thought that might be the case. I anticipated this by changing before coming.*
*Be that as it may, in an area as heavily patrolled as Manten, it's easier to move about in regular clothes than in school uniforms. Still, with Minato looking like an elementary schooler, I have my doubts as to how effective this will be.*
Miku: "Ah, wait a sec."
*As soon as Minato arrives and puts her bag down, she stands back up right away.*
*Some time passes before she returns, having bought a sandwich and iced café au lait.*
Miku: "I haven't eaten yet. Can we get a bite?"
Sayo: "You're asking me after you already bought it, are you sober?"
Miku: "Time to eat."
Sayo: "Tch."
*I feel irritated, but Minato's cooperation in my investigation of Manten Aquarium is essential.*
*As Minato eats her sandwich, I drink my club soda little by little. Slowly.*
*It's the roast beef and mascarpone cheese sandwich that caught my interest earlier. The thick, acidic scent that wafts my way is without a doubt a dressing with balsamic vinegar as its main ingredient.*
*The seemingly homemade roast beef doesn't seem to have many slices, but each one is thick. The cooked parts and the tinge of redness seem to create a good balance.*
Miku: "Sayo-chan, what's up?"
Sayo: "Nothing."
Miku: "Ah, you want a bite? Say 'ah~'"
Sayo: "Are you stupid?"
Miku: "Oh you don't? This is my first time eating with a friend, so I'm not good at reading the situation."
Sayo: "I'm not so good at this kind of thing myself. Still, don't just call me your friend."
*To be sure, her observation was correct, but I don't exactly want her to share her sandwich with me. If I'm going to have some, I'll buy my own.*
*Minato eats the rest self-consciously. There's not really much conversation, and only when she's finished drinking her iced café au lait does she suggest, Let's go."*
Miku: "That was delicious, but I'm full. Probably ate too much."
Sayo: "What, you knew you weren't going to finish it earlier? If I had known, I would have accepted your offer."
Miku: "So it wasn't that you didn't want it?"
Sayo: "I have my reasons."
Miku: "Well then, let's try sharing next time."
*I wonder if there will be a next time. The rumors of Manten Aquarium, as well as the truth... All of it might be revealed today.*
*Two hours later, our efforts prove fruitless as we stand stock still at the entrance.*
*With no results, the air between us is gloomy.*
Miku: "How weird~ Yesterday when I was walkin', the door was wide open."
Sayo: "I see."
Miku: "Do you... hate me?"
Sayo: "Not in particular. Although I didn't like you to begin with."
*Why does the discussion of whether or not I like her keep coming up?*
*What's most important right now is ascertaining the truth of the rumors. Minato doesn't appear to be lying. In which case, the mystery of the "Deep-sea Fish booth" deepens further.*
Sayo: "What was the difference between yesterday and today?"
*We have no choice but to look back inductively. I wondered if it was because we were together, so we tried one at a time, but nothing changed. So, what else.*
*Minato appears to be in thought, but finally a troubled look appears on her face, and she answers with a tone of supplication.*
Miku: "The difference... I dunno. Why?"
Sayo: "Well, tell me again, from the start."
Miku: "From the beginning, huh... I came to have fun at the seemingly normal Manten Aquarium and some guy called out to me... Speaking of which, I heard him talking about Manten Aquarium's urban legends."
Sayo: "Tch, so we need to go to him. If we don't find him, we won't learn anything. You don't remember anything in particular about him, do you?"
Miku: "When he called out to me, I thought he was just hitting on me. He was a tall man, but anything more detailed than that..."
*She says, looking flustered.*
*I wonder just how optimistic we can be. If we're ambushed within Manten Aquarium, we may actually meet the guy Minato is talking about.*
*I quickly deny these thoughts.*
*Despite hanging around Manten Aquarium for awhile, we find no clues, so we go to the club to do some research.*
*In other words, we're going completely in circles.*
Sayo: "Tch."
Miku: "Sayo-chan, are you mad?"
Sayo: "I'm not really mad at you."
Miku: "Sayo-chan, you a detective or somethin'?"
Sayo: "Wha? Are you stupid?"
Miku: "You just don't talk like a normal person is all. Sayo-chan, are you investigating Manten Aquarium?"
Sayo: "It doesn't matter, does it?"
Miku: "Not good. I want to be your friend, Sayo-chan. I don't... want you to hate me over something like this."
Sayo: "Ridiculous."
*I click my tongue, then heave a sigh.*
*Minato's point of view is always so different from mine. I have not the slightest idea how the conversation ended up this way.*
Sayo: "This is enough. I'll investigate on my own."
Miku: "Sayo-chan!"
*As I start to leave Manten Aquarium, Minato calls after me.*
Miku: "Um, if you have time this weekend, I want to come back here with you. If you're looking into those rumors, Sayo-chan, I'll help you."
Sayo: "Why?"
Miku: "I told you, didn't I? I wanna be your friend -- I wanna do all kinds of stuff with you, Sayo-chan."
Sayo: "That's why I'm asking you the reason why."
Miku: "Probably because you like music, and because you listen to what I have to say. But it could be something different. I don't really understand it myself."
Sayo: "It doesn't matter to me whether or not you're with me. Your thoughts aren't wrong. It's just that I'm asking for my own reasons."
*I say this curtly, and Minato looks down.*
*After a brief pause, I continue.*
Sayo: "Next Friday, I'll come here once more. I'll stop off at the cafe by the train station around the same time. Do as you like."
Miku: "Sayo-chan..."
Sayo: "Later."
Miku: "Okay, later."
*Even I don't know why I said it like that.*
*It wasn't that I like or dislike Minato. She shouldn't mean anything to me.*
*I don't feel at all like I've gotten any closer to the truth.*
*Even so, I have a gut feeling that Minato's experience is directly connected to the truth. Even if Minato herself turns out to be useless, there's no mistake that something may happen that involves her.*
*Not even attempting to hide my irritation, I walk along clicking my tongue numerous times.*
*After school, my homeroom teacher suddenly called me out. Minato Miku is home sick and I'm to take some important paperwork to her. I don't care if they're a summons or exam results, so I didn't pay attention.*
*If the family can't come, then friends are asked to deliver papers. When the homeroom teacher said this, my name apparently came up.*
*I thought about protesting that, since he was the teacher, he should take it himself, but then I remembered I was on cleaning duty, and could use this as an excuse to skip out.*
*It's better delivering something alone than having to do anything with my vapid classmates.*
*After school, I change and head for the address the homeroom teacher gave me.*
Sayo: "Hmm, so it's here."
*Using a private railway line from the West Gate of Manten Station, it takes about ten minutes. I buy drinks at a supermarket near the station.*
*It is not that far from the station, but even in the small distance I travel, I get the impression that I've long since left the city.*
*Minato's home is on the twelfth floor of an apartment building. The building is beautiful and seems to have been built within the last five years. The entrance is serene, like a hotel lobby, illuminated by warm-colored lighting.*
*I press the button for the intercom, and after waiting for about a minute, I hear Minato's voice.*
Miku: "Hi~i..."
Sayo: "Hurry and unlock it."
*The auto-lock is released and I take the elevator up. It's wide enough to fit about eight people in one car.*
*The interior of her apartment shares its orange-colored lighting with the entrance. The door faces away from the highway, causing the neighboring apartment to seem close.*
Miku: "Sayo-chan, thanks."
Sayo: "I just came because I was asked to."
*Minato's apartment is as pretty as I imagined it would be. There is little furniture, and everything is kept meticulously tidy. I figured she would be bad at keeping things clean based on her carefree personality, but that doesn't appear to be the case.*
*There are few utensils in the kitchen, and on closer inspection, dust is gathered in places out of reach. Well, that's a let down. I can venture a guess at the type of life she leads.*
Sayo: "I'll leave the documents on the table in your living room along with some sports drinks, club soda, and fruits. I'm opening your fridge... Hey, the meat and natto in here are going bad."
*The moment I open the fridge, a foul odor wafts out.*
*There's mold growing on the meat, and the natto is impossible to describe. Without a single word, I toss them in the trash.*
*Most of the trash appears to be wrappers for side dishes and junk food. Figures, she barely cooks at all. Even I'm not this bad.*
Miku: "I had no idea. That natto is already bad?"
Sayo: "There's a difference between fermentation and spoilage. Even though it's in the fridge, it's on the verge of going bad. Are your parents not around? Is that why you had me bring these papers in their stead?"
*I borrow a glass and rinse it out, then pour club soda inside. I decide to have a drink before I head home and sit down on the sofa.*
*Minato seems to have improved after having slept for half the day, so she pours one of the sports drinks I bought into a glass and sits down on the living room sofa. She sits down beside me, but it's a big enough sofa that it doesn't feel cramped.*
*After taking a sip, Minato responds.*
Miku: "You see, my mother is a single parent."
*Without lemon, I'm less than satisfied. I glance at the clock and see that it's already 4:30 p.m.*
*The numbers on the clock leave a bigger impression on me than Minato's lonely expression. It was definitely made in Italy. I look around, noting that she owns many expensive things, including the furniture.*
*Minato gives a small smile at the fact that I show no reaction.*
Miku: "Sorry for saying something like that, it has nothing to do with you."
*That sad expression seems to be saying that it still isn't enough. But I don't exactly care to hear her story.*
*Looking around her apartment gives me a general understanding, but those words confirm the fact that her mother rarely comes home.*
*However, none of that has anything to do with me.*
*For some time, the only sound is that of the clock hand. Time passes as it plays a fixed rhythm. I clean up my empty glass and say to Minato...*
Sayo: "Well, I have to go."
Miku: "Hey, Sayo-chan? Are we still on for visiting the aquarium we planned for Friday?"
*Minato asks me this after I've stood up.*
Sayo: "It's not exactly a plan, but why wouldn't we be? My objective hasn't been met yet, you should know that much."
Miku: "Good. I'll see ya then."
*See you then.*
*We go to the same school, but weird is weird.*
*This is how our relationship is. I don't want to be seen as being close to somebody -- at least, not enough so that they might think I have a friend, which is a possibility if they see us talking at school.*
Miku: "I'll walk you to the entrance..."
*She says as I'm already on my way out.*
*Minato finishes speaking, but it's a dull THUD that causes me to turn around."
Sayo: "Minato?"
*Minato has collapsed on the spot. I call out to her, but there is no response, so I walk back over to her.*
*When I touch her to sit her up, I feel that she has a high fever. Following her fall, her breathing has become ragged.*
Sayo: "You pushed yourself too hard. Why?"
Miku: "I didn't... push myself too hard. You came all this way... I just wanted to talk to you..."
Sayo: "Don't talk. Tch, anyway, you need to lie down on the sofa."
*It helps that the sofa is so big. As I help her, Minato lies down.*
*Minato has misinterpreted things. It's not that I came to her house for personal reasons, much less to chat.*
*However, now isn't the time to chastise her about such things.*
*Minato's fever is very high. She's short of breath from sitting up, and it seems to be getting harder and harder for her to breathe.*
Miku: "Urk... I'm fine, Sayo... cha..."
Sayo: "Don't speak."
*I fill a bowl with water, dip a washcloth inside, and place it on Minato's forehead. Sweat is building up, so I use a separate cloth to wipe it away.*
*A little bit of sports drink should be okay, so I pour some in her mouth. I'm worried she will become dehydrated. Then there's this May weather. It's been really hot lately, so she's probably really tired from that, too.*
*That's basically all I can do, so I devote the rest of my energy to things like changing the water in the bowl.*
*Her physical condition hasn't improved, but the symptoms have subsided somewhat. As this thought crosses my mind and I glance at the clock, 8 o'clock has already rolled around.*
*Minato seems sleepy, so I lend her my shoulder and put her to bed. As I do, I decide I should leave.*
*According to Minato, her mother should be home soon. Honestly, I feel worried about leaving her to a parent that lets her live this lifestyle, but Minato insists that she's all right, so I give in. Worrying won't help.*
Miku: "I've... always had a strong constitution, but..."
Sayo: "It's because of your eating habits. Say something to your mother tomorrow and reevaluate your diet."
*The cause is clear. Having suggested that, I continue with, "Later."*
Miku: "Sayo-chan... I'll... get better by Friday..."
Sayo: "It's fine, so be quiet. I'll go to Manten Aquarium, regardless of whether you're with me or not. There's no need for you to worry about it."
Miku: "So like you, Sayo-chan..."
*I begin to hear the soft breathing of someone who's fallen asleep.*
*As I take a step away and think it over, I heave a sigh, realizing what happens from here on doesn't concern me.*
Sayo: "Get better soon. Otherwise, this'll leave a bitter taste in my mouth."
*It's more likely than not that she can't hear me, but I realize at that moment that her breathing has gotten louder.*
*Just like that, I leave Minato's home behind.*
*Apparently, it was that very night that Minato leapt from her apartment building.*
*She didn't crash into the ground, instead getting caught in a tree with almost no injury, but it seems she won't wake up. The point of impact was probably pretty bad. There's also the possibility that she hit her head.*
*I hear that it wasn't a suicide. I've heard talk that it was because she tried to sit out on her balcony.*
*But that's hard to believe. All that's going around school is the rumor that Minato took her own life.*
*Being unable to wake up despite having no injuries, the rumor floating around the hospital is that she lost her life energy -- the thing that a person needs in order to keep on living. I wish that at least the doctors would refute such nonsense.*
*It's only a matter of time before her mysterious jump becomes intertwined with the urban legends surrounding Manten Aquarium. Only the most sensational things, with no basis in reality, become part of the rumors.*
*I don't have any desire to track down the whole truth, so for me, the information I've gotten up to now on the subject is enough.*
*Of course it seems that she won't be coming to school anymore. She isn't getting any visitors either.*
*With nothing but unanswered questions, it seems I'm left with a bad aftertaste after all. It reminds me of the flavorless taste of club soda with no lemon in it.*
*On Friday night, despite all of this, I go to the coffee shop. Even though I know she won't come, I feel I should keep up my end of the promise.*
*After I wait for an hour, Minato doesn't come.*
*After that, I head for Manten Aquarium alone, but it's a fruitless endeavor. I couldn't find the one spreading all the rumors after all.*
*Sounds like Silky Strawberry is performing again today.*
*I sit down at the furthest edge of the bar, and put my drink to my lips.*
*After the director -- my father -- passed away, my mother took custody of me.*
*My mother divorced my father when I was still young. I don't know why they did it, but she probably had money issues with my business-consumed, dreamer of a father.*
*My mother lived with her parents... my grandparents. As the daughter of the man who divorced their daughter, I was seen as nothing more than a burden, and those days were lonely and sad.*
*Not a single member of my family came the day of my junior high entrance ceremony.*
*When I came home, several days worth of meals for me were in the refrigerator, and money for daily expenses far more than a student would ever need were left on the table.*
*Back in those days, I hoped they still loved me, as they had paid for me to attend an expensive private school.*
*But I got my answer. The impression I got from the neighbors was that the reason I was able to attend the private school was because of my father's insistence.*
*On top of the desk was a memo pad with the words "We'll be on a trip until next week," written on it and money just left sitting there. Their car had also disappeared from the parking lot.*
*My school wasn't in the area, so no one knew when my entrance ceremony was. Sending me to a school so far away was the same thing as my mother putting me out of sight, out of mind.*
*That was fine with me. I came to the conclusion that the reason my father had custody of me was because my mother hadn't wanted me from the start.*
*Then on their way home they all died in an accident. My mother, grandparents, and also a young man that was with them.*
*So my inheritance passed down to me. There was a struggle against my relatives to see who would get custody of me.*
*When I saw the amount of my inheritance, I realized their divorce was so that my mother's family's assets wouldn't be depleted by my father's debts.*
*Rather than be sad, I looked to the future ahead. Using the name of some relative I didn't care for, I rented an apartment near the school I attended and, blowing through my inheritance, began living alone.*
*Learning the full truth did not sadden me. Instead, on the occasion of my father's passing, the feeling I had that I was always alone was reiterated for me once more.*
*When I come back to the present, Silky Strawberry is about to perform. Before the first song finishes, I leave the club.*
*Tonight's song resonates too heavily within my heart.*
*On the way home.*
*I'm reminded of when I walked to the station with Minato when I came here before. The lights of the town at night are so pretty, I realize I prefer walking alone.*
*The moon isn't out tonight though.*
Sayo: "Ahhh..."
*I remember that ridiculous rumor.*
*Like that on the day of a full moon, the water in the tanks turns to blood, or that fish with human faces swim about, or that those who get lost in the closed-down Deep-sea Fish booth never come home; worthless stories written on the internet.*
Sayo: "Tch."
*Not caring where I heard it, I click my tongue in the center of the road.*
*The answer has been there from the start. The day that something would happen, that's--*
*Several days later, I pay a visit to Manten Aquarium.*
*I occasionally hear about Minato's condition, but there's apparently no change.*
*Today being Sunday, the town is full of people, but Manten Aquarium is empty. Those rumors must have some influence to them.*
*Surely after the incident with Minato, the rumor has gained even more credibility.*
*Even without knowing the truth, somewhere, subconsciously, it must seem plausible enough.*
Sayo: "Let's go."
*On the day of the full moon, I am ready.*
*Even if it means losing my life to the full moon, I will find the truth.*
*Because...*
*There's nothing more I can lose...*
